ARTICLE 02 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 02 条：上帝的启示 (Revelation of God)

Article 2: Revelation of God

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

上帝的自我启示（God’s Self-Revelation）

我们相信，上帝已向所有人彰显了祂自己。从创世以来，上帝的大能与神性在受造界中一直显而易见。旧约启示上帝是与以色列建立圣约关系的那一位，藉此向万民显明祂永恒的救恩计划。正如新约所载，上帝在耶稣基督里作出了最崇高的自我启示。圣灵持续不断地向个人及教会启明上帝；这种启示始终与圣经全然一致。

God’s Self-Revelation

We believe that God has made Himself known to all people. God’s power and nature have always been evident in creation. The Old Testament reveals God as the one who established a covenant relationship with Israel to make known to all people the eternal plan of salvation. God revealed Himself supremely in Jesus Christ, as recorded in the New Testament. The Holy Spirit continues to make God known to individuals and the church; this revelation is always consistent with the Scriptures.

上帝成文的话语（The Written Word of God）

我们相信，全本圣经都是上帝藉着圣灵所默示的。同一位圣灵引导信仰共同体诠释圣经。耶稣基督的位格、教导与生平，使旧约与新约展现出连贯性与清晰性。旧约见证基督，新约则传扬基督。我们承认圣经是上帝无误的话语，是信仰与生活实践权威的指南。

The Written Word of God

We believe that the entire Bible was inspired by God through the Holy Spirit. The same Spirit guides the community of faith in the interpretation of Scripture. The person, teaching, and life of Jesus Christ bring continuity and clarity to both the Old and New Testaments. The Old Testament bears witness to Christ, and Christ is the One whom the New Testament proclaims. We accept the Bible as the infallible Word of God and the authoritative guide for faith and practice.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 9:1-17；12:1-3；出埃及记 6:2-8；诗篇 19:1-11；119篇；马太福音 5:17-18；路加福音 24:27, 44-47；约翰福音 1:16-18；16:13；使徒行传 8:34-35；罗马书 1:18-21；歌罗西书 1:15-23；提摩太后书 3:14-17；希伯来书 1:1-2；彼得后书 1:16-21。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 9:1-17; 12:1-3; Exodus 6:2-8; Psalm 19:1-11; 119; Matthew 5:17-18; Luke 24:27, 44-47; John 1:16-18; 16:13; Acts 8:34-35; Romans 1:18-21; Hebrews 1:1-2; Colossians 1:15-23; 2 Timothy 3:14-17; 2 Peter 1:16-21.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

启示（Revelation）是上帝使人类认识祂自己的行动或历程。既然所有人都是按着上帝的形象所造，人便具备了领受自上而来的亮光，并对神圣启示作出回应的潜质。

Revelation is the act or process by which God makes Himself known to human beings. Since all people are made in the image of God, they have the potential for receiving light from above and for responding to divine revelation.

上帝的自我启示（God’s Self-Revelation）

由于我们的堕落与属灵的盲目，若要获得对上帝使人得救的认识（saving knowledge of God），我们迫切需要神圣的救助。这种对上帝及其对人类救恩计划的认识，绝非单凭勤勉的研究或宗教上的努力所能达成。它必须从我们自身之外临到我们，完全超越了人类自我探寻的能力（约伯记 11:7）。

上帝竟然愿意向有罪且失丧的人类显明祂自己，正是祂奇妙而无限恩典的明证。若任凭我们自己，我们既无法真正明白上帝是怎样的一位，也无法探明祂对人类的心意。虽然上帝选择显明自己让我们深怀感恩，但我们对上帝及其作为的认识依然是有限的（约伯记 26:14）。基督徒的盼望在于：在将来天上的国度里，他们将更完全地认识上帝（哥林多前书 13:12）。

上帝藉着多种方式向人类启示祂自己。上帝的自我显明通常分为两类：“普遍启示”（general revelation）与“特殊启示”（special revelation）。普遍启示意味着上帝并未任凭自己没有见证，而是向全人类普遍地显明自己。上帝在受造界/大自然中启示自己（诗篇 19:1-4；使徒行传 14:17；罗马书 1:20）。上帝也在祂的审判中（罗马书 1:18）以及在人类历史中的恩典作为里使人认识祂。我们的良心同样为普遍启示作见证。

然而，唯有藉着上帝的“特殊启示”，人类才能认识祂的救赎计划。这种启示也是以极丰富多样的方式赐下的——藉着异梦与异象、天使的造访与神圣的显现（divine theophanies）、上帝的声音，以及对祂在祂子民以色列历史中大能作为的诠释。上帝行事始终带着主权自由；上帝随己意在何时、以何种方式发言，全在乎祂的圣裁。

God’s Self-Revelation

Because of our fallenness and spiritual blindness, we are all in desperate need of divine help if we are to come to a saving knowledge of God. Such a knowledge of God and His salvation plans for the human race cannot be attained by diligent research or by religious strivings. It must come to us from outside of ourselves and is beyond our own ability to discover ( Job 11:7).

That God was willing to make Himself known to a sinful and lost human race is a sign of His marvellous and infinite grace. Left to ourselves, we could not truly know what God is like, nor discover His purposes for humanity. Although we are deeply grateful that God chose to make Himself known, our knowledge of God and His ways is limited ( Job 26:14). It is the hope of Christian believers that they will know God more fully in the coming heavenly kingdom (1 Corinthians 13:12).

God reveals Himself to human beings in a variety of ways. God’s selfdisclosure is often divided into two categories: “general” and “special” revelation. General revelation implies that God has not left Himself without a witness to humanity in general. God reveals Himself in nature (Psalm 19:1-4; Acts 14:17; Romans 1:20). God also makes Himself known in His judgments (Romans 1:18) as well as in His gracious acts in human history. Our consciences also bear witness to a general revelation.

However, it is only by God’s “special” revelation that humans can come to know His saving plans. This revelation too was given in a great variety of ways—in dreams and visions, by angelic visits and divine theophanies, by the voice of God and by the interpretation of His mighty acts in the history of His people Israel. Always God acts with sovereign freedom; God speaks when and as He chooses.

神圣启示的领受者（Recipients of Divine Revelation）

从人类历史的起首，上帝就在祂的主权恩典中拣选了领受祂自我显明的人。这些以信心和顺服回应上帝启示的属人器皿，转而成为使者，与他人分享他们所领受关于上帝旨意的洞见。

虽然上帝早在列祖时代之前就已经显明了祂自己（创世记 3:13；9:1-17），但正是在亚伯拉罕蒙召时（创世记 12:1-3），上帝开启了以特殊方式向以色列民启示祂自己的历程。上帝将祂的自我启示主要聚焦于一个特定民族，绝非对非以色列人漠不关心。相反，上帝拣选以色列领受救恩之光，乃是为了使他们成为万民蒙福的管道。

从摩西开始——上帝拣选他作以色列的拯救者，以及上帝与这蒙救赎子民所立圣约的中保——上帝藉着赐给以色列先知的信息，持续渐进地显明祂的心意。及至时候满足（加拉太书 4:4），上帝藉着祂儿子耶稣基督的位格（希伯来书 1:1-2），作出了最崇高的自我显明；先知关于救恩的应许全都在祂里面得以成全。耶稣被称为上帝的“道/话语”（Word；约翰福音 1:1, 14）。

Recipients of Divine Revelation

From the beginning of human history, God in His sovereign grace chose the recipients of His self disclosure. These human agents who responded in faith and obedience to God’s revelation in turn became messengers who shared with others the insights into God’s will which they had received. Although God had made Himself known even in pre-Patriarchal times (Genesis 3:13; 9:1-17), it was with the call of Abraham (Genesis 12:1-3) that God began the process of revealing Himself in a special way to the people of Israel. By restricting His self-revelation primarily to one particular people, God was not indifferent to non-Israelite people. Rather, God chose Israel to receive the light of salvation and to become a channel of blessing to all the peoples of the world.

Beginning with Moses, whom God chose to be the deliverer of Israel and the mediator of the covenant which God established with this redeemed people, God continued to make His purposes known progressively through the messages which He gave to Israel’s prophets. And in the fullness of time (Galatians 4:4) God made Himself known supremely in the person of His Son, Jesus Christ (Hebrews 1:1, 2), in whom the prophetic promises of salvation were fulfilled. Jesus is called the “Word” of God ( John 1:1, 14).

在基督里的启示（Revelation in Christ）

藉着祂的道成肉身、在人间的起居生活、奇妙的教训，尤其是藉着祂的受死、复活与荣耀升天，耶稣向世人显明了父上帝——父上帝如此深爱这世上的人，甚至差遣祂的独生子，拯救世人脱离永死（约翰福音 3:16；14:8-11）。从来没有人见过上帝，唯有在父怀里的独生子，将上帝表明出来（约翰福音 1:18；马太福音 11:27）。

基督处于基督信仰的绝对核心；在祂里面，上帝使祂在人类历史中诸多自我显明的作为达到了最高峰（以弗所书 1:9-10）。然而，启示的历程即使在基督完成祂在地上的工作、重返荣耀并浇灌祂的圣灵之后，仍延续了一段时期。正如新约所记，这一历程在初代教会的生活中继续展开，基督生平的事迹在其中被记录与诠释。

为基督里这一终极启示作见证的是使徒们，耶稣拣选他们成为祂言语与作为的托管者与传递者。教会是建造在使徒和先知的根基之上（以弗所书 2:20）。作为基督事件见证人的使徒们离开历史舞台时，并没有继承者取代他们的地位。他们的著作构成了上帝新子民——教会——的奠基性文献，对历代基督徒始终具有不可动摇的权威。随着使徒著作的完成，圣经正典宣告确立闭合。

Revelation in Christ

By his incarnation, his life among the people, his marvellous teachings, and above all, by his death, resurrection, and glorious exaltation, Jesus revealed God the Father, who so loved this world of humanity that He sent His only Son to deliver it from eternal death ( John 3:16; 14:8-11). No one has ever seen God, but God’s Son, who is close to the Father’s heart, has made God known ( John 1:18; Matthew 11:27).

Christ stands at the very centre of the Christian faith; in him God brought His many acts of self-disclosure in human history to a climax (Ephesians 1:9, 10). But the process of revelation continued for some time even after Christ had completed his work on earth, had returned to glory, and poured out his Spirit. It went on in the life of the early church, as written in the New Testament where the events of the life of Christ were recorded and interpreted.

The witnesses to this final revelation in Christ were the apostles, whom Jesus had chosen to be the depositories of his words and works. The church is built on the foundation of the apostles and prophets (Ephesians 2:20). The apostles who were witnesses of the Christ-event were not replaced when they passed off the scene. Their writings are the foundation documents of the new people of God, the church, and they remain authoritative for Christian believers for all time. With the writing of the apostolic books, the biblical canon came to a close.

启示的记录（The Record of Revelation）

摩西和先知在基督徒所称的旧约圣经中，为我们留下了赐给他们的启示的记录。虽然旧约各卷书并非上帝对人类最终极的话语（以弗所书 3:5），但它们都是上帝所默示的（提摩太后书 3:16；彼得后书 1:20-21）。耶稣和使徒毫无保留地将旧约圣经视为上帝的话语（马太福音 5:18）。他们不仅不断引用旧约，而且在这些犹太经典中指出了预表基督降临及其救赎作为的人物与事件。

由于在先知作者的时代以色列民讲希伯来语，因此上帝给以色列的启示就是用这种语言传达的（其中有少数段落是用亚兰语写的）。在上帝的护理中，当时已有字母文字可用于将上帝的信息付诸笔墨，书写工具与材料也已齐备。将上帝的启示记录为这种人类文字形式的作者们，运用了丰富多样的文学体裁——叙事、诗歌、智慧箴言，以及一种被称为预言/先知文献的独特体裁。

尽管耶稣和与祂同代的人讲亚兰语，但使徒们对弥赛亚言行的见证是用通用希腊语（Hellenistic Greek）记录下来的，这是当时地中海沿岸所有地区通用的语言。既然基督设立了新约，这本文集便被称为新约圣经。

从基督教纪元最初几个世纪直到今日，圣经已被翻译成多种语言；因此，今天上帝仍继续藉着圣经的信息启示祂自己。当人们信服并接纳成文圣经中上帝的自我启示时，他们就成为了上帝新子民——教会——的成员。教会与旧约中真正的以色列一脉相承，因此教会的成员被称为亚伯拉罕的子孙（加拉太书 3:29）。

The Record of Revelation

Moses and the prophets have left us a record of the revelation given to them in the pages of what Christians call the Old Testament. Although the books of the Old Testament are not God’s final word to humanity (Ephesians 3:5), they were inspired by God (2 Timothy 3:16; 2 Peter 1:20, 21). Jesus and the apostles viewed the Old Testament Scriptures as the Word of God without any reservation (Matthew 5:18). Not only did they constantly quote the Old Testament, but found people and events in these Jewish Scriptures which foreshadowed the coming of Christ and his saving work.

Since the people of Israel spoke Hebrew in the days of the prophetic writers, God’s revelation came to Israel in that language (with a few portions in Aramaic). In the providence of God, an alphabet was available by which God’s messages could be put into writing, as were the necessary writing materials. The writers who put God’s revelation into this very human form availed themselves of a variety of literary genres—narrative, poetry, wisdom sayings, and a unique type of literature called prophecy.

Although Jesus and his contemporaries spoke Aramaic, the witness of the apostles to the words and deeds of the Messiah were recorded in Hellenistic Greek, the language spoken in all the Mediterranean lands. Since Christ established a new covenant, the collection of apostolic books is called the New Testament.

From the early centuries of the Christian era up to the present, the Bible has been translated into many languages, and so today God continues to reveal Himself through the message of the Scriptures. When people embrace God’s self-revelation in this written Word of God, they become members of the new people of God, the church. The church stands in continuity with the genuine Israel of the Old Testament and its members are therefore called children of Abraham (Galatians 3:29).

理解启示（Understanding Revelation）

对于基督徒而言，先知与使徒在其中为我们留下上帝自我显明记录的圣经，在教义与伦理事务上始终具有规范性准则（normative）的地位。因此，圣经读者审慎地诠释上帝的话语乃是至关重要的事。尽管上帝的子民在理解圣经各卷书的具体细节上或许永远无法达成完全的一致，但他们在圣经的核心信息上达成共识却至关重要。

圣经记载了上帝如何以祂的恩典为失丧的人类成就救赎计划的历程。然而，圣经不应仅仅被当作一本普通历史书来读。人们固然可以在圣经中找到关于人类诸多知识领域的宝贵信息，但正如圣经书卷所记载的那样，上帝的启示主要聚焦于圣洁且满有恩典的上帝对人类的拯救。

既然大多数基督徒读的是圣经译本，明白以下这一点就很重要：所有的翻译都是尝试用当今世界各族的语言来表达原文，并且在某种程度上已经包含了对圣经文本的诠释。因此，我们当感恩上帝为教会赐下了精通圣经写作原文的圣经学者。

圣经的作者们是受上帝默示的，因此圣经读者需要圣灵的帮助才能明白上帝话语的基本信息（约翰福音 16:8-11；哥林多前书 2:4）。撒但蒙蔽了不信之人的心眼，以致他们无法领会圣经中救恩的信息，除非上帝藉着祂的灵开启他们的心眼（帖撒罗尼迦前书 1:5；哥林多前书 2:14；哥林多后书 4:4；以弗所书 4:18）。

信徒同样需要上帝之灵的帮助，才能理解上帝在祂话语中所启示的真理（以弗所书 1:18；哥林多前书 2:10-16）。耶稣曾向祂的追随者应许赐下圣灵的帮助，使他们能明白耶稣的教导与作为（约翰福音 14:26；16:13）。然而，这绝不应当被理解为圣灵会直接解释圣经书卷在文本与文学上的复杂细节。虽然祷告与敬虔是顺服倾听圣经中上帝话语的先决条件，但单凭它们本身并不能保证对圣经文本作出正确的诠释。因此，我们必须在信徒团契中，并对照基督教会悠久的历史，来检验我们对圣经教导的理解。

在我们竭力明白圣经中上帝信息的过程中，必须确保自己理解圣经作者字词的原意以及它们所在的上下文语境。我们也必须注意句子和段落的语法结构。此外，我们必须考虑到旧约和新约书卷中丰富的文学体裁。例如，新约采用了一种被称为“福音书”的新文学体裁；此外还有大量的书信体文学，更不用说叙事文学甚至启示文学了。

圣经作者运用了许多修辞手法来表达思想，而在现代语言中往往没有完全对应的表达。因此，按字面意义诠释圣经（to interpret the Bible literally），是指我们要努力理解圣经中的意象，辨明受默示的作者想要向读者传达什么信息。作者在记录上帝传达给他们的信息时，心中都有特定的含义，而我们的职责就是发现这些原意，然后将它们应用到信徒个人和教会生活中出现的新处境中。确定经文的原始意义是我们的首要任务；第二项任务则是叩问自己：这段经文今天对我们意味着什么？

所有的文学作品都会反映其时代的文化，圣经书卷也不例外。上帝的启示是借着古代文化（闪族文化与希腊-罗马文化）的外衣临到我们的。因此，我们应当谨慎，不要把古代世界的文化习俗照搬到我们的现代生活中。我们的任务是发掘圣经中具有永久性和普遍适用性的教导，并将这些教导应用在我们今日世界的生活中。

Understanding Revelation

For Christian believers, the Bible, in which prophets and apostles have left us the records of God’s self-disclosure, remains normative in matters of doctrine and ethics. It is therefore of great consequence that Bible readers interpret God’s Word carefully. Although the people of God may never fully agree in their understanding of the details of the books of the Bible, it is of utmost importance that they agree on the fundamental message of the Scriptures.

The Bible is the story of how God in His grace carried out His plan of redemption for a lost human race. The Scriptures, however, should not be read simply as a history book. One can find valuable information on many areas of human knowledge in the Bible, but God’s revelation, as recorded in the books of the Bible, focuses primarily on the salvation of humankind by a holy and gracious God.

Since most Christians read the Bible in a translation, it is important that they recognize that all translations are attempts to render the original texts in the current languages of the peoples of this world, and are to some degree already interpretations of the biblical texts. We should therefore be grateful that God has given the church biblical scholars who are conversant with the original languages in which the books were written.

The writers of the Scriptures were divinely inspired and so the Bible reader needs the help of the Holy Spirit to comprehend the basic message of God’s Word ( John 16:8-11; 1 Corinthians 2:4). Satan has so blinded the minds of unbelievers that they cannot grasp the saving message of the Scriptures until God by His Spirit opens the eyes of their hearts (1 Thessalonians 1:5; 1 Corinthians 2:14; 2 Corinthians 4:4; Ephesians 4:18).

Believers too need the help of the Spirit of God to understand what God has revealed in His Word (Ephesians 1:18; 1 Corinthians 2:10-16). Jesus promised his followers the help of the Spirit in their effort to understand the teachings and works of Jesus ( John 14:26; 16:13). This should not, however, be understood to mean that the Spirit will explain the textual and literary intricacies of the biblical books. Although prayer and godliness are prerequisites for obedient listening to God’s Word in the Bible, they do not by themselves guarantee a correct interpretation of the biblical text. It is therefore of importance that we test our understanding of biblical teachings in the fellowship of believers and in the light of the long history of the Christian church.

In our efforts to understand God’s messages in the Scriptures, we must make sure that we understand the meaning of the words of the biblical writers and the context in which they are found. We also must pay attention to the grammatical structures of sentences and paragraphs. Moreover, we must take into account the many literary genres of both the Old and the New Testament books. The New Testament, for example, makes use of a new type of literature called “Gospels.” There is also a great amount of epistolary literature, not to mention narrative and even apocalyptic literature.

The biblical writers used many figures of speech to express themselves and often there are no exact equivalents in modern languages. To interpret the Bible literally would then mean that we seek to understand the imagery of the Bible and discern what message the inspired writer wanted to convey to readers. The writers had specific meanings in mind when they recorded the messages conveyed to them by God, and it is our duty to discover these meanings and then to apply them to new situations that arise in the life of the individual believer and the church. Establishing the original meaning of a text is our first task; the second is to ask ourselves what this text might mean for us today.

All literature reflects the culture of its day, and that holds for the biblical books as well. God’s revelation has come to us in the dress of ancient cultures, Semitic and Greco-Roman. We should then be careful not to carry the cultural practices of an ancient world over into our contemporary life. Our task is to discover the permanent and universally applicable teachings of the Bible and to apply these to our life in today’s world.

旧约与新约（Old and New Testaments）

旧约与新约的关系，是信徒在圣经诠释中所面临的较有难度的课题之一。既然亚伯拉罕、以撒、雅各的上帝，也是我们主耶稣基督的父上帝，我们就可以确信圣经信息有着根本的统一性。事实上，个别经文必须始终在圣经整体性的亮光下被理解。

然而，耶稣和祂的使徒清楚表明，旧约书卷属于预备时期，相比之下，新约书卷则出自应许成就的时代。由此可见，旧约应当在上帝于耶稣基督里终极启示的亮光下来看待。这一点从使徒行传中所记录的宣讲中可以清晰地看出来——这些讲道都以回顾救赎历史为开端，以基督里上帝的终极启示为高潮与结论。虽然旧约与新约一样都是上帝所默示的，但它是上帝初步的前置之言（preliminary word）。这种以基督为中心的释经进路，在16世纪宗教改革时期受到我们再洗礼派（Anabaptist）先祖的特别强调。

上帝在人类历史中不断进行的自我显明，引向了基督里神圣启示的高峰，这被称为“渐进启示”（progressive revelation）。渐进启示的深意在于：上帝在祂的恩典中，在过去的时代以人们能够理解的方式向世人说话；上帝不时向这些启示的领受者更加清楚地显明祂对人类的救赎心意。最终，上帝藉着祂自己的儿子耶稣基督完全显明了祂自己，旧约的一切盼望与应许都在祂里面得以完全成就。

从基督里上帝终极启示的至高点，教会如今展望现今世代的终局，那时基督将要荣耀再临。祂在人类历史终局时的荣耀显现，也被称为一种启示（哥林多前书 1:7；帖撒罗尼迦后书 1:7）。

在教会生活中时常出现的一个问题是：上帝是否继续直接向祂的儿女启示祂自己？“启示”一词偶尔被用来指上帝赐给个别信徒的属灵洞见。神学家更倾向于将这类洞见称为“光照”（illumination）。保罗祈求以弗所人能得着“赐人智慧和启示的灵”（以弗所书 1:17）。他谈到“上帝显明给我们的”事（哥林多前书 2:10）。此外，他设想当信徒聚集敬拜时，可能会有人说出“启示的话”（哥林多前书 14:6, 26, 30）。然而，对上帝作为的这种领会与洞见，必须始终与圣经中上帝的启示相和谐一致。对于基督教会而言，上帝成文且受圣灵默示的话语，始终是信仰与生活实践权威的最高指南。

Old and New Testaments

The relationship of the Old Testament to the New is one of the more difficult questions believers face in the interpretation of the Bible. Since the God of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob is also the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, we can assume the fundamental unity of the message of the Bible. In fact, individual passages should always be understood in the light of the wholeness of the Scriptures.

However, Jesus and his apostles clearly indicate that the books of the Old Testament belong to the period of preparation. The New Testament books, by contrast, come from the time of fulfillment. It follows, then, that the Old Testament should be seen in the light of God’s final revelation in Jesus Christ. One can see this clearly from the recorded sermons in the book of Acts, which begin with a review of salvation history and conclude with God’s final revelation in Christ. Although the Old Testament like the New was inspired by God, it was God’s preliminary word. This approach to the Bible was underscored particularly by our Anabaptist forebears at the time of the Reformation in the l6th century.

The ongoing self-disclosure of God in human history, leading to the climax of divine revelation in Christ, can be called “progressive” revelation. Progressive revelation means, among other things, that God in His grace spoke to men and women in past ages in ways they could understand. From time to time, God made His saving purposes for humanity known more plainly to the recipients of this revelation. Finally, God made Himself known through His own Son, Jesus Christ, in whom the hopes and promises of the Old Testament were fulfilled.

From the high point of God’s final revelation in Christ, the church now looks forward to the end of the present age when Christ will come again. His glorious appearance at the end of human history is also called a revelation (1 Corinthians 1:7; 2 Thessalonians 1:7).

A question that arises from time to time in the life of the church is whether God continues to reveal Himself directly to His children. The word “revelation” is used occasionally for insights which God gives to individual believers. Theologians prefer to describe such insights as “illumination.” Paul prays that the Ephesians might receive a “Spirit of wisdom and revelation” (1:17). He speaks of the things “God has revealed to us” (1 Corinthians 2:10). Moreover, He assumes that a word of revelation may be spoken when believers gather for worship (1 Corinthians 14:6, 26, 30). Such insights into God’s ways, however, must always be in harmony with the revelation of God in the Scriptures. For the Christian church, the written and inspired Word of God remains the authoritative guide for faith and practice.

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

引言：在受造界与圣经中认识上帝

我们宣认：上帝已向所有人启示了关于祂自己的真理。上帝最初藉着创造显明祂自己。正如我们从艺术家的作品中认识艺术家一样，我们藉着受造物认识上帝。如果我们所拥有的仅仅是创造，我们就不会知道上帝大能的救赎作为：道成肉身、十字架、复活、升天、五旬节、基督再临（Parousia）。如果说受造界是一幅美好但不完整的图画，那么圣经则指引我们更完全地认识上帝，并引领我们与耶稣建立亲密相交的关系——祂是上帝启示的丰盛圆满。

门诺弟兄会长期以来被称为“圣书之民”（people of the book）。作为一个教会群体，我们的焦点一直不是死板的信条条文（creedal statements），而是对圣经活泼严谨的研究。我们努力成为“圣道之民”（people of the Word），因为我们深信在圣经中，上帝向我们敞开了祂的神圣心怀。那句常被提及的问话：“圣经怎么说？”被理解为对这一问题的动态对应：“上帝正指示我们当如何生活？”在历史上，门诺弟兄会在其最佳状态下，始终秉持着早期再洗礼派（Anabaptist）先驱的热忱——即纯正的真道理解必须伴随着身体力行的舍己顺服。尽管门诺弟兄会并非忽视来自上帝的更具个人性、主观性的交通，但这些光照的洞见在传统上一贯会与圣经以及信徒共同体相互印证和检验。上帝成文的话语被确认为受圣灵默示的，且是信仰与生活实践权威的指南。

Introduction: Knowing God in Creation and Scripture

We confess that God has revealed the truth about Himself to all people. God began by revealing Himself through creation. Just as we learn about an artist from her work, we learn about God through creation. If creation were all we had, we would not know about God’s mighty acts of salvation: the incarnation, the cross, the resurrection, the ascension, Pentecost, the Parousia (Christ’s second coming). If creation is a good but incomplete picture, the Scriptures guide us to a more complete knowledge of God and open to us a relationship with Jesus as the fullness of God’s revelation. Mennonite Brethren have long been known as people of the book. As a church, our focus has not been on creedal statements but on the study of Scripture. We try to be people of the Word because we believe that in the Scriptures God has revealed His heart to us. The oft-repeated phrase, “What does the Bible say?” is understood to be the dynamic equivalent of the question, “What is God telling us about how to live?” Historically, Mennonite Brethren at their best have lived with the passion of the early Anabaptist reformers, the passion that right understanding must be followed by obedience. Although Mennonite Brethren have not been unaware of more personal, subjective communication from God, these insights of illumination are characteristically checked with Scripture and with the community of believers. The written Word of God is accepted as inspired and as the authoritative guide for faith and practice.

公开宣读圣经（Public Scripture Reading）

藉着圣经认识上帝的心意，将帮助我们扎根于上帝和信仰之中。在上帝启示的核心，是邀请人与上帝进入一种特殊的关系。圣灵使用圣经在信仰中建立我们，并显露我们生命中需要改变的事物。

主耶稣将圣经的宣读与阐释作为祂事工的起点。当祂还是殿里的少年时，耶稣就与律法教师就律法的诠释进行辩论（路加福音 2:46-49）。当祂在会堂开启公开事工时，耶稣藉着诵读以赛亚书 61章来阐释祂的使命（路加福音 4:18-19）。在那一场合，耶稣关心的不仅是经文的宣读，更是经文在祂事工中的生动成就。在与法利赛人和祭司的争辩中，耶稣展现了对希伯来圣经的高度熟稔。祂的事工以与两位门徒的一日同行为高潮，在这段路途中，耶稣藉着摩西和众先知来诠释祂的生命。耶稣树立了圣经在共同体生活与敬拜中居于中心地位的典范。

新约教会将圣经的诵读与诠释作为其敬拜聚会的核心组成部分。在使徒行传中，彼得、司提反和保罗的讲道都以引用和诠释圣经为特征。庇哩亚的信徒因热切考查希伯来圣经而受到特别赞许（使徒行传 17:11）。在罗马书 10:17中，保罗提醒我们，信道是从听道来的，听道是从基督的话来的。这里的重点放在“听”信息上，这有力地提醒了有声诵读的重要性。在哥林多前书 14:26中，保罗指导哥林多人敬拜要有秩序，在诸般崇拜礼仪元素中使用“教训/教导的话”。根据我们对当时犹太敬拜的了解，初代教会极可能是依靠个人在其非正式的崇拜礼仪中宣读经文。保罗写信给以弗所人，让他们唱诗篇（以弗所书 5:19），这是在敬拜中创造性运用圣经的又一例证。

传统上，门诺弟兄会利用宣读圣经这一崇拜礼仪环节，鼓励教会成员广泛运用其恩赐。通常，宣讲能力有限的教会领袖会被邀请诵读经文并祷告；具有圣经宣讲潜力的年轻信徒会被邀请对自己即将诵读的经文作简短点评分享。经过恰当的指导，宣读圣经者可以逐步发展宣讲福音的恩赐。

我们鼓励敬拜策划者将宣读圣经作为敬拜聚会的关键部分。崇拜经课（Lectionary readings）是把上帝的声音引入会众敬拜的一种常用方法。可在《共同崇拜书》（Book of Common Worship）中查阅的公用经课，提供了一套为期三年的系统经文阅读日程。每周都建议诵读旧约、诗篇、书信和福音书的经文。敬拜领袖也可以制定自己在公共敬拜中系统宣读圣经的方案。公开宣读圣经绝不可被忽视。

Public Scripture Reading

Getting to know God’s heart through Scripture will help us become grounded in God and in faith. At the heart of God’s revelation is an invitation to a special relationship with God. The Holy Spirit uses the Scriptures to build us up in faith and to expose things in our lives that need change.

The Lord Jesus made the reading and interpretation of Scripture the starting point of his ministry. As a boy in the temple, Jesus was disputing with the teachers of the law about its interpretation (Luke 2:46-49). As he opened his public ministry in the synagogue, Jesus interpreted his mission by reading Isaiah 61 (Luke 4:18-19). On that occasion, Jesus was concerned not only with the reading of the text but with its dynamic fulfillment in his ministry. In his disputes with the Pharisees and priests, Jesus showed great facility with the Hebrew Scriptures. His ministry was capped by a day-long walk with two disciples in which Jesus used Moses and the prophets to interpret his life. Jesus modelled the centrality of Scripture in community life and worship.

The New Testament church made the reading and interpretation of Scripture a central component of its worship gathering. In the book of Acts, the sermons of Peter, Stephen, and Paul are marked by Scriptural citations and interpretation. The believers at Berea received special commendation for their eager examination of the Hebrew Scriptures (Acts 17:11). In Romans 10:17, Paul reminds us that faith comes from hearing the message, and the message is heard through the word of Christ. The emphasis is placed on hearing the message, a good reminder of the significance of audible expression. In 1 Corinthians 14:26, Paul instructed the Corinthians to be orderly in their worship, using among other liturgical elements “a word of instruction.” Based on what we know from contemporary Jewish worship, it seems most likely that the early church depended on individuals to use Scripture readings in their informal liturgy. To the Ephesians, Paul wrote that they should sing psalms (Ephesians 5:19), another creative use of the Bible in worship.

Traditionally, Mennonite Brethren have used the liturgical element of Scripture reading to encourage the broad use of gifts by church members. Often church leaders whose capacity for proclamation is limited have been asked to read the Scriptures and pray. Young persons with potential in biblical proclamation have been invited to make brief comments about the passage they are about to read. With proper instruction, Scripture readers can develop gifts in proclaiming the gospel.

Worship planners are encouraged to make Scripture reading a key part of worship gatherings. Lectionary readings are one method being used to introduce the voice of God into congregational worship. The common lectionary, available in the Book of Common Worship, offers a planned threeyear reading schedule. Each week, readings from the Old Testament, the Psalms, the Epistles, and the Gospels are suggested. Worship leaders may wish to develop their own systematic plan for reading the Scriptures in public worship. Public reading of the Bible is not to be neglected.

个人研读圣经（Personal Scripture Reading）

圣经中的人物也为个人阅读和研读圣经树立了榜样。传福音的腓利遇见正在阅读以赛亚书的埃塞俄比亚太监，并为他讲解了经文（使徒行传 8:26-40）。保罗始终是一名研读圣经的学者，即便在狱中仍索要书卷和皮卷（提摩太后书 4:13）。圣经的研读、默想和背诵是早期信徒的鲜明特征。

教会应当培养读经、研经和背诵经文的属灵操练。各样团契活动与主日学活动有助于激发研经的动力。带来生命改变的研读圣经包含：祷告预备、仔细观察、深思默想、个人应用与忠心顺服（参见 Rumford，227-232页）。每日阅读正典的不同部分（包括福音书、书信、诗篇等），应当成为基督徒的鲜明标记。

Personal Scripture Reading

Personal Bible reading and study are also modelled by biblical figures. Philip the evangelist encountered the Ethiopian court official reading Isaiah and interpreted the text for him (Acts 8:26-40). Paul continued to be a student of the Scriptures, asking from prison for scrolls and parchments (2 Timothy 4:13). Scripture study, meditation, and memory characterized early believers. Churches should foster the disciplines of Bible reading, study, and memorization. Club programs and Sunday school activities help motivate Bible study. Life-changing Scripture study involves prayerful preparation, careful observation, thoughtful meditation, personal application, and faithful obedience (Rumford 227-232). Daily reading in various parts of the canon, including the Gospels, the Epistles, the Psalms, and so on, should characterize Christians.

圣经译本与版本（Translations and Versions）

圣经译本与版本的与日俱增，已成为北美基督教环境的一大特征。关于各种版本孰优孰劣以及所谓“唯独权威”的混乱争论，可能会使许多圣经读者感到困惑。牧师与堂会若在公共敬拜中选择采用单一或主要的圣经版本，将有助于促进共同体的合一。而研读圣经的人则可以通过参考多种版本来拓宽自己的理解。

对于那些坚称英王钦定本（King James Version, KJV）优于其他版本的人，该如何回应？经验表明，单凭逻辑论证可能不足以化解某些人对钦定本的强烈依恋。然而，必须指出的是，较新的译本得益于更优质的古代手抄本见证（superior textual witnesses）、更贴近现代的语言，以及对初学圣经者而言具有更高的易读性。

新国际版（NIV）因其卓越的可读性而在门诺弟兄会中广受欢迎。那些倾向于在指涉人类时使用包容性语言的人更青睐新修订标准版（NRSV）。而新美国标准版（NASB）则提供了贴近原文最直接的字对字直译。

Translations and Versions

A proliferation of biblical translations and versions has come to characterize the North American Christian milieu. Confusing arguments about the superiority and “unique authority” of various versions can befuddle many Bible readers. Pastors and congregations help bring unity to their community by choosing to follow a single or primary version for public worship. Students of the Scriptures broaden their understanding by using a variety of versions. How should one reply to those who insist that the King James Version has greater merit than others? Experience shows that logical argumentation alone may not be enough to counter the strong attachment some have to the King James. Nevertheless, it is important to note that the newer translations benefit from superior textual witnesses, more contemporary language, and greater accessibility for those who are new to Bible study.

The New International Version maintains great popularity among Mennonite Brethren because of its readability. Those who prefer to use inclusive language to refer to humans favour the New Revised Standard Version. The New American Standard Version gives the closest word-forword translation from the original languages.

知识的言语与预言（Words of Knowledge and Prophecy）

对启蒙运动视角的幻灭，似乎重新点燃了人们对超现象/超自然维度（paraphenomenal dimension）的兴趣。人们渴望明白上帝的旨意，希望能预知未来。当科学声称掌控公共领域、而巨大的范式转移却使社会动荡不安时，个人生活的不确定性便变得越来越难以忍受。依赖超自然或带有巫术色彩的神秘洞见，对基督徒也构成了日益增大的诱惑。

在此有必要提出几点警戒：

第一，所有关于先知性光照（prophetic illumination）的主张，都必须与圣经的启示保持一致。绝不能赋予任何预言性言语高于圣经本身的权威（申命记 13章；18:9-22）。

第二，旧约先知在与假先知的激烈争战中警告人们：当上帝宣告审判时，切莫轻信宣告救恩与平安的神谕。同样，对于那些许诺财富繁荣与身体健康的言语（成功神学），应当予以特别严格的审视与检验。

第三，凡宣称具有特殊洞见的言语，都必须在信仰共同体中接受检验，尤其是由地方教会领袖进行辨识与检验。

第四，要警惕对巫术化倾向（magical penchant）的日益沉迷。在努力寻求明了上帝指引的过程中，如果有人许诺无需经过共同体辨析即可提供捷径般的神秘洞见，跟从这样的人务须保持高度谨慎。

Words of Knowledge and Prophecy

Dissatisfaction with the Enlightenment perspective seems to have rekindled interest in the paraphenomenal dimension. People want to know God’s will. They want to be able to anticipate the future. Uncertainty about personal life becomes increasingly unacceptable when science claims to control the public domain, and yet huge paradigm shifts destabilize society. Reliance on supernatural or magical insight increasingly tempts Christians as well. Several words of caution are in order. First, all claims of prophetic illumination must be consistent with the biblical revelation. No prophetic word can be granted greater authority than the Bible itself (Deuteronomy 13; 18:9-22). Second, the Old Testament prophets in their struggle with false prophets warned against accepting oracles of salvation when God had announced judgment. Similarly, words that promise prosperity and health should be tested with special scrutiny. Third, every word that claims special insight must be tested within the community, especially by local church leaders. Fourth, be aware of the increasing penchant for the magical. Exercise caution in following those who promise insights that avoid community discernment in struggling to know God’s direction.

共同体释经学（Community Hermeneutics）

针对本信仰条款的评注概述了在理解圣经文学各样体裁时，需要具备释经上的严谨度与成熟技巧（hermeneutical sophistication）。例如，研读诗歌所使用的释经工具，自然不同于研读历史叙事文献所使用的工具。

评注同时强调：对圣经的诠释乃是圣灵在信徒共同体内部的工作。上帝应许圣灵必引导我们明白一切的真理。上帝的追随者在圣灵的引导下，与基督徒共同体中的其他成员展开积极对话，并发现上帝显明了真理，赋予人们在充满挑战与变迁的时代中生活的确据与信心。

这一真理具有若干实践层面的重要意义：

第一，那些既学会辨明上帝心意、又掌握包括精通原文在内的释经工具的教师，应当在圣经诠释中发挥引领作用。尽管他们并不因其学术素养而享有更高的特权权威，但当他们宣讲上帝的话语时，教会理当给予他们应有的尊重。

第二，当议题对于单个地方堂会而言过于复杂或引发分歧时，我们应当与同区会/联会（conference）内的其他教会共同协商。使徒行传第15章所树立的榜样，至今仍完全适用于我们。

第三，共同的辨析可能会考验我们的合一。在日益多元化的世界中，共识并非总能迅速达成。尤其在出现分歧之时，更需要悉心培养彼此之间的相互信任。

第四，讨论应当以基督里的爱心（charity）为特征。

第五，健康的冲突实际上能够促进教会的健康（哥林多前书 11:19）。

Community Hermeneutics

The commentary on this article of faith outlines the need for hermeneutical sophistication in understanding the various genres of biblical literature. One will pursue the study of poetry with different exegetical tools than are used with historical literature, for example.

The commentary also insists that interpretation of Scripture is the work of the Holy Spirit within the community of believers. God promises that the Holy Spirit will lead us into all truth. God’s followers, guided by the Holy Spirit, enter into active dialogue with others in the Christian community and discover that God reveals the truth, giving people confidence to live in challenging, changing times.

There are several practical implications of this truth. First, teachers who have learned both to discern the will of God and to use exegetical tools that include facility with the original languages are to give leadership in biblical interpretation. Although they have no greater authority because of their academic preparation, the church does well to show them respect as they proclaim the Word of God. Second, when issues become too complex or divisive for a single congregation, we do well to consult with other congregations in our conference. The Acts 15 model is appropriate for us today. Third, mutual discernment may test our unity. In an increasingly diverse world, consensus will not always come quickly. Mutual trust will need to be nurtured especially in times of dissension. Fourth, discussion should be characterized by charity. Fifth, healthy conflict can actually build church health (1 Corinthians 11:19).

认识上帝（Knowing God）

创造主邀请受造物进入与祂的相交关系之中。当我们在圣经中与上帝相遇时，我们发现自己——正如那些生平被记录在圣经中的男男女女一样——蒙受恩召，进入与上帝的亲密关系之中。

当我们藉着圣经认识上帝的心怀，并在事奉中顺服上帝的心意时，我们就在与上帝充满生机的动态关系中渐渐成长。真正认识上帝会彻底改变我们。当我们认识圣经中所启示的上帝时，我们就会对我们应当成为怎样的人建立起坚定的确信。当我们逐渐明白圣经时，我们就会深切关注在日常每时每刻中对上帝的顺服。我们学习认识那位在受造界、圣经、耶稣、圣灵以及基督徒共同体中所启示的上帝。

Knowing God

The Creator invites the created into relationship. As we meet God in the Scriptures we find ourselves—like the women and men whose stories are captured in the Scriptures—invited into an intimate relationship with God. As we get to know the heart of God through Scripture and as we serve God’s purposes, we grow in a dynamic relationship with God. Getting to know God changes us. As we get to know God as revealed in the Bible, we develop convictions about the kind of people we ought to be. As we come to understand the Scriptures, we come to care deeply about a daily, moment-bymoment obedience to God. We learn to know God as revealed in creation, the Scriptures, Jesus, the Holy Spirit, and the Christian community.

参考文献 四、参考书目与文献导读

ARTICLE 03 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 03 条：创造与人类 (Creation and Humanity)

Article 3: Creation and Humanity

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

创造 (Creation)

我们相信，起初上帝创造天地，万物都甚好。一切受造之物都彰显了上帝的主权旨意与精妙设计，但与造物主始终有别。宇宙属于上帝，祂以慈爱照料并喜悦维系着万有。受造界宣扬上帝的智慧与大能，呼召万民敬拜祂。

Creation

We believe that in the beginning God created the heavens and the earth, and they were very good. All of creation expresses God’s sovereign will and design, but remains distinct from the Creator. The universe belongs to God, who takes care and delight in sustaining it. Creation declares God’s wisdom and power, calling all to worship Him.

人类 (Humanity)

人类作为上帝创造之工的巅峰，受造的原意是要与上帝建立相通团契，并在彼此互助的关系中生活。上帝照着自己的形象造男造女。造物主赋予人类治理并看顾受造界的使命，视之为神圣的托付，并赐予人顺服或悖逆祂的自由。因着亚当和夏娃蓄意的悖逆，罪进入了世界。结果，人性遭到扭曲，人类与上帝及受造界隔绝疏离。受造之物服在败坏的辖制之下。人类与一切受造之物同切望得享释放。

Humanity

Humans, the crowning act of creation, were designed to live in fellowship with God and in mutually helpful relationships with each other. God created them male and female in the image of God. The Creator gave them the mandate to rule and care for creation as a sacred trust, and the freedom to obey or disobey Him. Through the willful disobedience of Adam and Eve, sin entered the world. As a result, human nature is distorted and people are alienated from God and creation. Creation is under the bondage of decay. Humans and all creation long to be set free.

新创造 (The New Creation)

罪恶、过犯与死亡终必不能得胜。上帝将要创造新天新地，在那里不再有邪恶、痛苦与死亡。这新创造的初熟征兆，已经显明在那些借着基督接受上帝赦免的人身上。在基督里，万有正在与上帝和好，并被重新创造。

The New Creation

Sin, guilt, and death will not prevail. God will create a new heaven and a new earth in which there will be no evil, suffering, and death. The first signs of this new creation are already present in those who accept God’s forgiveness through Christ. In Christ, all things are being reconciled and created anew.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 1–3章；诗篇 8:6；19:1-6；24:1-2；89:11；95:5；104篇；箴言 8:22-31；以赛亚书 40:12-31；44:24；约翰福音 1:1-4, 10；17:5；罗马书 1:19-20；5:17, 21；6:4；8:18-25；哥林多前书 8:6；15:20-27；哥林多后书 3:18；4:6；5:16-19；加拉太书 3:28；6:15；以弗所书 1:4, 9-10；2:11-22；4:24；歌罗西书 1:15-17；希伯来书 11:3；启示录 4:8-11；21:1-5；22:13。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 1-3; Psalm 8:6; 19:1-6; 24:1-2; 89:11; 95:5; 104; Proverbs 8:22-31; Isaiah 40:12-31; 44:24; John 1:1-4, 10; 17:5; Romans 1:19-20; 5:17, 21; 6:4; 8:18-25; 1 Corinthians 8:6; 15:20-27; 2 Corinthians 3:18; 4:6; 5:16-19; Galatians 3:28; 6:15; Ephesians 1:4, 9-10; 2:11-22; 4:24; Colossians 1:15-17; Hebrews 11:3; Revelation 4:8-11; 21:1-5; 22:13.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

本信仰条文的目的有二：首先，它旨在确立圣经关于上帝与受造宇宙之关系的教导；其次，它旨在就人类的起源、在受造界中的角色、当前的处境及其终极归宿作出清晰阐述。

The purpose of this article of faith is twofold. First, it is designed to affirm what the Scriptures teach about the relationship between God and the created universe. Second, it is intended to make a statement about humanity in regards to its origins, its role in creation, its present condition, and its ultimate destiny.

创造 (Creation)

第一段的目的，是回应现今社会中关于上帝与宇宙关系的几种普遍信念。一方面，宇宙源于偶然碰撞、地上生命乃盲目宿命之产物的观念，已被大众普遍接受为真理。人们在意识中不再将宇宙的存在与一位具有位格、满有慈爱、全能之上帝的有意且良善的作为联系在一起。在整个二十世纪，通常被称为进化论的达尔文主义（Darwinism），一直是推动这一思想转变的最强大力量。在其最通俗的表述中，该理论教导宇宙的存在与人类在地球上的出现，皆是一场由偶然巧合所启动的强大自然过程的产物。根据达尔文主义理论，宇宙形成的根本法则是随机性（randomness），而非一位满怀慈爱且具位格之上帝的心意。

另一方面，尤其是在“新纪元运动”（New Age movement）的影响下，相信存在着某种所有生命皆参与其中的“宇宙原力/普遍力量”，已成为一种流行风尚。根据这种世界观，人的生命并没有特殊的尊贵价值，人的个体性与独特性除了最终消融于这种非位格的生命力量之中外，别无其他归宿与目的。这一因新纪元运动以及《星球大战》（Star Wars）等电影而广为流传的观念，被称为泛神论（pantheism）。

由于我们身处一个对上帝与自然界之界限日益混淆的社会，教会宣告基督信仰与泛神论之间的根本区别就显得格外关键。泛神论主张上帝就是赋予宇宙生机的各种生命力量的总和。其推论是：如果物质宇宙不复存在，那么神明——或宇宙中被视为原始力量的存在——也将不复存在。然而，基督信仰教导我们，宇宙是暂时的（temporal）且附从偶存的（contingent）。换句话说，宇宙在时间上有起点，也有终点；它的存在完全仰赖上帝在历史中的介入以及祂持续不断的托住与维系。

泛神论与进化主义不仅影响我们如何看待宇宙和上帝，也深刻塑造了我们对人类的认知，以及对“何为人类”的定义。如果宇宙确实纯属偶然事故的产物，而人类不过是盲目进化过程的最终副产品，那么人的生命便毫无终极意义与价值可言。如此一来，每个人生存的目的就只能窄化为传递自身基因，以及为人类物种的演化作出贡献。一旦这一目的达成，个体的继续存在便不再具有任何必要性，除了为族群的基因存续效力之外，生命便再无更深层的目的。

这种进化主义以及新纪元意识形态的最终结局，实际上是在思想中彻底排除了圣经所启示的具有位格、道德属性且仁慈良善的上帝。在此，我们必须认清做出这种抉择所带来的严重后果。我们绝不可能在试图抹杀圣经所描绘的具位格之上帝的同时，还能保留对“人之本质与尊严”的合宜理解。若没有一个绝对的参照坐标，人类就根本无法理解人性和生存的根本特质。在缺乏这种基准的情况下，主观主义便成了界定人类与世界关系的唯一运作原则。人类于是成为自身的参照物；人类实际上将自己树立为宇宙的中心。这种哲学立场对整个伦理道德体系和人类各项事业带来了极其可怕的冲击。我们如何看待人的生命，以及如何应对诸如堕胎、安乐死、安宁缓和医疗（palliative care）、法律执行、国家本质等一系列伦理议题，完全取决于我们的参照坐标。没有上帝，我们注定会遗忘究竟是什么真正界定了人性，进而丧失行怜悯与慈悲的基石。

圣经教导我们，宇宙绝非某种随机、匿名或盲目的原始力量运作的后果。根据创世记中的创造记叙，宇宙是由一位具有位格、圣洁公义且充满怜悯的造物主所创造的；祂渴望与人类建立彼此相爱、互动相通的关系（创世记 1:1–2:1；诗篇 8:3-8；24:1-2；89:11；95:5；104:1-35；箴言 8:22-31；以赛亚书 40:12-31；约翰福音 1:1-4, 10；17:5；哥林多前书 8:6；歌罗西书 1:15-17；希伯来书 11:3；启示录 4:8-11）。

Creation

The purpose of the first paragraph is to respond to some widely-held beliefs in our society about the relationship between God and the universe. On one hand, the notion that the universe is the result of chance and that life on earth is the outcome of blind fate is generally accepted as true. The existence of the universe is no longer consciously associated with the intentional and benevolent action of a personal, loving, all-powerful Being. Darwinism, usually called the theory of evolution, has throughout the 20th century been the most powerful cause behind this development. In its most popular formulation, the theory teaches that the existence of the universe and the presence of humanity on the earth are the result of a powerful process which was set into motion by chance. According to Darwinian theory, randomness is the basic principle behind the formation of the universe rather than the intention of a personal and loving God.

On the other hand, it has become fashionable, particularly under the influence of the New Age movement, to believe that there is some universal force in which all living things participate. According to this worldview, human life has no special value and human individuality has no other destiny and purpose than to be dissolved in this impersonal life force. This concept, popularized by the New Age movement and by such movies as Star Wars, is known as pantheism.

Because we live in a society in which there is increasing confusion about God and nature, it becomes correspondingly vital for the Church to proclaim that there is a fundamental difference between Christianity and pantheism. Pantheism teaches that God is the sum total of the vital forces which animate the universe. The implication is that if the physical universe were to cease to exist, God, or whatever is understood as the primal force in the universe, would cease to exist as well. The Christian faith teaches that the universe is temporal and contingent. In other words, the universe has a temporal beginning and an end, and it depends on a historical intervention of God and His continual sustenance for its existence.

Pantheism and evolutionism not only affect how we view the universe and God, they also profoundly influence the way we perceive human beings and define what it means to be human. If the universe is indeed the result of an accident, and humankind the end-product of a blind evolutionary process, then human life has no ultimate meaning and significance. The purpose of each person’s life can then be focused on the need to transmit one’s genes and to contribute to the evolution of the human race. Once this purpose has been fulfilled, there is then no need for the person’s continued existence and no further purpose beyond that person’s contribution to the genetic survival of the race.

The end-result of such evolutionism, as well as of New Age ideology, is virtual elimination of the notion of a personal, moral, benevolent God. At this point, it is important to realize the grave implications of such a choice. We cannot pretend to eliminate the notion of a personal God, as portrayed in Scripture, without also eliminating a certain understanding of what it means to be human. It is ultimately impossible to understand the fundamental character of human nature and existence without an absolute point of reference. In the absence of such a context, subjectivity becomes the only operative principle in defining the relationship between humankind and the world. Humanity then becomes its own point of reference; it literally becomes the centre of the universe. Such a philosophical stance has formidable implications for the whole spectrum of ethics and human endeavours. The way we view human life and how we deal with ethical issues such as abortion, euthanasia, palliative care, law enforcement, the nature of the state, and so on, depend entirely on our frame of reference. Without God, we are condemned to forgetting what truly defines human nature and thus the rationale for compassion. The Scriptures teach that the universe is not the result of some random, anonymous, or blind primal force. According to the creation account found in Genesis, the universe was created by a personal, moral, and compassionate being who wishes to be in a loving and reciprocal relationship with humanity (Genesis 1:1-2:1; Psalm 8:3-8; 24:1-2; 89:11; 95:5; 104:1-35; Proverbs 8:2231; Isaiah 40:12-31; John 1:1-4, 10; 17:5; 1 Corinthians 8:6; Colossians 1:15-17; Hebrews 11:3; Revelation 4:8-11).

人类 (Humanity)

圣经的记载极其强调人的生命的独特性与神圣性。根据圣经，人的生命拥有特殊的价值与尊严，因为男女皆是照着上帝的形象受造的（创世记 1:26-27；9:6）。这一最根本的特质表明，人类受召在受造界中代表上帝。在圣经时代，理想的君王或其代理人的显著特征，是对臣民怀有深切的爱，并以整个王国的福祉为最高关切。理想的君王绝不滥用权力去剥削臣民、使百姓陷入困苦；相反，他全力以赴在自己的国度中促进和平、繁荣与公义（诗篇 72篇）。作为上帝的代表，人类肩负着看顾上帝的受造物、并为了受造界所有现今与未来的居住者的福祉妥善管理世界的神圣责任。治理全地的使命，绝未给近代因技术发展而演变出的自私且短视的剥削掠夺提供合法借口。恰恰相反，人类作为上帝的代表，必须为是否智慧善用地球的资源而向上帝交账。

然而，上帝与人类之间这种宣告人类依上帝形象而造的特殊关系，并不能涵盖故事的全貌。普世皆公认人性中存在着某种错谬扭曲。尽管人类能够取得极其辉煌卓越的成就，但人类的历史和我们内心的省察却迫使我们承认：在人心最深处的隐秘角落，潜藏着某种扭曲与败坏（罗马书 3:9, 23；5:12；7:7-25；以弗所书 2:1-3）。根据圣经记载，这种人性深层扭曲的根源，正在于人类行使自由意志的能力（创世记 3:1-24；4:1-13）。

在此需要指出的是，人类堕落的故事固然极其悲惨，但它同时也强有力地肯定了上帝赋予人类自由意志的崇高价值。创世记第3章提醒我们，人类拥有做出重大抉择的能力，而这些抉择塑造着他们的未来。这彻底粉碎了千百年来影响人类历史的各种还原论与决定论（宿命论）哲学体系。星象并不能预先决定人类的性格与命运——不论是古代美索不达米亚人的占星迷信，还是今天许多当代人的盲从。男女的命运也绝非单单由遗传基因、家庭背景、社会学或心理学因素所绝对决定。甚至连污鬼邪灵也无法对人类的心灵施加绝对的主宰（马可福音 5:1-20）。堕落的记叙强有力地证实：人类有能力在善与恶之间行使自由意志，并由此影响甚至彻底改变自身的命运（创世记 2:16-17；约书亚记 22:5；24:14-15；箴言 2:1-5；罗马书 12:1-2）。然而正如经文所记载，人类决意悖逆上帝，结果导致人与上帝隔绝、与自我分裂，并与自然疏离。这种悖逆给人类带来了毁灭性的后果。死亡以及各种形态的痛苦，皆直接源于这种不愿信靠上帝的心态与作为（罗马书 5:12）。

Humanity

The biblical record emphasizes the uniqueness and the sacredness of human life. According to the Scriptures, human life has special value and dignity because men and women are made in the image of God (Genesis 1:26-27; 9:6). This most basic trait indicates that human beings are called to represent God in creation. In biblical times, the ideal king or his representative was characterized by a profound love for his subjects and by an overriding concern for the welfare of his entire kingdom. The ideal king did not abuse his power by exploiting and impoverishing his subjects; he was to give his total attention to promoting peace, prosperity, and justice in his realm (Psalm 72). As representatives of God, human beings are responsible to care for God’s creation and to manage it for the benefit of all its present and future inhabitants. The mandate to rule the earth in no way legitimizes the selfish and shortsighted exploitation made possible in recent history by technology. On the contrary, as God’s representatives, humans are accountable to God for wise use of the earth’s resources.

This special relationship between God and humanity that declares humankind as created in the image of God, however, does not tell the whole story. It is universally recognized that there is something wrong in human nature. While human beings are capable of noble accomplishments, human history and our own self-awareness force us to recognize that there is something warped in the deepest recesses of the human heart (Romans 3:9,23; 5:12; 7:7-25; Ephesians 2:1-3). According to the biblical record, the genesis of this deep distortion of human nature can be found in humanity’s ability to exercise free will (Genesis 3:1-24; 4:1-13).

At this point, it is important to note that as tragic as the story of the fall might seem, it is also a powerful affirmation of the value God attributes to human free will. Genesis 3 reminds us that human beings have the capability to make significant choices, and that these choices shape their future. This flies squarely in the face of all the reductionist and deterministic philosophical systems which have shaped human history over the centuries. The stars do not predetermine the character and destiny of human beings, as the ancient Mesopotamians believed and as many of our contemporaries accept. Men and women are not solely the result of genetic, family, sociological, or psychological factors. Even demons cannot exert absolute control over the human spirit (Mark 5:1-20). The story of the fall forcefully affirms that human beings have the capability to exercise their free will in respect to good and evil, and can thus influence or even radically change their destiny (Genesis 2:16-17; Joshua 22:5; 24:14-15; Proverbs 2:1-5; Romans 12:1-2). But as the biblical text records, human beings decided to disobey God, resulting in alienation from God, from themselves, and from nature. The consequences of this disobedience have been catastrophic for the human race. Death and suffering in all of its manifestations have resulted directly from this unwillingness to trust God (Romans 5:12).

新创造 (The New Creation)

尽管人类当前的处境充满危难与困顿，圣经的记载却坚决宣告：悖逆及其对人类和受造界所造成的毁灭性影响绝非最终结局。上帝深爱世人，并且无条件地委身于祂起初的心意——即建立一群由甘心乐意永远爱祂、事奉祂的男女所构成的子民。上帝开辟了一条再造之路，来重修因人的悖逆而毁坏的万有。我们或许无法完全参透其中的奥秘，但上帝必须行作一件具有宇宙性规模的大工，来为人类成就救赎。上帝差遣祂自己的独生子受死在十字架上，好使我们能在最深刻的意义上与祂和好、与自我和好，并与受造界和好。圣经表明，这种和好的功效并非专为遥远的将来所保留。圣经反复宣告，上帝救赎大工的果效在现今这个时代就已经清晰可见并大有成效。这新创造的初熟征兆，已显明在那些借着耶稣基督接受上帝的邀请与祂和好的人身上（罗马书 5:17, 21；6:4；8:18-25；哥林多前书 15:20-27；哥林多后书 3:18；4:6；5:16-19；加拉太书 3:28；6:15；以弗所书 1:4, 9-10；2:11-22；4:24；歌罗西书 1:15-17；启示录 21:1-5）。

The New Creation

In spite of the precarious character of humanity’s condition, the biblical record forcefully asserts that disobedience and its devastating effects on humanity and creation are not final. God loves humanity and is unconditionally committed to bringing about His original project to create a people composed of men and women who will freely love and serve Him for all eternity. God has provided a way to recreate what was destroyed through human disobedience. We do not fully understand why, but it was necessary for God to do something of cosmic proportion to provide redemption for humanity. God sent His own Son to die on a cross so that we may, in the most profound sense of the word, be reconciled with Him, with ourselves, and with creation. The Bible states that the outworkings of this reconciliation are not exclusively reserved for some future time. Scriptures repeatedly state that the results of God’s redemptive work are already visible and effectual in the present time. The first signs of this new creation are visible in those who accept God’s invitation to be reconciled to Himself through Jesus Christ (Romans 5:17, 21; 6:4; 8:18-25; 1 Corinthians 15:20-27; 2 Corinthians 3:18; 4:6; 5:16-19; Galatians 3:28; 6:15; Ephesians 1:4, 9-10; 2:11-22; 4:24; Colossians 1:15-17; Revelation 21:1-5).

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

生命中最基本的需求之一，就是明白我们从何而来、为何在此，以及将往何处去。信仰宣言第3条正是针对这些核心议题作出了回应。关于生命的起源、我们与环境的关系，以及上帝对其受造界逐步展开的计划，都属于此探讨的范畴。第3条为我们提供了一个清晰的圣经架构，使我们得以明白自己在宇宙中所处的角色与使命。

A basic need in life is to know where we are from, why we are here, and where we are going. These essential issues are addressed in Article 3 of the confession. Questions regarding the origin of life, our relationship to the environment, and God’s unfolding plan for His creation are all part of this discussion. Article 3 gives us a biblical framework for understanding our part in the universe.

圣经与科学 (Scripture and Science)

宣告上帝是宇宙的创造者，告诉了我们究竟从何而来。虽然许多人借助探索行星、恒星以及太空最遥远的深处来探寻物质宇宙和生命的起源，但圣经告诉我们：“起初，上帝创造天地”（创世记 1:1）。我们的宇宙绝非凭机缘偶然演变而来；我们并不是某种随机而混乱的意外产物。相反，我们是上帝精妙设计的一部分。上帝是我们生命的赐予者，我们唯独从祂得着存留。

国际知名天文学家、宇宙生命领域的权威罗伯特·贾斯特罗（Robert Jastrow）曾发表过如下著名评论：

“这已不再是再苦干一年、多研究十年、多一次精密测量或提出另一种理论的问题；就目前而言，科学似乎永远无法彻底揭开创造奥秘的面纱。对于那些靠着对理性力量的笃信而活的科学家来说，故事的结局就像一场噩梦。他们翻山越岭，攀登无知的高山；他们即将征服最高的山峰；当他们费尽全力翻过最后一块岩石时，迎候他们的却是一群早已在那里静坐了数个世纪的神学家。”（《上帝与天文学家》，第115–116页）

这就引出了神学与科学之间如何关联的问题。自伽利略时代以来，教会就一直在这一问题上竭力探索与角力。今天，我们对创造论与进化论之间的争辩都十分熟悉。有一种被称为“圣经创造论”（biblical creationism）的观点，旨在反击科学界的进化论。该观点试图从创世记的记叙中挖掘科学数据，却往往忽略了经文本身所蕴含的神学初衷。圣经确实教导上帝创造了天地，人类在上帝的受造物中独具地位；然而上帝究竟“如何”成就这一切，圣经并未详加说明。我们教会中的一些人认为圣经教导上帝是在不超过一万年前、于六个由24小时构成的自然日中创造了宇宙。另一些人则认为上帝完全可以使用更漫长的时间跨度，甚至包括融入现代科学某些发现的受造演进过程。在我们对待创造的具体观点上，应当允许存有差异，并在主里彼此尊重。将对创世记的某一种特定解经与科学发现直接对立起来，或者简单粗暴地将人贴上“圣经创造论者”或“进化无神论者”的标签，对教会的建造毫无益处。

一种更为有益的途径，是让圣经教导与科学探索彼此启发、相互对话。约翰·波尔金霍恩（John C. Polkinghorne）是剑桥大学的物理学家，既是一位享誉世界的著名科学家，又是英国圣公会的牧师。在他的《科学时代中的上帝信仰》（Belief in God in an Age of Science）一书中，波尔金霍恩阐述了一种因科学探索而得到坚固而非受到威胁的信仰。他既能坚定地宣告上帝是造物主，又能欣然接纳一种流动、灵活且向上帝护理（divine providence）敞开的宇宙科学观。波尔金霍恩将上帝创造宇宙的具体过程留给科学研究去探索。在他看来，受造界是一个值得探索的奥秘。我们在有关创造的讲道与教导中，必须审慎自守，只宣讲圣经明确启示的真理。圣经与科学绝非势不两立。

Scripture and Science

To state that God is the Creator of the universe tells us where we come from. While many study the planets, stars, and furthest reaches of space to try to discover the origins of the physical universe and life, Scripture tells us, “In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth” (Genesis 1:1). Our universe did not evolve by chance. We are not part of a random and chaotic accident. Rather, we are part of God’s design. God is the one to whom we owe our existence.

Robert Jastrow, an internationally known astronomer and authority on life in the cosmos, makes the following comment: It is not a matter of another year, another decade of work, another measurement, or another theory; at this moment it seems as though science will never be able to raise the curtain on the mystery of creation. For scientists who have lived by their faith in the power of reason, the story ends like a bad dream. They have scaled the mountains of ignorance; they are about to conquer the highest peak; as they pull themselves over the final rock, they are greeted by a band of theologians who have been sitting there for centuries (115-116).

This raises the question of how theology relates to science. The church has been wrestling with this issue since the time of Galileo. Today we are aware of the debate between creation and evolution. One view, known as “biblical creationism,” seeks to counter scientific theories of evolution. It mines the Genesis account for scientific data, more than recognizing its theological intent. Scripture does teach that God created the heavens and the earth and that human beings are unique among God’s creation. Exactly how God accomplished this is not altogether clear. Some in our churches understand Scripture to teach that God created the universe in six 24-hour days no more than 10,000 years ago. Others leave room for God to have used longer periods of time, including an evolving process of creation that incorporates some of the findings of modern science. We should allow for differences in our views of creation and exercise mutual respect for one another. To pit an interpretation of Genesis against the findings of science or to label people as either biblical creationists or evolutionary atheists does not serve us well. A more helpful approach is to allow biblical teaching and scientific inquiry to inform each other. John C. Polkinghorne is a Cambridge physicist who is both a renowned scientist and an Anglican clergyman. In his book Belief in God in an Age of Science, Polkinghorne articulates a faith which is strengthened rather than threatened by scientific inquiry. He is able to affirm God as Creator and embrace a scientific view of the universe which is fluid, flexible, and open to divine providence. Polkinghorne leaves the process God used in creating the universe open to scientific investigation. In his mind, creation remains a mystery worth exploring. We must be careful in our preaching and teaching about creation to affirm only what the Bible says. Scripture and science need not be antagonistic.

上帝与受造界 (God and Creation)

后现代文化的一个显著趋势，是从机械论的宇宙观转向某种更具“属灵”色彩的宇宙观。诸如《星球大战》（Star Wars）与《狮子王》（The Lion King）之类的电影，模糊了上帝与自然界的本质界限。上帝被贬低为一种“非位格的生命原力”，抑或大自然被神化为永恒轮转的“生命之环”（生生不息）。这直接触及了敬拜的核心问题：大自然取代了上帝成为终极实在。其结果便是敬拜受造之物而不敬拜造物的主——保罗将这种行为归咎于人类的背逆（罗马书 1:25）。

对环境的过度执迷同样可能导致自然崇拜。为了纠正过去对生态环境的漠视，当代文化很容易走向另一个极端，即把大自然“灵性化”，赋予其神圣地位。这种情感极易演变成一种带有崇拜光环的敬意。人们宣称神圣实在就蕴藏在大自然本身之中，以致上帝与受造物之间的界限荡然无存。

这种立场之所以存在严重偏差，至少有两大原因：首先，脱离上帝而企图拯救大自然的努力是苍白空洞的。普天之下没有任何人比造物主上帝更深爱受造之物。任何真正的环保关怀，都必须植根于上帝对祂宇宙的慈爱与呵护。若以牺牲造物主为代价来膜拜受造界，实际上是摧毁了生态关怀的根本基石。其次，受造界本身完全明白自己对上帝的依附关系，并将荣耀归给作为宇宙创造者与维系者的上帝（诗篇 19:1-6；24:1-2；104篇）。我们作为上帝的受造物，理当紧随受造界的引领去敬拜独一真活上帝，而非膜拜大自然。

这对教会的崇拜生活也带来了牧养层面的启迪。我们应当鼓励在上帝所造的大自然中举行户外崇拜。教会营会、公园夏季崇拜以及户外洗礼，都能增进我们对上帝的赞美。然而，若有人主张“在大自然之美中经历上帝”可以替代信徒定期聚集敬拜，我们就必须严肃拷问：我们的敬拜焦点究竟何在？我们虽然欣赏上帝的创造，惊叹于上帝的奇妙作为，但我们的敬拜绝不能导向受造界，而必须单单归给造物的主宰。

God and Creation

The trend in our postmodern culture is to move from a mechanistic to a more “spiritual” view of the universe. Movies such as Star Wars and The Lion King obscure the distinction between God and nature. God is reduced to an “impersonal life force” or nature is deified as a “circle of life.” This relates to the issue of worship. God is replaced by nature as the ultimate reality. The result is that creation is worshipped instead of the Creator—an act Paul attributes to human disobedience (Romans 1:25).

Excessive concern for the environment can also lead to nature worship. In addressing past neglect of the environment, the temptation in our culture is to “spiritualize” nature, granting it divine status. Such feelings can take on an aura of worship. Divine reality is said to be found in nature itself. Any distinction between God and creation is lost.

Such a position is problematic for at least two reasons. First, its attempt to rescue nature without reference to God rings hollow. No one cares more for creation than God its Creator. Any concern for the environment must remain grounded in God’s love and care for His universe. To worship creation at the expense of the Creator undermines the very foundation of environmental concern. Second, creation itself recognizes its dependent relationship to God and gives Him glory as Creator and Sustainer of the universe (Psalm 19:1-6; 24:1-2; 104). As God’s creatures, we are to follow creation’s lead in worshipping God instead of nature.

There are also implications for congregational worship. We should encourage outdoor worship settings in God’s creation. Church camps, summer worship services in the park, and outdoor baptisms can all enhance our praise to God. However, when some propose that experiencing God in the beauty of nature is an acceptable alternative to gathering regularly with God’s people for worship, we should raise the question of where our worship is focused. While we appreciate God’s creation and marvel at God’s handiwork, our worship is to be directed not to creation but to God the Creator.

人类与受造界 (Humanity and Creation)

作为人类，我们应当如何与受造界相处？我们需要警惕并避免两种极端。

一种极端是将人类仅仅视为众多生命形态中的普通一种，认为人类并不比花草、树木或动物更具价值。试对比一下公众对拯救濒危动物物种的热情与对饥饿儿童困境的关注程度即可见一斑。曾有汽车保险杠贴纸写道：“拯救鲸鱼，鱼叉刺人。”对许多人来说，动物权益竟然变得比人权更为重要。

然而圣经明确教导，人类在上帝的一切造物中具有无可比拟的独特性。唯有人是按着上帝的形象受造的（创世记 1:26-27）。这一事实将我们与一切其他受造物截然区别开来。诗篇第8篇将人类的尊贵地位置于浩瀚宇宙以及栖息在地上的走兽、飞鸟和游鱼之中来阐述。上帝叫我们“比天使微小一点”（诗篇 8:5），并赐给我们远超其他生命的荣耀与尊贵为冠冕。尽管一切生命皆有其价值，但没有任何生命的价值能超越人的生命。如果有人对自己宠物的健康关切备至，却对同胞肢体的福祉漠不关心，这便是对人之生命价值的严重曲解。我们对人生命的信念，直接关乎诸如堕胎、安乐死以及基因研究等重大伦理议题（参见第14条“生命的神圣性”）。

我们需要避免的第二种极端，是认为人类凌驾于受造界之上的崇高地位，赋予了人随意支配甚至滥用受造物的特权。将治理全地的权柄扭曲为任意宰割、毁损和毁灭的特许状。某石油公司的一则广告曾宣称：“大自然固然顽强，但我们比它更强悍。”这种态度把地球当作纯粹可以买卖、剥削、虐待、消费并随意遗弃的商品。

近几十年来兴起的环保意识在很大程度上纠正了这种错误态度，但许多教会在这方面却往往滞后。例如，一项旨在调查人们对动物世界之态度的研究表明：一个人参加宗教崇拜聚会的频率越高，此人对自然界持专横或压迫态度的倾向反而越严重（Granberg-Michaelson, 2-3）。究其原因，何以致此？

原因之一，可能是基督徒不愿被等同于激进极端环保主义者的政治诉求。另一个原因，则在于缺乏合乎圣经的健全未来观与末世观。人们往往会想：既然上帝将来要创造新天新地（彼得后书 3:11-13；启示录 21:1-5），那我们为何还要费心保护现今的环境呢？正如一首福音诗歌所唱：“这世界非我家，我不过是客旅。”

这种对受造界漫不经心、轻率随意的态度，忽视了两个核心真理：其一，上帝在基督里叫万有与祂和好的终极旨意，不仅包括人，也包括上帝的整个受造界及其中的各样造物（以弗所书 1:9-10；歌罗西书 1:19-20）。其二，基准于上帝对宇宙的复兴重塑蓝图，上帝托付给人照管受造界的使命从未中止（创世记 1:28；2:15；诗篇 8:6）。

圣经教导我们，承担生态环境责任是基督徒的神圣使命。在所有人当中，基督徒面对环境保护理应是最负责任的群体。何以见得？部分原因在于我们受召去关爱他人——例如积极应对污染威胁、推动可持续发展、关注全人类的生存危机等等。然而，承担环保责任的终极理由，在于基督徒深切明白与造物主建立个人关系的真义：是祂创造了我们，创造了世界，并深切顾惜祂所造的一切生灵与万象。如果我们深爱并敬畏我们的造物主，就必然会爱惜看顾祂的创造。这具体落实于在我们的家庭、职场和教会中，自觉努力节制消费、珍惜自然资源并积极推行废物循环回收。

Humanity and Creation

How do we as human beings relate to creation? There are two extremes to avoid. One is to consider human beings as merely one life form among others with no more significance than the grass, the trees, or the animals. Compare, for instance, the degree of public interest in saving threatened animal species to the plight of starving children. There is a bumper sticker that says, “Save a whale; Harpoon a human.” For many, animal rights have become more important than human rights.

Scripture teaches that human beings are unique among God’s creation. Only people are created in God’s image (Genesis 1:26-27). This sets us apart from all of creation. Psalm 8 speaks about our significance in relation to the vast expanse of the universe and the animals, birds, and fish which inhabit our planet. We are made just a little less than God (Psalm 8:5). God has crowned us with glory and honour beyond that of other life. Although all life is valued, no life is more valuable than human life. People who are more committed to the health of their pets than the well-being of their fellow human beings distort the value of human life. What we believe about human life has implications for ethical issues such as abortion, euthanasia, and genetic research. (See article 14, “Sanctity of Life.”) There is a second extreme to avoid. It advocates that our superior position over the rest of creation gives us the right to use or abuse it at will. Exercising dominion over the earth becomes a licence to dominate, deface, and destroy. A commercial for an oil company stated, “Nature is tough, but we are tougher.” This attitude treats the earth as a commodity which can be bought and sold, exploited and abused, consumed and discarded.

Concern for the environment in recent decades has corrected much of this attitude, but many churches tend to lag behind in this area. A survey taken to determine attitudes toward the animal world, for instance, revealed that the more frequently a person attended religious services, the greater the person’s tendency was to hold domineering or oppressive attitudes toward nature (Granberg-Michaelson, 2-3). What could account for this? One reason may be a reluctance to be identified with extreme environmentalist agendas. Another reason may be an inadequate view of the future. If God is going to create a new heaven and a new earth (2 Peter 3:11-13; Revelation 21:1-5), why preserve the present environment? A gospel song puts it this way, “This world is not my home, I’m just a passin’ through.” Such a cavalier attitude toward creation fails to recognize two things. One is God’s ultimate intention in reconciling all things in Christ, which includes God’s creation and God’s creatures (Ephesians 1:9-10; Colossians 1:19-20). A second is God’s continual mandate to care for His creation in light of His restorative goal for the universe (Genesis 1:28; 2:15; Psalm 8:6). Scripture teaches that environmental responsibility is a Christian mandate. Of all people, Christians should be the most responsible when it comes to the environment. Why? Partly because of our call to care for others by addressing, for example, the threat of pollution, the need for sustainable development, the concern for global survival, and so on. But the ultimate reason for environmental responsibility is that Christians understand what it means to have a personal relationship with the Creator—the one who made us, who made the world, and who cares for all His creatures and His creation. If we love and respect our Creator, we will care for His creation. This includes a conscious effort to reduce consumption, respect natural resources, and recycle materials in our homes, workplaces, and churches.

人类与创造力 (Humanity and Creativity)

上帝按着祂的形象创造了我们。祂在我们内心深处安置了建造、栽种、想象与创造的渴望。我们道德操守、性特质（sexuality）以及创造力的展现，都在映照着上帝的形象。尽管人的生命因堕落而受损有瑕，但依然充满着上帝所赋予的种种潜能与契机。

教会有责任也有契机去鼓励信徒充分发挥上帝所赐予的创造性恩赐。我们的堂会常以优秀的讲道人、教师和音乐家著称。但那些艺术家、诗人、木匠和厨师呢？在教会中，还有许多富有创造力的恩赐值得我们去尊崇与展现。手工艺博览会、戏剧与舞蹈表演、服饰展、美食集市以及花艺展示，都是彰显上帝丰富创造力的方式。

不仅如此，上帝造男造女，正是为了彰显祂的样式。这意味男女双方的恩赐都应当在各样服事领域中积极发挥与参与。上帝赐下属灵恩赐并不受性别限制。当弟兄或姊妹在发掘与运用其创造性恩赐上受到阻碍或压制时，上帝的形象便受到了亏损与扭曲。

上帝赋予我们自由，使我们能够以荣耀祂的方式发挥人类的创造力。当我们在上帝的世界中作为尽责的受造者、同心协力在富有创意的和谐中同工时，我们便切实彰显了上帝的形象。

Humanity and Creativity

God created us in His image. He placed within us a desire to build, to plant, to imagine, and to create. Our expressions of morality, sexuality, and creativity all reflect God’s image. Although marred by the fall, human life is still full of God-given possibility and potential.

The church has an opportunity and an obligation to encourage a full expression of God’s creative gifts among its people. Our congregations are noted for excellent preachers, teachers, and musicians. What about artists, poets, carpenters, and chefs? There are many more creative gifts we can celebrate in the church. Arts and craft festivals, drama and dance presentations, fashion shows, food fairs, and flower displays are all ways to reflect God’s creativity.

God also created us as male and female to display His likeness. This includes the active participation of both men’s and women’s gifts in various areas of service. God grants gifts irrespective of gender. God’s image is distorted when men and women are discouraged from developing and using their creative gifts.

God has given us the freedom to express our human creativity in ways which bring Him glory. We reflect God’s image when we work together in creative harmony as responsible creatures in God’s world.

第 04 条：罪与邪恶 (Sin and Evil)

Article 4: Sin and Evil

🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

罪及其后果 (Sin and Its Consequences)

我们坚信，起初人类顺从了诱惑者，堕落陷入罪中。自那时起，人人都违背上帝，选择犯罪，亏缺了上帝的荣耀。结果，罪与邪恶在世界上获得了立足点，破坏了上帝对受造秩序的心意，使人类与上帝疏离，并进而与受造界、彼此以及自身相隔绝。人类的有罪性导致肉体与属灵的死亡。因人人都犯了罪，所有人都面临与上帝永远的分离。

Sin and Its Consequences

We believe that the first humans yielded to the tempter and fell into sin. Since then, all people disobey God and choose to sin, falling short of the glory of God. As a result, sin and evil have gained a hold in the world, disrupting God’s purposes for the created order and alienating humans from God and thus from creation, each other and themselves. Human sinfulness results in physical and spiritual death. Because all have sinned, all face eternal separation from God.

执政与掌权之权势 (Principalities and Powers)

罪是一种奴役人类的权势。仇敌撒但企图统治受造界，利用罪以骄傲和自私来败坏人性。在罪中，人们离弃上帝，将关于上帝的真实变为虚谎，敬拜事奉受造之物，不事奉造物之主。罪使个人和群体陷入邪灵那执政与掌权者的捆绑之中。这些权势也通过政治、经济、社会、乃至宗教体制运作，引诱人远离圣洁、公平与公义。无论在言语、行为、思想还是态度上，全人类都在罪的辖制之下，靠自己绝无法胜过其权势。

Principalities and Powers

Sin is a power that enslaves humanity. Satan, the adversary, seeks to rule creation and uses sin to corrupt human nature with pride and selfishness. In sin, people turn from God, exchanging the truth about God for a lie, worshipping and serving the creature rather than the Creator. Sin opens individuals and groups to the bondage of demonic principalities and powers. These powers also work through political, economic, social, and even religious systems to turn people away from holiness, justice, and righteousness. Whether in word, deed, thought, or attitude, all humans are under the domination of sin and, on their own, are unable to overcome its power.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 3章；6:11-12；诗篇 14:1-3；36:1-4；52:1-7；58:1-5；82篇；以赛亚书 53:6；以西结书 16:49-50；阿摩司书 2:4-8；马可福音 7:20-23；约翰福音 8:34, 44；罗马书 1:21-32；3:9-18, 23；5:12-14, 18-19；6:23；加拉太书 5:19-21；以弗所书 2:1-3；6:12；彼得前书 5:8-9；约翰一书 1:8-10；启示录 12:9。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 3; 6:11-12; Psalm 14:1-3; 36:1-4; 52:1-7; 58:1-5; 82; Isaiah 53:6; Ezekiel 16:49-50; Amos 2:4-8; Mark 7:20-23; John 8:34, 44; Romans 1:21-32; 3:9-18, 23; 5:12-14, 18-19; 6:23; Galatians 5:19-21; Ephesians 2:1-3; 6:12; 1 Peter 5:8-9; 1 John 1:8-10; Revelation 12:9.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

罪与邪恶的真实性贯穿于整部圣经叙事之中，并且在我们自身的实际经验中清晰可见。我们对罪与邪恶的认识，植根并成长于旧约与新约圣经的见证之中。

The reality of sin and evil can be traced throughout the biblical story and is clearly seen in our own experience. Our understanding of sin and evil grows out of both the Old Testament and the New Testament.

罪及其后果（旧约视角）(Sin and its Consequences [Old Testament])

《创世记》关于创造与堕落的记载，为圣经中的罪恶观奠定了根基（创世记 1–3章）。上帝创造了世界，并宣告那是“好的”。然而，当亚当和夏娃顺从诱惑者、选择违背上帝的命令时，罪与邪恶便侵入了这一美好的受造界并使其败坏。因此，从圣经的一开始，罪与邪恶就是需要被解决的问题。这与关于邪恶的其他观点形成了鲜明对比，例如反映了《创世记》时代流行世界观的巴比伦创世神话。在巴比伦的神话解释中，世界是由一位被谋杀的神明身躯创造而成的，因此邪恶被视为本质性地交织在受造界肌理之中的一部分；邪恶与罪恶被看作是生命中必须忍受的既成事实，而非需要被解决的问题。

堕落的叙事同时表明，罪破坏了上帝对人际关系的心意。罪不仅导致上帝与人之间的疏离隔绝，还在人与人之间、两性之间、以及人类与受造界之间制造了隔阂与异化。罪的终极后果乃是死亡（创世记 2:17；罗马书 6:23）。罪所带来的疏离后果，要求一种以“和好”（reconciliation）为核心的解决方案（创世记 3章）。

此外，堕落的叙事也表明，罪的本质在于将自身伪装成令人渴慕的模样（创世记 3:6；哥林多后书 11:13-15）。然而其结果却是，罪损毁了人类里面上帝的形象。人性的每一个层面都遭到了罪的浸染与破坏。没有任何单一层面——无论是理性、性欲还是肉体本身——应当被指认为罪性的首要载体（创世记 3:14-19；罗马书 1:21-32）。既然每一个层面都受到了影响，人类没有任何机能（如理性或良心）能够提供一个未受扭曲的、通往上帝的联络点。罪所带来的全面堕落与败坏（depravity），要求一种必须由上帝主动发起救赎的解决方案。

以色列民在旷野漂流的记载，描述了他们屡次陷入反叛上帝的罪咎之中；他们被称为悖逆、硬着颈项的百姓。这一问题的实质，在于否定耶和华的权威。他们要么选择以公开的不顺服直接对抗上帝的权柄，要么选择以发怨言和抱怨间接削弱上帝的权威（申命记 1:26-27；诗篇 78:8, 17-20, 40-42, 56-57）。这种叛乱激起了耶和华的烈怒，导致对个人以及对全体百姓的定罪与刑罚。解决这种叛乱悖逆问题的途径，一方面在于摩西屡次向上帝代求赦免之恩，另一方面在于百姓重新立志顺服耶和华的权柄（申命记 9:6–10:13）。

今天的人类仍然不断面临着借由否定上帝的终极权威来反叛上帝的诱惑。例如，所谓的现代世界观倾向于将自主的人类理性置于上帝的权威之上；而后现代世界观则倾向于消解任何形式的终极权威，包括上帝统治我们生命的权柄。后现代观点在摆脱一切顺服的“解放”幌子下，诱使人们陷入背叛上帝的罪中。

在旧约中，上帝主动与以色列民立约，以建立与他们的圣约关系。该圣约勾勒出了上帝降临之国度中公平与公义的异象。对上帝的忠诚意味着遵行律法，其中涵盖了宗教、社会与道德层面的规范。在一个层面上，“罪”（sin）或“过犯”（transgression）意味着“偏离目标”（未中靶心），或未能达到律法的客观标准。无论是蓄意的叛逆还是无意的越轨违律，都会招致来自上帝的定罪。罪所招致的定罪后果，需要一种赦免的解决方案，而上帝在旧约中借着献祭制度提供了这赦罪之途。

然而，罪绝非仅仅局限于个人对耶和华或某一特定律法条文的不顺服。贯穿整个历史书和先知书，我们看到以色列作为一个建制民族，其罪行主要体现为拜偶像。拜偶像意味着以色列背弃了与耶和华的圣约关系，离弃上帝，转而接纳异邦诸神以及这些神明所代表的宗教、社会与道德模式。因此，罪涉及对耶和华之约的集体背弃，进而导致对不义体制的卷入与认同。因着他们群体的共同抉择，耶和华便将以色列交在那些列强的权势之下，任由列强征服并蹂躏他们。罪所导致的奴役后果，要求一种以“释放”（liberation）为核心的解决方案。在旧约中，上帝借着兴起士师或拯救者来释放百姓，并向他们施恩赐下更新的立约关系，从而将他们从奴役他们的权势中拯救出来（士师记 2:11-19；但以理书 9章）。

旧约的圣约神学教导，上帝将在历史之中对付邪恶。换言之，上帝将借着兴起一个公义的民族和一位公义的君王来解决邪恶的问题。然而到了新约时代，以色列内部有些人认为，现今的世界已被撒但、死亡和邪恶势力败坏得如此深重，以至于唯有上帝从历史之外施行直接而根本性的超然干预，才能彻底解决罪与邪恶的问题。

Sin and its Consequences (Old Testament)

The Genesis account of creation and the fall lays the foundation for the biblical view of sin and evil (Genesis 1-3). God created the world and pronounced it good. Sin and evil subsequently intruded on and corrupted this good creation when Adam and Eve yielded to the Tempter and chose to disobey God’s command. From the beginning of the Bible, therefore, sin and evil are problems that require a solution. This contrasts with other views of evil, such as the perspective portrayed in the Babylonian creation myth which reflects the prevailing worldview during the time of Genesis. In that explanation, where the world was created from the murdered body of a god, evil was understood to be intrinsic in the fabric of creation. Evil and sin were considered facts of life to be endured, not problems to be solved. The account of the fall also illustrates that sin violates God’s purposes for human relationships. Not only does sin result in alienation between God and people, it also produces alienation between individuals, between the genders, and between people and creation. The ultimate consequence of sin is death (Genesis 2:17; Romans 6:23). Sin’s consequence of alienation requires a solution of reconciliation (Genesis 3).

Furthermore, the account of the fall illustrates that it is the nature of sin to disguise itself as desirable (Genesis 3:6; 2 Corinthians 11:13-15). The result, however, is that sin mars the image of God within humans. Every aspect of human nature is affected by sin. No single aspect—such as reason, sexuality, or the physical body—should be identified as the primary carrier of sinfulness (Genesis 3:14-19; Romans 1:21-32). Since every aspect is affected, no human faculty—such as reason or conscience—provides an undistorted point of contact with God. Sin’s consequence of depravity requires a solution in which God takes the initiative.

The stories of the people of Israel wandering through the wilderness describe the many times they were guilty of rebellion against God; they were called a rebellious and stiff-necked people. The issue was one of denying authority to Yahweh. Either they chose to oppose God’s authority directly by disobedience, or they chose to undermine God’s authority indirectly by grumbling and complaining (Deuteronomy 1:26- 27; Psalm 78: 8, 17-20, 40-42, 56-57). This rebellion provoked the Lord to anger and resulted in condemnation and punishment of individuals and of the people as a whole. The problem of rebellion was addressed by Moses’ repeated intercession to God for gracious forgiveness and by the people’s renewed commitment to submit to the authority of Yahweh (Deuteronomy 9:6-10:13).

Humanity continues to face the temptation to rebel against God by denying God’s ultimate authority. The so-called modern worldview, for example, is apt to place the authority of autonomous human reason above the authority of God. The postmodern worldview tends to undermine any form of ultimate authority, including the authority of God to rule our lives. Under the guise of liberation from all submission, the postmodern view tempts people to the sin of rebellion against God.

In the Old Testament, God initiated a covenant with the people of Israel to establish a relationship with them. This covenant outlined a vision of justice and righteousness for the coming kingdom of God. Allegiance to God meant following the law which included religious, social, and moral prescriptions. At one level, sin or transgression meant missing the mark or failing to live up to the objective standard of the law. Both deliberate rebellion and accidental transgression of the law resulted in condemnation from God. Sin’s consequence of condemnation required a solution of forgiveness that God provided in the Old Testament through the sacrificial system.

Sin, however, was not confined to individual disobedience to Yahweh or to a particular law. Throughout the historical books and the prophets, we see that Israel’s sin as an established nation consisted chiefly of idolatry. Idolatry meant that Israel broke the covenant relationship with Yahweh by turning away from God and by adopting the gods of other nations as well as the religious, social, and moral patterns they represented. Sin therefore involved collective rejection of Yahweh’s covenant, which led to participation in systems of injustice. In response to their corporate choice, Yahweh handed Israel over to the power of these nations, who conquered and abused them. Sin’s consequence of enslavement requires a solution of liberation. In the Old Testament, God saved the people from the powers that enslaved them by raising up judges or saviours to liberate the people and by offering them a renewed relationship with God ( Judges 2:11-19; Daniel 9).

The Old Testament covenant theology taught that God would deal with evil within history. In other words, God would resolve the problem of evil by raising up a righteous nation and a righteous king. By the time of the New Testament, some within Israel considered this present world to be so corrupted by Satan, death, and the forces of evil that only the direct, radical intervention of God from outside history would be able to deal adequately with sin and evil.

执政与掌权之权势（新约视角）(Powers and Principalities [New Testament])

罪是一种权势。使徒保罗明确区分了“诸般具体的罪行”（sins）与“罪本身的权势”（sin）。他将罪描绘为统摄这现今世代的君王，奴役着除耶稣之外的所有受造物和全人类，并将他们置于死亡的权势之下（罗马书 3:9-12；6:6, 12-23；以弗所书 2:1-3；6:11-12）。罪如同一座巨大的磁场，将所有受造物都卷入其强大的引力场之中；人类的任何尝试，哪怕是遵循上帝所启示的宝贵律法，都无法打破这种引力，也无法拯救那些被其紧紧辖制的人（罗马书 3:20；7:5-25；加拉太书 2:16）。

亚当的犯罪行径使罪的权势得以侵入世界，并随之将除耶稣之外的所有人都拉入其统治之中（罗马书 5:12-21）。对于原罪的这种理解，必须与对人类自身责任的强调保持平衡。除耶稣之外，所有人都借着具体的罪恶行径，主动选择屈从于罪的权势；因为世人都犯了罪（罗马书 3:23）。人们往往正是在追逐声望、权力和世俗安全感的过程中，选择背离上帝，容让罪的权势在他们的生命中牢牢立足。

对观福音将邪恶的权势描绘为具象化在污鬼身上的力量，这些污鬼对人的肉体与道德施加着极大的影响力。这群污鬼的首领被称为撒但、魔鬼或别西卜（马太福音 12:24-29）。在旷野中，耶稣以圣经真理质问并抵挡了撒但；在祂的公职事奉中，耶稣直面并赶逐污鬼，并且赐给祂的门徒同样的权柄去行这事。

保罗使用了“执政的与掌权的”（powers and principalities）这类用语。尽管这些权势起初并非必然是邪恶的，但它们却能够沦为上帝的仇敌，从而败坏并奴役人类（哥林多前书 15:24-25；以弗所书 6:12）。群体、国家和体制结构都极易受到鬼魔势力的侵蚀与操控。诸如政府、军队、经济体系、教育或宗教机构、家庭系统，以及由阶级、种族、性别或国籍所决定的结构，都可能煽动人们去行那些他们在个人层面上绝不会主动选择的恶事。此类体制所行使的集体性、持久性力量，其破坏力远远超过支持或顺从这些体制的所有个体力量之总和。

从圣经的开篇直至终局，罪及其后果都得到了详尽的描述与阐发。罪是一个极其严峻的根本问题，它所产生的大恶果为：疏离（alienation）、败坏（depravity）、定罪（condemnation）以及奴役（enslavement）。

尽管上帝在整部旧约中提供了对付罪的途径，但上帝解决罪之问题的终极方案，乃是将基督差入人类历史之中。通过耶稣完全顺服的生命、受死与复活，祂彻底粉碎了罪与死亡的权势。在基督里，上帝兴起了一位大能远胜于罪恶的救主，祂能够将上帝的子民从屈从于罪之主权的奴役中释放出来。上帝主动施恩，借着基督赐下了赦免、和好与复兴。

尽管罪的权势已被击碎，人类却依然经历着罪与邪恶的余波与影响。我们热切期盼上帝借着基督的最终得胜对这个世界施行彻底的终极干预，从而使罪的问题得到根本性、永恒性的彻底解决。

Powers and Principalities (New Testament)

Sin is a power. Paul differentiates between sins and sin. He depicts sin as ruling over this present age, enslaving all creation and all people except Jesus and putting them under the power of death (Romans 3:9-12; 6:6, 12-23; Ephesians 2:1-3; 6:11-12). Sin is like a magnetic field that pulls all creation into its force and no human attempt, not even following the gift of God’s revealed law, can break that force and free those within its grasp (Romans 3:20; 7:5-25; Galatians 2:16).

Adam’s sinful action allowed the power of sin to gain entrance into the world and consequently to pull all people except Jesus into its rule (Romans 5:12-21). This understanding of original sin must be balanced by an emphasis on human responsibility. All people except Jesus choose to submit to the power of sin by behaving sinfully; all have sinned (Romans 3:23). Often it is through the human pursuit of prestige, power, and security that people choose to turn away from God and allow the power of sin to gain a hold in their lives.

The synoptic Gospels depict the power of evil as embodied in unclean spirits which exert great physical and moral influence over people. The chief of these demons is called Satan, the devil, or Beelzebub (Matthew 12:24-29). In the wilderness, Jesus resisted Satan by challenging him with the truth of Scripture. In his ministry, Jesus confronted and cast out unclean spirits and gave his disciples power to do likewise.

Paul uses the language of powers and principalities. Though not all necessarily evil, powers and principalities can be enemies of God and thus they can corrupt and enslave humanity (1 Corinthians 15:24-25; Ephesians 6:12). Groups, nations, and structures are susceptible to demonic forces. Structures such as governments, military forces, economic systems, educational or religious institutions, family systems, and structures determined by class, race, gender, or nationality can incite people to do evil they would not have chosen on their own. Such systems exercise a collective, enduring power far more destructive than the sum of the individuals who support or comply with them.

Sin and its consequences are described and developed from the beginning of the Scripture to the end. Sin is an enormous problem that produces results of alienation, depravity, condemnation, and enslavement.

Although God provided ways of dealing with sin throughout the Old Testament, God’s final solution to the problem of sin was to send Christ into human history. In his obedient life, death, and resurrection, Jesus broke the power of sin and death. In Christ, God raised up a Saviour with power that is stronger than sin and who can liberate God’s people from submission to the lordship of sin. God took the initiative and provided forgiveness, reconciliation, and restoration through Christ.

Even though the power of sin is broken, humanity continues to experience the effects of sin and evil. We look to God’s radical intervention in this world through Christ’s final triumph to bring the problem of sin to its absolute solution.
