ARTICLE 08 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 08 条：基督徒洗礼 (Christian Baptism)

Article 8: Christian Baptism

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

宣告 (Confession)

我们确信，当人领受上帝的救恩恩赐时，当奉父、子、圣灵的名受洗。洗礼是罪得洁净的记号；也是与教会所立的圣约，表明立志靠着圣灵的大能行在基督的道路中。

Confession

We believe that when people receive God’s gift of salvation, they are to be baptized in the name of the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. Baptism is a sign of having been cleansed from sin. It is a covenant with the church to walk in the way of Christ through the power of the Spirit.

意义 (Meaning)

水洗是一个公开的记号，表明一个人已经悔改认罪、领受罪的赦免、与基督同死向罪死去、同复活得着崭新的生命，并领受了圣灵。洗礼是信徒归入基督身体（彰显于地方教会之中）的记号。洗礼也是一项誓约，表明立志照着各人所蒙赐的恩赐来服事基督。

Meaning

Baptism by water is a public sign that a person has repented of sins, received forgiveness of sins, died with Christ to sin, been raised to newness of life, and received the Holy Spirit. Baptism is a sign of the believer’s incorporation into the body of Christ as expressed in the local church. Baptism is also a pledge to serve Christ according to the gifts given to each person.

资格 (Eligibility)

洗礼是为那些公开宣认耶稣基督为主和救主、并立志作为地方教会肢体顺服跟随基督的人设立的。洗礼也是为那些明白其意义、能够向基督和教会负责，并基于对耶稣基督的信仰回应而自愿提出要求的人所预备的。

Eligibility

Baptism is for those who confess Jesus Christ as Lord and Saviour and commit themselves to follow Christ in obedience as members of the local church. Baptism is for those who understand its meaning, are able to be accountable to Christ and the church, and voluntarily request it on the basis of their faith response to Jesus Christ.

实践 (Practice)

我们实行由地方教会施行的水浸礼。地方堂会可以接纳那些在宣信信仰后以其他模式受洗的人成为会友。凡声称在婴儿时期受洗、并希望成为门诺弟兄会堂会会友的人，当在宣认自己的信仰后接受洗礼。

Practice

We practice water baptism by immersion administered by the local church. Local congregations may receive into membership those who have been baptized by another mode on their confession of faith. Persons who claim baptism as infants and wish to become members of a Mennonite Brethren congregation are to receive baptism on their confession of faith.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

马太福音 3:13-17；28:18-20；使徒行传 2:38；罗马书 6:2-6；哥林多前书 12:13；歌罗西书 2:12-13；加拉太书 3:26-27；以弗所书 4:4-6。

📖 Scripture References

Matthew 3:13-17; 28:18-20; Acts 2:38; Romans 6:2-6; 1 Corinthians 12:13; Colossians 2:12-13; Galatians 3:26-27; Ephesians 4:4-6.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

洗礼是新约圣经中一项极其重要的行动。主颁布的大使命尤为凸显了其非同寻常的分量。在大使命中，唯一的命令式核心动词是“使万民作我的门徒”。这一命令由两个具有解释说明作用的分词短语来具体定义：“奉父、子、圣灵的名给他们施洗”以及“凡我所吩咐你们的，都教训他们遵守”（马太福音 28:19-20）。门徒训练（Discipling）不可分割地包含了施洗与教导。

为何必须施洗？因为洗礼象征并宣告了向过去的死亡、罪恶的洁净，以及融入一个新的信仰共同体。人若不从过去的罪咎与偶像捆绑中得着释放，若不在一个新的信仰圣约群体中重新确立生命的中心，就根本谈不上真正作基督的门徒。基督赋予教会的宣教使命，正是借着为人们施洗，并教导他们恪守耶稣的教训，从而塑造他们成为门徒。

我们必须牢记：初代教会是一场“第一代信徒”的属灵运动，每一个加入教会的人都是第一代归信者。在那个时代，归信与受洗在实质上是紧密交织为一体的事件：信心的决志、圣灵的领受、以及融入教会（受洗），这三者彼此紧扣、几乎同时发生。

新约圣经中有十一处核心经文深刻阐释了洗礼的丰富神学意涵（罗马书 6:3；哥林多前书 6:11；10:2；12:13；加拉太书 3:27；以弗所书 4:5；5:26；歌罗西书 2:12；提多书 3:5；希伯来书 10:22；彼得前书 3:21）。这些经文清楚表明，“洗礼”一词在不同的经文脉络中展现出多重且深刻的侧重点。我们必须逐一严谨考察每一处经文，方能拼合出关于洗礼的完整神学全貌。

Baptism is a very important act in the New Testament. The Great Commission highlights its significance. The only command in the commission is to “make disciples of all nations.” The command is defined by two explanatory phrases, “baptizing them” and “teaching them to obey everything I have commanded” (Matthew 28:19-20). Discipling involves baptizing and teaching. Why baptism? Because baptism means death to the past, cleansing from sin, and incorporation into a new community of faith. People cannot be discipled apart from their liberation from the bonds of the past and their recentring in a new community of faith. The missionary assignment given the church by Christ is to disciple people by baptizing them and instructing them to observe the teachings of Jesus.

It is important to remember that the early church was a first generation movement, and everyone entering the church was a first generation believer. Conversion and baptism were essentially one event; the faith decision, receiving of the Holy Spirit, and incorporation into the church (baptism) occurred in very close relation to each other.

The meaning of baptism is interpreted in 11 primary texts in the New Testament (Romans 6:3; 1 Corinthians 6:11; 10:2; 12:13; Galatians 3:27; Ephesians 4:5; 5:26; Colossians 2:12; Titus 3:5; Hebrews 10:22; 1 Peter 3:21). These make it clear that the term “baptism” has different meanings in particular texts. Each text must be examined in order to put together the puzzle of the larger whole.

1. 洗礼作为归入与联合 (Baptism as Incorporation)

罗马书 6:3、加拉太书 3:27、哥林多前书 10:2 以及 12:13 将洗礼明确界定为一种“归入”与“联合”（incorporation）。

罗马书 6:3 与加拉太书 3:27 将洗礼描述为“受洗归入基督”（baptized into Christ）。这一短语的深层含义是指融入以耶稣为元首的信仰共同体，而绝不仅仅是指开启与基督个人的奥秘联合，或建立一种孤立的私人关系。哥林多前书 10:2 中的平行表述进一步印证了罗马书 6:3 中“归入”的社群性意涵：使徒保罗在谈及以色列人过红海时，称他们是“受洗归入摩西”（baptized into Moses）。这显然不是指信徒与摩西个人建立了某种私人密契关系，而是指他们被完全纳入了以摩西为领袖和元首的立约子民群体之中。

哥林多前书 12:13 更进一步指出，洗礼乃是“受洗成了一个身体”（baptized into one body），即归入以基督为元首的教会。洗礼意味着正式成为这群以基督为首、为领袖的子民中的一员。作为“归入”的洗礼，意味着决绝地割断自己的过去，并与耶稣基督的教会合而为一。使徒行传 2:47 记载“主将得救的人天天加给他们”，正是生动描绘了新归信者被真正接纳并融入教会身体的过程。

Baptism as Incorporation

Romans 6:3, Galatians 3:27, 1 Corinthians 10:2 and 12:13 define baptism as incorporation.

Romans 6:3 and Galatians 3:27 describe baptism as “into Christ.” The phrase means incorporation into the community of which Jesus is the head, not only the initiation of a mystical union with Christ, or an individual relationship with him. The meaning of incorporation in Romans 6:3 is underlined by a parallel phrase in 1 Corinthians 10:2. There baptism is spoken of as “into Moses.” This does not refer to baptism into an individual relationship with Moses, but into the people of whom he was the head or leader.

Baptism in 1 Corinthians 12:13 is “into one body,” into the church of which Christ is the head. Baptism means to become part of the people of whom Christ is the head, the leader. Baptism as incorporation means leaving one’s past and becoming one with the church of Jesus Christ. The Acts 2:47 reference to the Lord adding “to their number daily those who were being saved” describes the incorporation of new people into the church.

2. 洗礼作为洁净 (Baptism as Cleansing)

哥林多前书 6:11、以弗所书 5:26、提多书 3:5 以及希伯来书 10:22 将洗礼阐明为一种“洁净”（cleansing）。

在哥林多前书 6:11 中，使徒保罗宣告不道德的生活举止与基督徒的身份全然不相容，因为信徒如今已经“洗净、成圣、称义了”。经文中的“洗净”（washing）历来被普遍理解为指向洗礼。洗礼象征着从罪恶的污秽中彻底得着洁净，这种洁净使得继续沉溺于罪中变得毫无道理、格格不入。

以弗所书 5:26 指出，基督深爱教会的一个明证，就是他“要用水借着道把教会洗净，成为圣洁”，好使教会在他面前圣洁无瑕、毫无玷污皱纹。这里的“用水借着道把教会洗净”，在解经史上通常被阐释为指向洗礼的水洗。

提多书 3:5 将基督所成就的宏大拯救描述为借着“重生的洗和圣灵的更新”。此处的“重生的洗”，同样是用洗礼的具象表述来指向救恩，并且在此处与领受圣灵紧密结合在一起。

在希伯来书 10:22 中，基督徒受到恳切的劝勉，当怀着诚心和充足的信心来到上帝面前：“心中天良的亏欠已经洒去，身体用清水洗净了”。身体用清水洗净，正是清晰指向洗礼的施行。洗礼所表达的洁净，使基督徒得以坦然无惧地进入至圣之上帝的圣洁临在之中。

洗礼代表着脱离罪恶的彻底洁净，这种洁净宣告了昔日生活模式的终结与不合时宜，同时也为信徒坦然进入上帝的同在敞开了大门。洗礼的这一洁净维度，与犹太教的历史传统以及施洗约翰的事工（“传悔改的洗礼，使罪得赦”——马可福音 1:4）有着深厚的承继与连续性。

Baptism as Cleansing

First Corinthians 6:11, Ephesians 5:26, Titus 3:5, and Hebrews 10:22 speak of baptism as cleansing.

Immoral behaviour in 1 Corinthians 6:11 is declared inappropriate for Christians because they have been washed and sanctified. The “washing” is usually understood as a reference to baptism. Baptism signifies a cleansing from sin that makes sinning inappropriate.

One evidence that Christ loved the church in Ephesians 5:26 is that he cleansed “her by the washing with water through the word” in order that the church may be pure, holy, without blemish. The “washing with water” is usually interpreted as baptism.

Titus 3:5 describes the salvation effected by Christ as “the washing of rebirth and renewal by the Holy Spirit.” Again, the washing reference is another way of speaking of baptism, this time linked with the reception of the Holy Spirit.

In Hebrews 10:22, Christians are exhorted to approach the presence of God “having our hearts sprinkled to cleanse us from a guilty conscience and having our bodies washed with pure water.” The washing of the body with pure water is a reference to baptism. Baptism involves cleansing that enables Christians to enter the presence of the holy God.

Baptism represents a cleansing from sin that makes former life patterns inappropriate, and that opens access to the presence of God. This aspect of baptism is in continuity with the practice of baptism in Judaism and by John the Baptist (“a baptism of repentance for the forgiveness of sins”—Mark 1:4).

3. 洗礼作为合一 (Baptism as Unity)

哥林多前书 12:13、加拉太书 3:27 以及以弗所书 4:5 将洗礼与信徒的合一（unity or oneness）紧密联系在一起。

哥林多前书 12:13 强调，洗礼将众人联合“成了一个身体”，这在教会内部产生了一种深刻的社会学维度的合一。无论是犹太人还是希腊人，无论是为奴的还是自主的，都在这同一共同体中融为一体，并且“饮于一位圣灵”。加拉太书 3:27 指出洗礼带来了完全相同的变革果效：它消解并跨越了世人之间那些根深蒂固、造成隔阂的关键界线——不再分犹太人或希腊人、自主的或为奴的、男或女。社会各阶层与不同种族背景的人，都借着受洗归入基督而紧密相联。

以弗所书 4:5 将洗礼与合一并列为同等维度的属灵实质，正如“一个身体、一位圣灵、一个指望、一主、一信、一洗、一上帝”一样。洗礼将背景各异的多元人群融合成一体的圣约子民。洗礼宣告了教会内属天的合一，将原本截然不同、甚至昔日彼此为敌的人，熔铸为同一位基督的身体。

Baptism as Unity

First Corinthians 12:13, Galatians 3:27, and Ephesians 4:5 link baptism and unity or oneness.

Baptism in 1 Corinthians 12:13 incorporates people “into one body” which affects sociological unity in the church. Jew and Greek, slave and free are united into one community, and one Spirit nurtures all. Baptism has the same consequence in Galatians 3:27. It erases the critical distinctions between people, Jew/Greek, slave/free, male/ female. Different people and social classes are united through baptism into Christ.

Baptism means oneness in Ephesians 4:5, as does one body, one Spirit, one hope, one Lord, one faith, one God. Baptism unites diverse people into one people. Baptism signifies unity in the church, creating one body out of very different people, even former enemies.

4. 洗礼作为新生命 (Baptism as New Life)

歌罗西书 2:12 将洗礼与在基督里的崭新生命牢牢结合在一起。在基督里生命的丰盛，是借着与基督一同受非人手所行的割礼（意味着受死归入基督的死）、在洗礼中与基督一同埋葬，并借着那叫基督从死里复活之上帝的信实大能作为，与他一同复活而实现的。

因此，洗礼不仅宣告了与过去的彻底诀别，更紧密连结于新生命、上帝之国的生命、以及在基督里满溢丰盛的生命。

Baptism as New Life

Colossians 2:12 associates baptism with new life in Christ. Fullness of life in Christ is a function of being circumcised with Christ (meaning, died in his death), being buried with Christ in baptism, and being raised through the faithful working of God.

Baptism is associated with new life, the life of the kingdom of God, and fullness of life in Christ.

5. 洗礼作为得救 (Baptism as Salvation)

在所有论及洗礼的经文中，彼得前书 3:21 是公认极具解释难度的一处经文。它将洗礼与得救直接并提，这是新约圣经中唯一一处如此明确地将二者并置的经文。根据这段经文的论述，基督徒洗礼的水，正是对应（或作为预表性实体）当年在大洪水泛滥中拯救挪亚一家八口出死入生的水。

经文通过一个鲜明的“不是……乃是……”（not/but）对比结构，精准限定了洗礼作为得救的真实内涵：“这洗礼本不在乎除掉肉体的污秽，只求在上帝面前有无亏的良心”（或译：“并非除掉身体的肉体污垢，而是向上帝立下无亏良心的誓约”）。“除掉身体的污垢”可以理解为指道德层面的洁净，正如雅各书 1:21 所强调的“脱去一切的污秽和盈余的邪恶”。这一层含义在随后的短语中得到了强有力的深化：“向上帝立下无亏良心的誓约”。“无亏的良心”（good conscience）与“纯洁的心”（pure heart）完全同义，指的是内在真实的灵性纯洁。洗礼绝不是在事后向上帝乞求一颗无亏的良心，而是出于一颗已经蒙血洁净、存着无亏良心的内在实际所发出的行动。向上帝“起誓/立约”（pledge to God）是一个全人全然向上帝负责、向上帝委身的行动。在这段经文中，水洗的仪节是以内在道德与灵性的真实洁净为绝对预设前提的。

那么，洗礼究竟如何“救”人？其神圣运作机制，大概类似于耶稣在福音书中多次对信靠他的人所说的宣告：“你的信救了你。”救恩的实现既要求神圣的主动恩典，也要求人真实由衷的信心回应。“不是……乃是……”的限定短语，界定了“洗礼拯救人”的真谛：洗礼之所以参与到拯救的历程中，是因为它紧随着真实悔改与洁净的历程，这一历程使人获得无亏的良心，而受洗者正是本着这无亏的良心，在洗礼中向上帝立下终生信靠、顺服跟随的神圣誓约。

Baptism as Salvation

One baptism text, 1 Peter 3:21, is notoriously difficult. It links baptism and salvation, the only such explicit association in the New Testament. According to the passage, the water of Christian baptism corresponds to, is a type of, the water which saved Noah and his family.

The meaning of baptism as salvation is clarified by a “not/but” phrase: “not the removal of dirt from the body but the pledge of a good conscience toward God.” The “removal of dirt from the body” can be read as a reference to moral cleansing, as in James 1:21. That meaning is underscored in the next phrase, “the pledge of a good conscience toward God.” “Good conscience” is synonymous with “pure heart”; it refers to genuine inward purity. Baptism is not asking God for “a good conscience” but results from “a good conscience.” The “pledge to God” is an action directed toward God. In this passage, moral cleansing is presupposed by the act of water baptism.

How does baptism save? Probably in a way similar to something Jesus said on several occasions in the Gospels, “your faith has saved you.” Salvation requires both a divine initiative and a human response. The “not/but” phrase qualifies the statement that baptism saves. It saves in that it follows the process of repentance and cleansing that produces a good conscience, which in turn constitutes a pledge on behalf of the baptized person to God.

6. 洗礼的综合神学意义 (The Meaning of Baptism)

归纳而言，洗礼意味着：那些脱离罪恶得蒙洁净、蒙赐全新生命的人，被正式归入并联合成为基督身体的教会。洗礼所蕴含的每一个神学维度，都具有不可估量的重要性。

首先，洗礼是一场极具震撼力的社会学事件（sociological event）。它将新信徒融入教会身体，抹平了世上一切因文化、种族、民族、阶级和性别所造成的隔阂与等级差异。洗礼真正在人间孕育出一种全新的圣约团契，使所有人在十字架和平的根基上享有平等的立足之地。

其次，洗礼也是一场极具震撼力的救赎事件（salvation event）。它包含了一个深切悔改、脱离罪咎与邪恶洁净的过程；并且使人连于一种具有全新质感的生命——上帝之国的生命，在基督里丰盛满溢的生命。

新约圣经中所有关于洗礼的经文一致教导我们：洗礼与“归入联合、合一、洁净以及新生命”这些核心概念密不可分。作为耶稣在大使命中所明确指定的“使人作门徒”的首要途径，洗礼具有无可替代的枢纽地位。洗礼将一系列彻底改变人生命的抉择与行动紧密串联在一起：悔改与洁净、融入教会的立约共同体、以及与教会中的属灵肢体合而为一。

在教会历史上，神学家对洗礼的理解往往走向两个极端：要么采纳圣礼观（sacramental view），认为洗礼作为圣事本身能够客观测度地媒介上帝的恩典（甚至产生事功自效 ex opere operato 的效果）；要么采纳象征观（symbolic view），认为洗礼仅仅是一种单纯象征已经发生的内在属灵现实的外在符号。再洗礼派（Anabaptists）历史性地断然拒绝了圣礼主义的因果生效说，坚定选择了象征意义。然而，一种更为全备而平衡的圣经神学视角，则行走在这两种极端之间：洗礼低于圣礼，却又高于单纯的象征（Baptism is less than sacrament, but more than symbol）。洗礼促成了真实的生命改变，这改变既真实反映了神圣恩典的临在，也切实体现了人类信心的现实回应。洗礼之所以能产生这种真实的属灵果效，是因为它与信心的决志（悔改与信靠）以及领受圣灵紧密交织在一起，绝非脱离信心与圣灵而孤立运作。

门诺弟兄会正是使用“记号”（sign）这一纯正的圣经词汇来精准表达这种全备的涵义。在圣经中，“记号”首先指向上帝的大能作为：例如，上帝带领以色列人出埃及的大能拯救被称作记号（出埃及记 10:1；民数记 14:11）；耶稣所行的神迹在原文中就是记号（约翰福音 2:11；12:37；20:30）；使徒所行的神迹奇事也是记号（使徒行传 4:16；6:8；罗马书 15:19）。其次，“记号”在圣经中也指向人类顺服上帝的回应行动：例如，以色列人将羔羊的血涂在门框和门楣上作为记号（出埃及记 12:13）；吃无酵饼作为记号（出埃及记 13:9）；神将律法佩戴在手上、系在额上作为记号（申命记 6:8）；守安息日作为上帝与属祂子民之间的世世代代的记号（出埃及记 31:13；以西结书 20:20）。

尽管新约圣经并未直接使用名词“记号”来界定洗礼，但从上述圣经脉络来理解洗礼完全切合圣经要旨。洗礼既代表了上帝在耶稣基督里主动施行的拯救大工，也代表了人类对上帝浩大作为的全人由衷回应。它是上帝向信徒守约信实的永恒记号，也是信徒立志在上帝的立约子民当中忠心跟随基督的神圣誓约。正因如此，洗礼在人的真实生命中带来了深刻而切实的改变。

The Meaning of Baptism

Baptism means the incorporation of people who have been cleansed from sin and gifted with new life into the church as one body. Each component meaning of baptism is significant. Baptism is a powerful sociological event. It incorporates believers into the church. It erases all of the cultural, racial, ethnic, class, and gender distinctions that divide people in the world. Baptism effects community and levels the ground in the community.

Baptism is also a powerful salvation event. It involves a process of profound repentance and cleansing from sin and evil. And it is associated with a new quality of life, the life of the kingdom of God, a life of fullness in Christ.

The New Testament texts on baptism teach that baptism is vitally connected to these ideas of incorporation, unity, cleansing, and new life. It is important as the first means identified by Jesus in discipling people. Baptism links a series of actions and decisions that change people: repentance and cleansing, incorporation into the community of the church, oneness with the people in the church.

Historically, baptism has been interpreted either sacramentally, as mediating the grace of God, or symbolically, as symbolizing an internal reality that has occurred. Anabaptists have rejected the sacramental understanding of baptism and opted for a symbolic meaning. However, a more holistic understanding walks between the two options. Baptism is less than sacrament, but more than symbol. It effects real change that reflects both divine grace and human reality. It does this in close association with the faith decision (repentance and trust) and the receipt of the Holy Spirit, and not apart from them.

Mennonite Brethren have used the term “sign” to express this meaning. A “sign” is a biblical term that refers first of all to an act of God (God delivered Israel from Egypt, Exodus 10:1, Numbers 14:11; Jesus performed signs, John 2:11, 12:37, 20:30; the apostles did signs and wonders, Acts 4:16, 6:8, Romans 15:19). Second, a sign also refers to human action (the Israelites put blood on their doorposts as a sign, Exodus 12:13; unleavened bread was a sign, Exodus 13:9; the law was given to Israel as a sign, Deuteronomy 6:8; the Sabbath was a sign, Exodus 31:13, Ezekiel 20:20).

While the New Testament does not describe baptism as a sign, it can be understood in this way. It represents both God’s saving action in Jesus Christ and the response of human beings to God’s action. It is a sign of God’s faithfulness to the covenant with believers, and the commitment of believers to follow God faithfully in the midst of His covenant people. As such, it effects change in the lives of people.

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

在教会礼仪（ordinances）的实践领域中，从教义信念过渡到实际操作往往充满张力与挑战，甚至可能比践行其他信仰宣言条款更为棘手。以下是我们在洗礼领域践行信仰宣言教训时，所面对并必须审慎处理的一些核心牧养议题。

The transition from belief to practice in the ordinances of the church can be difficult, perhaps even more so than with other confessional articles. Following are some of the practical issues we grapple with in practicing the teachings of the confession in the area of baptism.

1. 教导的重要性 (The Importance of Teaching)

针对洗礼的属灵意义提供清晰、严谨且充分的教导，对教会而言永远至关重要，尤其是对那些预备领受洗礼的信徒更是如此。

在当今时代，许多决志归主的人并不理解洗礼的必要性，有些人甚至从未亲身观摩或参与过洗礼仪式。深入细致的圣经教导，能帮助他们明白洗礼绝非仅仅是一种走过场的外在肉体仪式。我们渴望受洗者能够真正体会并完全经历到——这一出于顺服基督的庄严行动所带来的全备属灵益处。因此，将洗礼的每一个神学与属灵层面清晰教导、透彻解明，是极为必要的。

我们绝大多数人对洗礼意义的理解，都是随着年日的成长而逐步深化的。正如人生中其他重大抉择一样，当我们对洗礼的内涵有了更丰富的体悟，洗礼在我们生命中的价值也会与日俱增。我们越能帮助信徒使受洗成为一个成熟、深思熟虑且负责任的信仰抉择，这项教会礼仪对他们个人以及对整个教会所产生的属灵价值就会越发深厚丰盛。

The Importance of Teaching

Clear and adequate teaching on the meaning of baptism is always important for the church and especially for those being baptized.

Many people who become Christians today do not understand the necessity of baptism, and have never been present at a baptism. Careful biblical teaching helps such persons understand that baptism is more than just an outward physical act. We want persons being baptized to appreciate and experience the full benefit of this act of obedience to Christ. Therefore it is important that each facet of baptism be clearly taught and understood. Most of us grow in our understanding of baptism over time. Like other important decisions in life, the value of baptism grows for us as we learn more about its meaning. The more we can help people make this a mature and responsible act, the richer the value will be for them and for the church.

2. 受洗的年龄 (The Age of Baptism)

既然我们确信洗礼是在基督里得着新生命的高度视觉化、公开性的神圣记号，那么在信徒经历归信之后尽早为其施洗，自然是合乎圣经的最正常做法。事实上，在新约圣经中，归信与受洗本就是同一个属灵经验密不可分的两个部分。这是因为初代教会是一场初创的第一代运动，绝大多数加入教会的都是心智成熟的成年归信者。然而，在随后的世代中，随着信徒子女在相当幼小的年龄便表达归信，受洗的年龄界定便成了一个更为棘手复杂的牧养课题。

我们的信仰宣言通过确立三项总括性的把关原则，试图应对这一难题：

  1. 洗礼是为“明白其意义的人”设立的；
  2. 洗礼是为“能够向基督和教会负责”的人设立的；
  3. 洗礼是为“基于对耶稣基督的信仰回应而自愿提出要求”的人设立的。

这三项原则绝不能进行流于表面的简单化解读；相反，应当结合信仰宣言中关于洗礼“意义”的部分来作深入的属灵衡量。例如，受洗候选人应当能够切实表明，他们已经真正理解并在个人生命中经验了阐释救恩的五大核心要义：已经“悔改认罪”、已经“蒙受罪得赦免”、已经“与基督同死向罪死去”、已经“同复活得着崭新的生命”，并且已经“领受了圣灵”。

牧者在现实牧会中常常面临的一种巨大试探，就是妥协退让于家长的期待或社交压力，过早为年幼的孩童施洗。尽管孩童对救恩的单纯理解可能代表了一种真实纯正的初阶属灵经验，但这种经验往往尚不足以构成领受洗礼所必需的成熟理解与契约担当。因此，牧者需要具备极大的牧养敏感度与属灵洞察力：既要小心呵护、避免浇灭年轻信徒内心对主真诚的渴慕，又要竭力防范因给缺乏充分理解的孩童施洗而使神圣的教会礼仪沦于轻浮与庸俗化。

The Age of Baptism

Since we understand baptism to be a highly visual, public sign of new life in Christ, it is natural to baptize persons as soon as possible after the conversion experience. In fact, in the New Testament conversion and baptism are linked as two parts of the same experience. This is because the early church was a first generation church and it was mostly adults who became believers. However, in following generations with the conversion of children, often at a very early age, it became a more difficult matter.

The confessional statement seeks to address this matter by introducing three qualifying statements as general principles. First, “baptism is for those who understand its meaning”; second, it is for those who “are able to be accountable to Christ and the church”; and third, it is for those who “voluntarily request it on the basis of their faith response to Jesus Christ.” These three statements should not be interpreted in a simplistic sense; rather, they should be interpreted in the light of the “Meaning” section of the confession. For example, candidates should be able to demonstrate understanding and personalization of the five statements which explain salvation: having “repented of sins,” having “received forgiveness of sins,” having “died with Christ to sin,” having “been raised to newness of life,” and having “received the Holy Spirit.”

A temptation pastors face is to acquiesce to the pressure to baptize young children. Though their understanding of salvation may represent an authentic initial spiritual experience, it may not represent an adequate understanding for baptism. Therefore, considerable sensitivity and discernment are needed both to avoid quenching the inner aspirations of the young believer, and to avoid trivializing the ordinance by baptizing children who do not have an adequate understanding of the act.

3. 只求受洗却不加入地方教会 (Baptism Without Incorporation into the Local Church)

既然我们坚信洗礼是“信徒归入基督身体（彰显于地方教会之中）的记号”，我们就必然将受洗与加入地方教会的会籍（membership）紧紧捆绑在一起。地方教会正是基督那看不见的身体在历史时空中最具体、最真实的可见彰显。

然而在当今社会，将洗礼与教会会籍割裂开来的文化压力日益剧烈。这种现象深刻反映了当代世俗文化中极端个人主义泛滥的病态趋势。在许多现代人心中，“问责负责”（accountability）与“顺服”（submission）已经成了极难被容忍和接纳的陈旧概念。北美的现代社会完全是由宪法所保障的个人权利至上观念所驱动的，因此对许多初信者而言，“彼此顺服”与“向教会负责”几乎成了如同外星语言般难以理喻的陌生概念。

越来越多的归信者只想受洗，却坚决抗拒融入地方教会——因为地方教会必然要求肢体之间彼此负责、顺服属灵权柄、投身舍己服事，并且在时间与物质财富上尽忠履行基督徒的管家职分。更有一些初信者为自己辩护，宣称自己是“普世教会”的成员，因而不必委身于任何一处具体的“地方教会”。

这一问题的核心根源，在于人们对“归属于具体教会”的必要性与现实意义产生了深刻怀疑。对许多人来说，他们内心深处渴望属于上帝，但对委身于一处特定的地方堂会却退避三舍、犹豫不决。在他们眼中，教会往往被视作一个僵化的世俗体制，被看成是阻碍个人属灵成熟与自由服事的绊脚石，而不是一个坚固、升华基督徒生命与信仰的属灵祝福。

面对这一牧养顽疾，我们可以从以下几个关键方面进行切实的教导与重建：

  • 第一，厘清个人性与个人主义的本质界限：我们绝不能否定基督徒信仰深刻的个人性（personal nature），但我们绝不可将“救恩的个人性”与世俗的“个人主义”（individualism）混为一谈。信仰是个人对上帝的委身，但上帝从来没有呼召信徒作孤立的属灵个体。
  • 第二，彻底教导互为肢体的圣经真理：我们必须持续不断地宣讲并教导圣经的核心真理——基督徒彼此不可或缺，正如身体上的各个器官或组织唯有与其他肢体紧密相连，方能正常发挥功能并维持生命。对于“成为基督身体（教会）的肢体究竟意味着什么”，进行全面、系统且深入的教导，其紧迫性与重要性无论如何强调都不为过。
  • 第三，悉心建立健康的圣约团契：我们必须更加自觉、专注地去打造有温度、有真理的信仰共同体——在这样的共同体中，每一位肢体都受到真诚的尊重与珍视，各人的属灵恩赐得到确认与成全，信徒在爱中彼此造就、互相劝勉，为基督和彼此舍己服事的心志受到持续的激励。
  • 第四，领袖以身作则树立身体生活的榜样：作为教会领袖，我们必须在同工团队彼此相交、以及凡事顺服基督为教会元首的实际生活中，活出肢体生活的真实见证与生命典范。
  • 最后，坚持在地方教会公共身体的崇拜脉络中施行洗礼：洗礼必须在地方教会整体会众的聚集见证中公开举行，使其成为全教会共同经历、同蒙坚固的圣洁盛典。任何私人的、小组化、或在营会/退修会中举行的脱离地方教会会众的孤立洗礼，无论对于受洗者个人还是对于教会整体的建造而言，通常都是极不理想的。

Baptism Without Incorporation into the Local Church

Since we believe that baptism is the “sign of the believer’s incorporation into the body of Christ as expressed in the local church,” we have tied baptism and membership in a local church together. The local church is the expression of Christ’s body on earth.

There is considerable pressure today to separate baptism from church membership. This attitude reflects the increasing tendency toward individualism in our culture. Accountability and submission are not readily accepted concepts. Since our North American societies are driven by constitutional guarantees of personal rights, the call to mutual submission and accountability is strange language to many believers.

Increasingly, converts want baptism but not incorporation into the local church, which demands accountability, submission, service, and stewardship of time and resources. Some converts see themselves as members of the universal church but not the local church.

At the heart of this matter is the question of the relevance of belonging to a church. For many persons, there is a longing to belong to God but a hesitancy to commit to a particular congregation. The church is often seen as an institution which is more of a hindrance to maturity and service than a blessing which enhances Christian faith.

The problem can be addressed in several ways. First, we must never deny the personal nature of the Christian faith. But we also must not confuse the personal nature of salvation with individualism. Second, we must teach the biblical truth that Christians need each other just as one organ or tissue of the body needs the others in order to function and to be sustained. The importance of systematic and thorough teaching of what it means to be part of Christ’s body, the church, cannot be over emphasized. Third, we must work more intentionally at creating communities of faith where each member is held in esteem, where members find an affirmation of giftedness, where Christians are built up and admonished, and where service to Christ and each other is encouraged. Fourth, as leaders, we must model the meaning of life in the body with each other and with Christ as the head of the body. Finally, it is important to conduct baptism in the context of a local church body, and to make the event an experience which the entire body shares. Individual, small group, or camp/retreat baptisms are generally not ideal for the individual or the church.

4. 重洗（再次受洗）问题 (The Rebaptism Question)

我们的信仰宣言明确重申了门诺弟兄会的历史立场：“凡声称在婴儿时期受洗、并希望成为门诺弟兄会堂会会友的人，当在宣认自己的信仰后接受洗礼。”

在牧养实践中，围绕“重洗”往往会引发两个非常具体且敏感的挑战：

挑战一：信徒在生命复兴后质疑早年受洗的有效性

第一种情形涉及这样一类信徒：他们在早年曾对基督作过某种委身并受了洗，但在多年之后经历了翻天覆地的信仰复兴与生命更新。这种强烈的新经历使他们对多年前早期的委身产生严重怀疑。他们可能会认定自己早年的属灵经验（包括当年的洗礼）是虚假或毫无意义的，因而强烈要求再次受洗。

在这种情况面前，牧者的属灵辨识力（pastoral discernment）至关重要。确有少数情况是：该信徒当年确实从未重生，直到如今才第一次真正与基督建立了个人救赎的关系，若是如此，他确实需要接受受洗。然而在更为普遍的情况下，人往往是在经历了人生的风浪、岁月沉淀或重大的属灵突破之后，才真正豁然领悟多年前所作决志的深远内涵。新的人生经历需要与过往的人生经历有机整合，生命才能走向整全与成熟。因此，最合宜的牧养回应，是引导信徒将旧有的恩典历程与全新的复兴更新整合起来，而不是草率否定过去的恩典。已婚的人都能深深体会：当年的婚礼誓约仪式，不可能完全涵盖日后在漫长岁月中逐渐深化的夫妻关系，这种深度的舍己相爱往往是在婚后很久才逐渐体悟出来的。然而，日后深化的感情经历绝不会否定最初的婚约承诺与婚礼，反而正是建立在最初婚约的基石之上。

挑战二：来自婴儿洗礼传统的新加入者

第二个牧养挑战关乎那些来自婴儿洗礼传统、如今希望加入我们堂会的信徒。对于这批人群，往往会引出两个不同层面的实际问题：

其一，原先婴儿洗礼的有效性问题，以及婴儿洗礼与信徒洗礼的关系。

对于当事人的父母而言，当年的婴儿洗礼极大概率是一次充满虔诚、深思熟虑且意义非凡的信仰托付。如果牧者或教会带着傲慢去贬低、贬损婴儿洗礼，或者将这两种洗礼经验进行优劣对立的攀比争辩，是毫无属灵益处且极具破坏性的。更妥善的牧养智慧是：客观承认当年的洗礼所代表的真实意义——那是心存敬虔与善意的父母，在他们所理解的认知限度内，为自己的孩子向上帝寻求最大属灵福祉的真诚举动。在此温和尊重的基调之上，我们便能满有恩典与自由地教导圣经真道：当人成长为心智成熟的个体时，受洗归入基督与教会的真正目的与崇高价值。我们应当努力阐明圣经关于信徒洗礼的启示，同时毫不伤害父母的美好初衷或有益的家庭传统。在举行信徒洗礼的公共仪式上，牧者若能向全堂会众温和说明：这位弟兄/姊妹幼年时曾由敬虔的父母带到上帝面前举行仪式，而今天他/她所作的，则是出于自己成年后的个人成熟宣信与顺服跟随，这往往大有助益。这本身就能转化为一次极佳的圣经真道教导契机，使会众中其他具有类似背景的人同得益处。在许多情况下，极为明智的做法还包括：建议受洗者预先向其家人和亲友坦诚而温和地解释自己作为成年人接受信徒洗礼的心路历程，并诚挚邀请亲友亲临洗礼现场见证这一神圣时刻。

我们还必须体认到：当信徒在少年时期领受坚振礼（confirmation）时若曾作过口头的信仰宣认，并且他们一直将此视为自己对主个人的信心告白时，问题可能会变得更加敏感复杂。换言之，门诺弟兄会的实践是在信徒作出个人宣信“之后”为其施洗；而他们是在作出个人信仰宣认“之前”就已经领受了洗礼——即便他们当年的坚振宣信完全是出于至诚、正直与真实的爱主之心。

其二，未接受信徒洗礼者的事奉与领袖任职资格。

涉及婴儿洗礼者的第二个棘手难题，是关于未重新接受信徒洗礼者在教会中的事奉定位。他们当中许多人是生命高度成熟、在圣经真道上深有根基、并且已经深度投入到我们教会生活中的优秀信徒。然而，由于他们未曾接受信徒洗礼，按照门诺弟兄会的宪章体制，他们无法取得地方堂会的正式会籍。这一困境在现实中常常愈发复杂和混乱，因为不同地方堂会对此采取了截然不同的政策：有些堂会对这类信徒在教会中的事奉完全不设任何限制；有些堂会允许他们参与除核心领导职分之外的各项事奉；还有些堂会则严格禁止他们担任任何形式的正式服事岗位。上述每一种做法，都极易在教会内部引发复杂的牧养张力与人际困惑。

面对处于这种张力中的信徒，我们所能做的主要就是以极大的爱心劝勉大家彼此包容、存着忍耐与体谅的心共同寻求上帝的心意。通常随着时间的推移，事情往往走向以下三种可能的结果：

  1. 他们在真理的引导和圣灵的感动下，跨越心理障碍，最终乐意接受信徒洗礼（再次受洗）；
  2. 他们选择作为参与团契生活的非注册信徒（fellowshipping adherents）继续留在教会，虽然始终不正式加入会籍，但持续在爱中团契；
  3. 他们最终因为会籍政策的限制而渐行渐远，转去加入其他对会籍要求更为开放宽松的宗派教会。

The Rebaptism Question

Our confessional statement affirms that “Persons who claim baptism as infants and wish to become members of a Mennonite Brethren congregation are to receive baptism on their confession of faith.”

Two pastoral issues are relevant in this area. The first involves people who, a long time after making some kind of commitment to Christ, have a life-changing faith renewal experience. The new experience calls into question the earlier commitment. Such people may assume that the earlier experience, including baptism, was meaningless and needs to be repeated. Pastoral discernment here is critical. It may be that the person in fact has entered into a personal relationship with Christ for the first time, and therefore needs to be baptized. On the other hand, it is not uncommon for important experiences or decisions of life to be more fully understood long after the event or in light of new experiences. New experiences need to be integrated with former experiences for life to be whole. Therefore, the most appropriate pastoral response is to help integrate the old and the new rather than to invalidate the old. Married people understand that the marriage ceremony does not reflect the depth of relationship which develops over time, and sometimes much later in life. The later experiences do not invalidate the initial commitment and ceremony, but build on them. The second pastoral issue concerns new persons coming to our congregations from traditions in which they were baptized as infants. Two different issues often emerge for such people. First is the validity of the prior baptism, or the relation of the infant baptism to the believer’s baptism. For the parents of the individuals, the original baptism was most likely a thoughtful and important act. Nothing is accomplished by putting down the infant baptism, or by comparing the two experiences in a competitive way. It is better to acknowledge the earlier baptism for what it was: the result of well-meaning parents seeking what they understood to be best for the child. We are then free to teach the purpose and value of baptism into Christ and the church as mature believers. We should try to clarify our understanding of biblical teaching without reflecting negatively on the good intentions of parents or on a meaningful family act. It may be helpful for the pastor to tell the church as part of the believer’s baptism event that the person was presented for baptism by his or her parents and that what is occurring now is different. This can become a teaching opportunity helpful for others with a similar background. In many cases, it may also be wise to suggest that a person explain in advance to family and friends the decision to be baptized as an adult, and to invite family and friends to be present for the baptism. It is important to recognize that the issue can become further complicated when persons have made a verbal confession of faith at the time of their confirmation, which they may see as their personal statement of believing faith. In other words, we baptize after the personal statement of faith; they have been baptized before the statement of faith, though that statement may well have been given with all sincerity, integrity, and belief. The second issue involving those baptized as infants is the leadership/ service qualifications of non-rebaptized persons. Often these are mature believers, well-instructed in the Christian faith, who have become involved in our church life. Having not taken part in believer’s baptism, however, they are disqualified from membership in the local congregation. This problem is often compounded because in such cases some congregations place no restrictions on service in the church; others allow non-leadership service; others ban all types of formal service in the church. Each of these practices can create pastoral problems.

All we can do is counsel patience and understanding for such persons. Often through time, they either come to accept rebaptism; or they simply stay with the church as fellowshipping adherents and never join the church; or they eventually fade away to another congregation which has a more open view of membership.

🏠 返回 Evan's Thinking 首页

ARTICLE 09 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 09 条：主餐（圣餐） (Lord's Supper)

Article 9: Lord’s Supper

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

意义（Meaning）

教会遵照基督的设立领受主餐。主餐指向基督：祂的身体为我们舍弃（擘开），祂的宝血为我们流出，为要向信徒保证救恩的确据，并立定新约。在主餐中，教会认同基督为救赎世人所献上的生命，并宣告传扬主的死，直等到祂来。主餐彰显了所有信徒在基督里的交通与合一。这是一席记念、庆祝与赞美的筵席，坚固信徒活出真实的门徒生命并忠心事奉。

Meaning

The church observes the Lord’s Supper, as instituted by Christ. The Supper points to Christ, whose body was broken for us and whose blood was shed to assure salvation for believers and to establish the new covenant. In the Supper, the church identifies with the life of Christ given for the redemption of humanity and proclaims the Lord’s death until he comes. The Supper expresses the fellowship and unity of all believers with Christ. It is a supper of remembrance, celebration, and praise which strengthens believers for true discipleship and service.

实践（Practice）

为预备进入主餐的团契相通，所有信徒当自我省察。凡明白主餐意义、在言语和生命中承认耶稣基督为主、对所在会堂（地方教会）彼此顺服负责、并与上帝及他人活在和好正常关系之中的人，皆受邀领受主餐。新约圣经中的常规模式乃是先受洗，后领受主餐。

Practice

In preparation for the fellowship of the Lord’s Supper, all believers examine themselves. All those who understand its meaning, confess Jesus Christ as Lord in word and life, are accountable to their congregation, and are living in right relationship with God and others are invited to participate in the Lord’s Supper. The normal pattern in the New Testament was that baptism preceded participation in the Lord’s Supper.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

马太福音 26:26-30；使徒行传 2:41-42；哥林多前书 10:16-17；11:23-32。

📖 Scripture References

Matthew 26:26-30; Acts 2:41-42; 1 Corinthians 10:16-17; 11:23-32.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

新约圣经在四处经文中记载了主餐的设立：马太福音 26:26-29；马可福音 14:22-25；路加福音 22:14-22；哥林多前书 11:23-26。在这些记述中，主餐的涵义藉着五个主题得以阐释。

The institution of the Lord’s Supper is narrated in four accounts in the New Testament: Matthew 26:26-29; Mark 14:22-25; Luke 22:14-22; 1 Corinthians 11:23-26. The Supper is interpreted by five themes in these accounts.

主要主题（Major Themes）

这四处关于主餐的记述共有三个共通的核心主题：

  1. 第一个主题是指着饼说的“这是我的身体”。
  2. 第二个主题是将基督的宝血与圣约的立定紧密相联。（马太福音与马可福音作“这是我立约的血”；路加福音与哥林多前书 11 章作“这杯是用我血所立的新约”）。
  3. 第三个主题是将主餐视作对未来的期盼与前瞻。（马太福音、马可福音、路加福音作“不再喝……直到那日”；哥林多前书作“传扬主的死，直等到祂来”）。

主餐的核心涵义正是由这三大主题所界定的：

  • 首先，主餐指向耶稣基督在十字架上的牺牲受死。
  • 其次，主餐是一项圣约事件；它在上帝与上帝的子民之间确立了圣约。
  • 第三，主餐指向终末的圆满成就以及弥赛亚的丰盛筵席。

主餐的历史根基构成了理解基督之死与圣约立定的背景。主餐是在犹太逾越节的背景中设立并遵行的，而逾越节正是以色列民藉着出埃及事件获得释放与拯救的伟大历史事件。藉由宝血/死亡成就的拯救，以及上帝与以色列所立的圣约，构成了逾越节涵义的核心要素。在逾越节期间设立的主餐，标志着一场从奴役走向释放的“新出埃及”之揭幕，以及一个“新约”的正式开启。新约的子民，正是那些藉着耶稣基督接受上帝救恩，并进入由耶稣的生与死所建立之圣约共同体的人。

Major Themes

Three themes are common in all four accounts of the Lord’s Supper. One theme is “this is my body” in reference to the bread. A second theme is the association of the blood of Christ and the establishment of a covenant (“This is my blood of the covenant” in Matthew and Mark; “This cup is the new covenant in my blood” in Luke and 1 Corinthians 11). The third theme is the Supper as an anticipation of the future (“not drink . . . until that day” in Matthew, Mark, Luke; “you proclaim the Lord’s death until he comes” in 1 Corinthians). The central meaning of the Lord’s Supper is defined by these three themes.

The Lord’s Supper, first of all, points to the sacrificial death of Jesus Christ on the cross. Second, the Lord’s Supper is a covenant event; it establishes a covenant between God and the people of God. Third, the Supper points to eschatological fulfillment and the messianic banquet.

The historical roots of the Lord’s Supper are the context for understanding the death of Christ and the establishment of a covenant. The Supper was observed as part of the Jewish Passover, the great event of liberation and salvation of Israel through the Exodus. Salvation through blood/death and the establishment of God’s covenant with Israel are the key components of meaning in the Passover. The Lord’s Supper during Passover signals the inauguration of a new exodus from bondage to liberation and the inauguration of a new covenant. The people of the new covenant are those who accept God’s salvation through Jesus Christ and who enter the covenant community established by the life and death of Jesus.

延伸主题（Additional Themes）

除了上述三大核心主题之外，在各段记述中还可见另外两个主题：

第一，在马太福音、马可福音和路加福音中，杯作为基督赎罪之死的象征，被融入到对“圣约”的阐释中。马太福音记载这“血之杯”是“为多人流出来，使罪得赦”，马可福音记载是“为多人流出来的”，路加福音则记载是“为你们流出来的”。耶稣之死作为一项立约事件，向圣约的子民赐下了赦罪之恩。

第二，在路加福音与哥林多前书 11 章中，主餐被描述为一项“记念”的行动。它提醒信徒：基督正是为了他们而活、为他们舍命，藉此立定了圣约。

这些主题展现了过去、现在与未来的连贯一致性：基督在过去的受死，成就了当下的赦罪并立定了新约，而这约必将在未来迎来终极的圆满。

Additional Themes

In addition to the three central themes, two others are found in the various accounts. First, the cup as a symbol of Christ’s atoning death is added to the “covenant” interpretation in Matthew, Mark, and Luke. The “cup of blood” is “poured out for many for forgiveness” in Matthew, the cup “poured out for many” in Mark, the cup “poured out for you” in Luke. Jesus’ death as a covenant event offers forgiveness of sin to the people of the covenant. Second, the Lord’s Supper is described as an event of remembrance in Luke and 1 Corinthians 11. It reminds believers of the life and death of Christ for them to establish the covenant.

These themes represent the continuity of past, present, and future. Christ died in the past in order to forgive and establish a new covenant in the present that is to be fulfilled in the future.

圣约的涵义与要求（Covenantal Implications）

除了四处叙事经文对主餐的阐述外，保罗在哥林多前书 10:14-22 与 11:27-34 中，亦勾勒了对主餐的两项重要阐释。这两项阐释均聚焦于“圣约”这一核心主题。

在哥林多前书 10 章中，保罗正在针对拜偶像的问题展开辩驳。他在阐释中倒转了主餐的顺序，使杯先于饼。牺牲的要素在此处更明确地与圣约主题紧密相联。耶稣的牺牲之死，是为了确立一个圣约——一个合一的圣约、一个身体、一种生命的伙伴相通（partnership of being）。因着基督是一位，基督的身体（即圣约共同体）也必然是一个。主餐在所有领受者之间缔结了深厚亲密的关系。信徒与基督并彼此之间的合一，排除了与偶像背后的诸神立约或相交的一切可能。

在哥林多前书 11:27-34 中，保罗从主餐的角度来阐明教会的本质涵义。基督的牺牲不仅确立了个体与基督之间的关系，同时也建立了上帝子民的圣约共同体。保罗将“这是我的身体”这一短语解释为指向教会。“分辨是主的身体”（29节）意味着看清“归属基督”与“归属教会”之间密不可分的纽带。若未能在教会中践行合一，就表明此人并未真正归属于基督，因而必招致审判。哥林多教会的问题在于，信徒漠视自己作为肢体的合一，从而撕裂了上帝藉着基督所建立的圣约共同体。因此，教会肢体蒙劝勉要医治彼此之间的分裂，“彼此等待”，使主餐能够真正彰显在基督里与在教会中的合一。

Covenantal Implications

In addition to the interpretations of the Lord’s Supper within the four narratives of the Supper, Paul outlines two interpretations of the Supper in 1 Corinthians 10:14-22 and 11:27-34. Both interpretations centre on the covenant theme. In 1 Corinthians 10, Paul is combating the problem of idolatry. He reverses the order of the Supper; the cup precedes the bread in the interpretation. The sacrificial element is connected more explicitly to the covenant theme. The sacrificial death of Jesus is for the establishment of a covenant, a covenant of oneness, a body, a partnership of being. Because Christ is one, the body of Christ or the covenant community is one. The Supper produces an intimate relationship among those who participate. The oneness with Christ and each other precludes a covenant or partnership with the gods behind the idols.

Paul interprets the meaning of the church in terms of the Lord’s Supper in 1 Corinthians 11:27-34. The sacrifice of Christ does not only establish a relationship between the individual and Christ; it also establishes the covenant community of God’s people. Paul interprets the “this is my body” phrase to refer to the church. “Recognizing the body” (v. 29) means to see the connection between belonging to Christ and belonging to the church. Failure to practice oneness within the church shows that one does not belong to Christ, and thus is subject to judgment. The problem in Corinth is that people were fragmenting the covenant community which God established through Christ by disregarding their unity as members of the body. The members of the church are exhorted to heal the divisions among them and “to wait for each other” so that the Supper may represent the oneness in Christ and the church.

遵行主餐（Observance）

主餐兼具圣约与记念的性质，这解释了初期教会为何如此频繁地遵行主餐——他们很可能在家庭教会的每一次聚会以及更大型的聚集时都举行主餐。以色列民在逾越节藉着记念出埃及来更新圣约；基督徒则藉着记念耶稣的生与死来更新这约。

Observance

The Supper as covenant and as remembrance explains why the early church observed the Supper often, probably at every gathering of believers in house churches and in larger gatherings. Israel renewed the covenant by remembering the exodus in Passover. Christians renew the covenant by remembering the life and death of Jesus.

意义（Meaning）

在更广泛的基督教会传统中，对主餐的理解往往分为两派：圣礼论（sacramentally，认为主餐中保/传递上帝的恩典）或纯象征论（symbolically，认为它仅仅象征某种内在的属灵实际）。正如在洗礼上的理解一样，我们将主餐诠释为“低于圣礼，却高于纯粹的象征”（less than sacrament but more than symbol）。门诺弟兄会习惯使用“记号”（sign）一词来探讨主餐的意义。它既代表上帝在基督里施行拯救的行动与新约，也代表信徒在与上帝并弟兄姊妹所立的圣约中再次委身守约。在这一圣事中，信徒与基督重聚相交，也与彼此重新连结。正因如此，主餐常被称为“交通/共融”（communion）。它不仅象征团契共同体，也促成共同体（both represents and effects community）——即恢复并深化与基督及与信徒肢体的相通。

主餐也被称为“感恩祭/圣餐”（eucharist，该词字面意思即为“感恩”），因为它庆祝上帝藉着基督所赐下的救恩与圣约，并热切期盼基督国度在终末的完满实现。

它之所以被称为“主餐”（Lord’s Supper）或“主的筵席/主的桌子”（Lord’s Table），是因为它乃是由基督亲自设立，并且是基督亲自邀请信徒前来赴席。

Meaning

In the larger Christian church, the Lord’s Supper has often been interpreted sacramentally (it mediates the grace of God) or symbolically (it symbolizes an internal reality). As in the case of baptism, we interpret the Lord’s Supper as less than sacrament but more than symbol. Mennonite Brethren have used “sign” to talk about the meaning of the Supper. It represents both God’s saving action and new covenant in Christ, and the recommitment of believers to faithfulness in covenant with God and fellow believers. Believers are reunited with Christ in the event and with each other. That is why the Supper is often called “communion.” It both represents and effects community—renewed fellowship with Christ and with fellow believers. The Supper also is called “eucharist,” which simply means “thanksgiving,” because it celebrates God’s salvation and covenant through Christ and looks forward to the eschatological fulfillment of Christ’s kingdom. The Supper is called “the Lord’s Supper” or “Lord’s Table” because Christ established it and invites believers to participate in the Supper.

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

主餐拥有悠久深远的历史。主餐的根基深深扎根于以色列民族历史的出埃及事件之中。

The Lord’s Supper has a long history. The roots of the Lord’s Supper are found in the exodus event of Israel’s national history.

历史渊源与铺垫（Setting the Stage）

上帝设立逾越节筵席，是为了提醒以色列民：从埃及的奴役中蒙拯救付出了极大的代价。在逾越节之夜被杀并被食用的羊羔，以及涂抹在圣约子民房屋门框上的羔羊之血，构成了永恒的见证，见证上帝奇妙预备的救恩（见出埃及记 12:1-30）。

在耶稣被钉十字架的前夕，门徒与耶稣一同吃逾越节筵席。根据祂的吩咐，逾越节的涵义被赋予了全新的转变，用来象征由“上帝的羔羊”耶稣所献上的牺牲与成就的救恩（马太福音 26:17及以下；路加福音 22:7及以下；马可福音 14:12及以下；约翰福音 13:21及以下）。祂的赎罪之死，使动物献祭的需要永远终结。耶稣应许，这场筵席将一直延续到永恒之中；在祂赎罪受死、复活并升天之后，祂将在祂的国度里与祂的新妇——上帝的新子民——同喝新酒（马太福音 26:29）。

因此，当教会聚在一处领受主餐时，它不仅将整部圣经救赎历史重现于记念之中，更将盼望的目光投向那日——我们在乐园中与救主同饮新酒的日子。

Setting the Stage

God instituted the Passover meal to remind Israel that salvation from bondage in Egypt was at great cost. The lamb which was slain and eaten on Passover night, and whose blood was applied to the door posts of the homes of the covenant people, was to be an eternal witness to the salvation which God would miraculously provide (see Exodus 12:1-30).

The disciples of Jesus, on the night before his execution, ate the Passover meal together with Jesus. On his instruction, the meaning of the Passover was changed to symbolize the sacrifice and salvation provided by Jesus, the Lamb of God (Matthew 26:17ff.; Luke 22:7ff.; Mark 14:12ff.; John 13:21ff.), whose atoning death ended forever the need for animals to be sacrificed. Jesus promised that this meal would continue on into eternity where, after his atoning death, resurrection, and ascension, he would drink new wine with his bride, the new people of God, in his kingdom (Matthew 26:29). So, when the church gathers to partake of the Lord’s Supper, it brings not only all of biblical salvation history into memory, but also projects its thinking forward to the day when we will drink new wine with the Saviour in paradise.

教牧应用的必要性 (The Need for Pastoral Application)

圣经就主餐的意义与践行向我们提供了清晰明确的教导，但对于教会生活中浮现的种种实际操作问题，圣经并未提供巨细靡遗的微观细则。践行主餐的呼召在圣经中有清楚的训示；然而，鉴于我们的文化环境与新约时代相去甚远，我们必须在我们当下的时代与地方教会中，审慎思想其意义与践行方式。以下各段旨在回应我们教会所面临的一些实际议题，以帮助我们忠实于对圣经关于主餐教导的理解。

The Need for a Pastoral Application

The Scriptures provide us with some clear teaching regarding the meaning and practice of the Lord’s Supper, but we do not have finely-tuned details to address the practical questions which arise in our church life. The call to practice the Lord’s Supper is clearly taught in the Scriptures but since our culture is so far removed from the New Testament days, we need to think carefully about its meaning and practice in our times and in our churches. The paragraphs which follow are intended to address some of the practical issues facing our churches as we seek to be true to our understanding of the biblical teaching concerning the Lord’s Supper.

教导主餐意义的重要性（The Importance of Teaching its Meaning）

我们再也不能理所当然地以为，每个参加我们聚会的人对主餐的理解都是相同的。清晰教导我们所理解的圣经关于这顿记念筵席的真理，至关重要。在此需要阐明三个核心要点：

第一，作为再洗礼派（Anabaptists），我们将主餐理解为一项“记号”（sign）事件。 这与其他基督教传统形成鲜明对照：天主教持“变体论”（transubstantiation，主张主餐饼杯经祝圣祷告后，实质上转变成了基督肉身的血肉）；路德宗持“同质论/圣体共在论”（consubstantiation，主张经祝圣祷告后，主耶稣真实的临在存在于饼杯之内、其旁、其间）。

再洗礼派的立场是：主餐是一项“教会礼仪/命令”（ordinance），而非机械传递恩典的“圣礼/圣事”（sacrament）。恩典、赦免、饶恕与新约，并非藉由领受主餐这一仪式本身而被客观测度地赋予（effected）；相反，领受主餐乃是表明恩典已被领受、人们已经进入了新约的关系与圣约共同体中。在主餐中，我们吃喝，是因着“我们是上帝所救赎的子民”这一既定事实。在领受中，我们公开承认基督为我们舍命牺牲，承认我们已被接纳进入上帝的新约子民之中，并庆祝我们在教会中与基督的合一。

第二，主餐本质上是专属于教会的盛事（a church event）。 新约圣经中的常规模式是：洗礼在领受主餐之前。我们历史上的传统做法也始终将受洗作为领受主餐的先决条件。乃是教会在此庆祝自己与基督的合一。我们必须格外谨慎，不可让主餐变质为庆祝基督合一之外的任何事物。例如，我们强烈不赞成在婚礼中藉着领受这象征性的饼杯来象征一男一女在婚姻中的结合。

第三，深入理解主餐的多维时空意涵（context）至关重要。 主餐呼召我们回顾过去，记念在历史中以及在我们身上所成就的大事；它呼召我们直面当下，对照我们所重生的新生命来省察自己；它呼召我们眺望未来，如此反复遵行，直到历史的终局。

主餐涵盖人类经验的三重时态，其包容万象的深邃本质意味着：我们必须谨慎从事，切不可仓促草率，也不可机械式地带领教会走过仪式。在当今这个人们极少停下脚步反思信仰生活的世界中，主餐呼唤我们进入沉静与深思。在这样的氛围中，庆祝乃是藉由沉思默想，从我们的心底涌流向上帝。

The Importance of Teaching its Meaning

We can no longer assume that all who attend our congregations understand the Lord’s Supper in the same way. It is important that we clearly teach what we understand the Bible to say about this commemorative meal. Three issues will need to be addressed.

First, we as Anabaptists understand the Lord’s Supper to be a “sign” event. This is in contrast to other Christian traditions which believe in transubstantiation (the belief that when the elements of the Lord’s Supper are sanctified through prayer they actually become the physical flesh and blood of Christ) or in consubstantiation (the belief that when the elements are sanctified through prayer, the real presence of the Lord Jesus is in and around and among them).

The Anabaptist position is that the Supper is an ordinance and not a sacrament. Grace, pardon, forgiveness, and new covenant are not effected through participation in the Supper, but rather participation in the Supper represents the fact that grace has been accepted and people have entered a new covenant relationship and community. In the Supper, we eat and drink to the reality that we are the redeemed people of God. In participating, we acknowledge the sacrificial death of Christ on our behalf, our incorporation into the new covenant people of God, and celebrate our union with Christ in the church.

Second, the Lord’s Supper is uniquely a church event. The normal pattern in the New Testament is that baptism precedes participation in the Lord’s Supper. Our historic practice also required baptism as a prerequisite to participation in the Lord’s Supper. It is the church which celebrates its union with Christ. Care must be given so that the Lord’s Supper does not become something other than a celebration of our unity with Christ. For example, we strongly discourage using this symbolic meal at weddings to symbolize the union of a woman and a man in marriage.

Third, it is important that we understand the context of the Lord’s Supper. It calls us to the past, to remember that which has happened in history and to us; it calls us to the present, to examine ourselves in the light of the new life to which we have been born again; and it calls us to the future, to do this repeatedly until the end of history.

The nature and all-inclusiveness of the Lord’s Supper, in that it embraces all three tenses of human experience, means that care must be given that we do not rush into it nor lead the church mechanically through its practice. In a world where we seldom stop to reflect on the meaning of our faith-life, the Lord’s Supper calls us to silence and reflection. Celebration in this setting flows from us to God through thoughtfulness and meditation.

主餐的主持与施行（The Administration of the Lord’s Supper）

关于谁可以主持与分发主餐饼杯的问题，人们提出了许多疑问：必须有牧师在场吗？谁可以分发/服事饼杯？一个家庭可以在家中举行合乎圣经的主餐吗？主餐可以在远离母会的青年退修会、营会周末或其他类似聚集上举行吗？年老体弱卧床无法出门的信徒（shut-ins）可以在家中或病房私下领受主餐吗？

信仰宣言仅通过推论涉及了这些问题，指出乃是“教会”遵行主餐。由此看来，凡经教会认可与授权的，便可在实践中施行。

此外，新约圣经并未将主餐的主持权专属指定给圣职人员（clergy）。事实上，新约圣经的指向恰恰相反，它确立了“信徒皆祭司”（priesthood to all believers）。然而与此同时，教会拥有“捆绑与释放”（binds and looses）的权柄，因此教会理当提供指引与规范，以维系主餐的纯正完整与圣洁尊严。新约时代的许多教会都在家庭中聚会，主餐通常在小型群体中规律地举行，但他们绝非群龙无首、没有带领者。小型聚集藉着同领主的筵席而将目光完全聚焦于救恩，具有极其亲密而深具感染力的属灵力量。但必须格外留心，绝不可让主餐流于轻率与庸俗化（trivialized）。

举行主餐的频次同样是一个值得探讨的议题。有些教会传统每周举行，有些每月一次，有些甚至每年才举行一次。许多门诺弟兄会堂会逐渐形成了大约每月举行一次的常规模式。这一模式似乎能有效防范主餐沦为空洞且频繁重复的仪式，同时又提供了足够频密的机会，让信徒抒发对救恩的感恩与喜乐。

在我们的传统中，主餐通常采用切碎的薄饼与葡萄汁作为圣餐元素。尽管我们绝大多数教会已不再使用葡萄酒，但仍有部分堂会选择沿用葡萄酒。在当今这个无论出于自愿节制抑或戒瘾戒酒（clinical abstinence）都十分必要的时代，这一点同样需要审慎考量与评估。偶尔变换饼的形式（例如使用整块擘开的饼或不同质地的饼），或许有助于强化一个观念：饼杯是记号而非实体本身。举行主餐的场景亦可有所不同。偶尔将主餐与爱筵（共同进餐）结合起来，使信徒与上帝的相交以及信徒彼此之间的团契相通并列彰显，不失为一个切实可行的做法。

The Administration of the Lord’s Supper

Many questions are voiced regarding the matter of who may administer the elements of the Lord’s Supper. Must a pastor be present? Who may distribute/serve the elements? May a family have a legitimate Lord’s Supper in the home? Can it be practiced at youth retreats, camp weekends, and other such gatherings away from the home congregations? May shut-ins have the Lord’s Supper privately in homes and hospital rooms?

The confessional statement addresses these matters only by inference, noting that it is “the church” which observes the Lord’s Supper. It then appears that what the church sanctions is approved for practice. Further, the New Testament does not assign the leading of the Lord’s Supper to clergy. In fact, the New Testament goes in the opposite direction by assigning priesthood to all believers. At the same time, the church “binds and looses,” so it is expected to provide guidance and direction in order to preserve the meal’s integrity and holiness. Many of the New Testament churches met in homes where the Lord’s Supper was regularly celebrated in small groupings, but they were not leaderless. There is something very intimate and powerful about a small gathering focusing attention on salvation through participation at the Lord’s Table. Care must be given that the Lord’s Supper not become trivialized.

The matter of frequency is also an issue. Some church traditions celebrate weekly, others monthly, some even annually. Many Mennonite Brethren congregations have settled on a pattern of about once a month. This seems to provide an effective guard against it becoming an empty, oft-repeated ritual, and yet offers adequate frequency of opportunity to express thankfulness and joy in salvation.

In our tradition, small pieces of bread and grape juice are usually used as elements. Though most of our churches no longer serve wine, there are still some congregations who choose to do so. In a day when both voluntary and clinical abstinence may be necessary, this also needs consideration and review. It may be helpful to vary the bread symbol occasionally to help reinforce the idea that the elements are the sign rather than the reality. The settings may also vary. It may be a workable idea to combine the Lord’s Supper with a meal occasionally so that both fellowship with God and with believers are set side by side.

领受主餐的资格与参与（Participation in the Lord’s Supper）

近年来，有些堂会选择邀请尚未受洗的信徒一同领受主餐。这引发了一系列疑问：究竟谁受邀领受主餐？谁可以参与其中？这一议题可从几个层面来探讨：

第一，必须明确确立：这是“主的筵席”。 祂是筵席的召集者，是祂邀请我们前来赴席。参与者乃是那些听从呼召成为上帝子民、成为基督新妇、成为教会的人。这份邀请带有一种非常明确且刻意的“排他性”（exclusiveness）。唯有那些经过深思熟虑、亲自决志信靠耶稣为救主、为自己的罪真实悔改、并蒙受了赦罪之恩与永生盼望的人，才受邀来到主的桌前。

第二，关于预备成熟的年龄（age of readiness）。 年轻的基督徒到何种年龄才算成熟到可以领受主餐？信仰宣言如此表述：“凡明白主餐意义、在言语和生命中承认耶稣基督为主、对所在会堂负责受彼此监督、并与上帝及他人活在合宜正常关系中的人，皆受邀领受主餐。”

孩童的思维通常具象多于抽象。要明白主餐的意义，意味着必须具备将“历史事件”与“象征记号”区分开来的能力。信仰宣言在陈明主餐“指向基督”以及在主餐中“教会认同基督的生命”时，便隐含了这种认知成熟度的要求。

当尚未进入青春期的孩童及其父母急切要求参与主餐时，牧者进行温和的牧养探访、一同重温研读信仰宣言的内容，乃是明智之举。牧者当充分肯定孩子们对基督的爱以及跟从、顺服基督的心愿。这可以成为深入对话的基石，帮助他们开始明白目前尚无法完全参透的真理。在此过程中，温和宽慰孩子、让他们明白适度的等待将会带来更成熟、更喜乐的领受体验，通常是最合宜的牧养之道。

第三，领受主餐前当自我省察的呼召。 如果自我省察的结果显示需要认罪或与人和好挽回关系，最理想的方式是立即予以处理。会堂中的每一位信徒都当活在彼此守望、互相负责的圣约关系中。哥林多前书 11:27-32 暗示：一旦施行了悔改与认罪，就当受到鼓励前来领受主餐；而若拒绝悔改，则是不可领受主餐的正当理由。如果因现实条件所限，与人和好或公开认罪必须在主餐之后才能完成，那么心中立定志向尽快去行，便已是领受主餐的充分预备。

第四，教牧辅导与恩典的平衡。 这一问题还有另一面，极需教牧辅导与智慧带领。从本质上讲，主餐正是为我们这些带有罪性烙印的世人设立的。受邀来到这筵席前的，乃是蒙恩得救的罪人。如果我们过分强调道德上的完美无瑕，而轻忽贬抑了基督救赎恩典的大能，我们就会不由自主地将注意力完全聚焦于自己的“不配”，而无法欢庆我们在上帝面前作为蒙收纳之儿女的崭新身份。

最后，我们因深知上帝鉴察每位信徒的内心与隐秘生活而大得安慰。有些人自觉不配而不敢领受，另有些人虽不配却依旧前来领受。归根结底，这是上帝的筵席，凡在祂桌前所发生的一切，上帝自己是终极的裁判者。

Participation in the Lord’s Supper

In recent years, some congregations have chosen to invite believers who are not baptized to join in the Lord’s Supper. This raises a number of questions. Who is invited to the Lord’s Supper? Who may participate in its practice? This matter may be addressed at several levels.

First, it is imperative that it be the Lord’s table. He is the convener, the one who invites us to participate. The participants are those who have heeded the invitation to become the people of God, the bride, the church. There is a very intentional exclusiveness to this invitation. Only those who have made a deliberate personal choice to believe in Jesus as Saviour, have repented of their sins, and have received forgiveness of sin and eternal life are invited to the Lord’s Table.

The second issue is the age of readiness. When are young Christians old enough to participate in the Lord’s Supper? The confessional statement puts it this way: “All those who have understood its meaning, confess Jesus Christ as Lord in word and life, are accountable to their congregation, and are living in right relationship with God and others are invited to participate in the Lord’s Supper.”

Children usually think more concretely than abstractly. To understand the Lord’s Supper means that one must be able to separate event from symbol. The confessional statement infers this kind of stance when it states that the Supper “points to Christ” and that in the Supper “the church identifies with the life of Christ.”

When preadolescent children and/or their parents press for participation, a gentle shepherding visit to review the confessional material is wise. It is well to affirm children for their love for Christ and their desire to follow and obey him. This can become the foundation for a discussion which will help them to begin to understand what they do not yet know in fullness. It is usually wise to reassure children that waiting will result in a more mature and joyful participation in the event.

Another issue is the call to self-examination before participation in the Lord’s Supper. If this self-examination results in the need for confession or restoration, it is preferred that this be dealt with immediately. Each believer in the congregation is to live in mutual accountability. First Corinthians 11:2732 infers that when repentance and confession have been made, participation is encouraged. Resistance to repentance is grounds for non-participation. If confession will have to be accomplished following the Lord’s Supper, a heart commitment to do so is adequate preparation for participation.

There is another side to this question that needs pastoral counsel and leadership. The Lord’s Supper by definition is for us as humans, women and men who are marked by sinfulness. It is sinners saved by grace who are invited to this Supper. If we overemphasize the ideal of moral perfection and underemphasize the redeeming grace of Christ, we will tend to focus attention on our unworthiness rather than to celebrate our new status as adopted children of God.

Finally, we are comforted by the knowledge that God sees the heart and inner life of each believer. Some will feel unworthy and will not participate while others who are unworthy will participate anyway. In the end, it is God’s table, and God is the final arbiter of all that happens at His table.
