ARTICLE 15 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 15 条：管家职分 (Stewardship)

Article 15: Stewardship

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

上帝创造的使命（God’s Creation Mandate）

我们相信，宇宙及其中的万物都属于创造主上帝。上帝已将看顾大地的责任托付给全人类，人人都有责任妥善管理其资源。忠心的管家职分运用大地的丰饶来满足人类的需要，但坚决抵制对大地及其居民的不义剥削。上帝一切的恩赐都当存感恩之心领受，并带着责任感加以使用。

God’s Creation Mandate

We believe the universe and everything in it belong to God the Creator. God has entrusted the care of the earth to all people, who are responsible for managing its resources. Good stewardship uses the earth’s abundance to meet human need, but resists the unjust exploitation of the earth and its peoples. All God’s gifts are to be received with thanksgiving and used responsibly.

负责任的生活（Responsible Living）

承认耶稣为主，会带来价值观的彻底转化。耶稣警告说，我们不能既事奉上帝，又事奉金钱。终日汲汲于金钱与产业、沉溺于放纵享受的生活、以及一心为私利积蓄财富，皆与圣经的教导背道而驰。

Responsible Living

To confess Jesus as Lord transforms values. Jesus warns that we cannot serve both God and wealth. Preoccupation with money and possessions, selfindulgent living, and eagerness to accumulate wealth for personal advantage are not in keeping with the teaching of Scripture.

慷慨奉献（Generous Giving）

圣经教导我们，作为对上帝恩惠的感恩回应，应当透过教会实行乐意、牺牲且按比例的奉献。基督徒不把自己的任何财产据为私有，而是以荣耀上帝的慷慨方式，管理自己的一切资源，包括金钱、时间、恩赐才干与影响力。他们不蔑视穷人，而是在教会内部实行互助，并与有需要的众人分享自身所有。上帝的子民立志持守简朴与知足的生活方式。

Generous Giving

The Bible teaches cheerful, sacrificial, and proportional giving through the church in grateful response to God’s goodness. Christians do not claim any of their possessions as their own, but manage all their resources, including money, time, abilities, and influence, in generous ways that give glory to God. They do not despise the poor but practice mutual aid within the church and share what they have with others in need. God’s people seek to embrace a lifestyle of simplicity and contentment.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 1:28；利未记 25章；申命记 15:7-11；诗篇 24:1；115:16；箴言 14:31；阿摩司书 6:4-7；玛拉基书 3:6-10；马太福音 6:19-34；25:14-30；路加福音 6:38；12:13-21；使徒行传 2:42-47；4:32-37；哥林多前书 4:7；16:2；哥林多后书 8–9章；加拉太书 6:7；以弗所书 4:28；提摩太前书 6:6-10, 17-19；雅各书 2:1-7, 15-16；5:1-6；约翰一书 3:16-18；犹大书 11。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 1:28; Leviticus 25; Deuteronomy 15:7-11; Psalm 24:1; 115:16; Proverbs 14:31; Amos 6:4-7; Malachi 3:6-10; Matthew 6:19-34; 25:14-30; Luke 6:38; 12:13-21; Acts 2:42-47; 4:32-37; 1 Corinthians 4:7; 16:2; 2 Corinthians 8-9; Galatians 6:7; Ephesians 4:28; 1 Timothy 6:6-10, 17-19; James 2:1-7, 15-16; 5:1-6; 1 John 3:16-18; Jude 11.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

“管家”（Stewards）是指受他人托付、通过看顾与管理代为掌管贵重事物，并必须向所有者交账的人。从最广泛的意义上说，全人类对大地都负有管家责任。再者，教会区别于所有其他人类机构之处，在于她受托保管福音的宝藏（林前 4:1）。从最具体的个人层面而言，每个人作为上帝各样恩赐的管家，都必须向上帝交账。

Stewards are persons who hold something of value in trust for another through care and management, and who give an account to an owner. In the widest sense, humanity has a stewardship responsibility for Earth. Moreover, the church is distinct from all other human agencies in that it holds in trust the treasure of the gospel (1 Corinthians 4:1). In the narrowest sense, each individual is accountable as a steward of God’s gifts.

大地的管家职分（Stewardship of the Earth）

上帝对受造物的关怀（Divine Concern for Creation）

基督徒显然包含在“创造使命”（creation mandate）之中，该使命呼召人类“治理这地”，正如伊甸园中的亚当所显明的那样——他受命耕种并看守伊甸园（创 1:28-31；2:15）。上帝是无可争辩的万物创造者与拥有者（创 1:1；14:19；诗 24:1），祂怜恤大地及其走兽飞鸟，顾惜祂所造的一切（拿 4:11；诗 104:10-30；36:6；珥 2:22）。先知们展望了一个人与动物和谐共处的时代（赛 11:1-10；参 65:25；35:1-7, 9；何 2:18）。上帝的救赎计划最终也包括新天新地的创造（赛 65:17；罗 8:20-22）。

人类对受造物的责任（Human Responsibility for Creation）

作为大地的管家，意味着要妥善管理水资源与森林等自然资源，并以上帝所流露的同等怜悯去照料动植物。圣经律法中的例证证实了这一点：鸟窝不可连母带雏一并毁坏取去（申 22:6-7）；牲畜当受顾惜照料（申 25:4；箴 12:10）；用于军事战争的树木砍伐受到严格限制（申 20:19）；出产受造物所需粮食的土地不可遭竭泽而渔的剥削——这一教导在第七年安息年及禧年让土地休耕的律例中阐述得尤为明晰（利 25章）。

道德与环境（Morality and the Environment）

一个群体的道德生活与自然界的状况之间存在着紧密的纽带。人类的强暴败坏导致了挪亚时代的大洪水（创 6–9章）。大地的浩劫乃是人类罪恶的直接恶果（何 4:1-3）。耶利米描绘了一幅宛如“核冬天”般的凄凉景象，正如其上下文所揭示的，这结局完全归因于人类的罪孽过犯（耶 4:23-26）。在极大程度上，正是出于人类的贪婪，森林滥伐导致土地荒漠化，农药化肥过度使用导致土壤退化，水质污染导致鱼类鸟类死亡。由此可以得出正面积极的结论：人类居民若能活出无私、怜悯与负责任的生活，大地就必得蒙造就与医治。

Stewardship of the Earth

Divine Concern for Creation: Christians are obviously included in the “creation mandate” which calls for humans to “have dominion over the earth” as illustrated in the Garden of Eden by Adam, who was mandated to till the ground and guard it (Genesis 1:28-31; 2:15). God, the undisputed maker and possessor of all (Genesis 1:1; 14:19; Psalm 24:1) has compassion for the land and its creatures and cares for all His creation ( Jonah 4:11; Psalm 104:10-30; 36:6; Joel 2:22). The prophets envision a time of harmony between humans and animals (Isaiah 11:1-10; cf. 65:25; 35:1-7, 9; Hosea 2:18). God’s salvation plan also includes the eventual creation of a new earth (Isaiah 65:17; Romans 8:20-22). Human Responsibility for Creation: To be a steward of the earth means to manage well such natural resources as water and forests, and to tend plants and animals with the same compassion God shows. Examples from biblical law substantiate this. Bird nests are not to be destroyed (Deuteronomy 22:6-7). Animals are to be cared for (Deuteronomy 25:4; Proverbs 12:10). Limits are put on the use of trees for war (Deuteronomy 20:19). The ground, which produces what creatures need, is not to be exploited—a teaching made clear in the provision that the land be left fallow every seventh year and also during the year of Jubilee (Leviticus 25).

Morality and the Environment: There is a strong link between the moral life of a people and the condition of the natural world. Humanity’s violence brought the Noahic flood (Genesis 6-9). Havoc on the earth will be the consequence of human evil (Hosea 4:1-3). Jeremiah paints a scene not unlike a nuclear winter, an outcome, as the larger context makes clear, due to human wrongdoing ( Jeremiah 4:2326). It is in large part because of human greed that land becomes a desert due to deforestation, soil deteriorates with overuse of chemicals, fish and birds die from water pollution. The conclusion follows, positively stated, that the earth will be helped by unselfish, compassionate, and responsible living by its human inhabitants.

负责任的生活（Responsible Living）

视角的重塑（Realigned Perspectives）

负责任的生活，首先包含对生命、时间、才能、机遇与物质财富均来自上帝的完全承认。上帝是万有的源头与合法拥有者（诗 24:1）。祂是人类生命的始源（创 2:7, 21-23），并赐下生育繁衍的恩赐使人类得以代代相传（得 4:13；撒上 1:27）。上帝赐予得货财的力量（申 8:18；代上 29:12），也赐予各样才能，如工匠技艺（出 36:1 中的比撒列与亚荷利亚伯）与智慧洞察力（王上 3:10-12 中的所罗门）。在圣经中，土地被反复指明为上帝赐给以色列的“恩礼”或“赏赐”；单单在申命记中，此类表述就多达三十处（如 5:31；9:6；26:9）。亚伯拉罕、以撒和约伯等先祖，其丰盛的物质财富皆仰赖上帝所赐（伯 42:10）。上帝乃是一切美善恩赐与全备赏赐的赐予者（雅 1:17）。

上帝慷慨的恩赐呼召人类向赐恩者作出感恩的回应。信徒受到告诫，不可心高气傲（提前 6:17-19）。功成名就与富足者必须抵制自夸的诱惑（申 8:11-17）。相反，当持守感恩的心态。此外，既然上帝的恩赐具有“信托”的性质，人类就必须为管理和使用这些恩赐的方式负责交账。

管理上帝的恩赐（Managing God’s Gifts）

上帝一切的恩赐——恩赐才能、财富、时间、生态环境——都当善加管理。生命的恩赐及其各样美福自当尽情享受（提前 6:17）。旧约和新约圣经都没有因为富有本身而谴责富人，但两约都对财富的使用给予了详尽的指导，并警告富足所带来的种种诱惑。

耶稣定罪自私自利，但并不排斥一切合理的自身益处。财富应当被用作一项投资，“为自己积攒财宝在天上”（太 6:20，原作者强调）。一个人不能既事奉上帝，又事奉金钱（太 6:24）。基督徒既然立志先求上帝的国和祂的义，就会按照这一优先次序来安排自己的财务（太 6:19-21, 33）。正如有人所言，每一项消费支出的决定，本质上都是一项属灵的决定。

经文反复劝诫人们要顾念穷人。上帝对穷苦之人表达了特别的眷顾（preferential outlook）。先知们对剥削压迫穷人者发出了严厉的谴责（赛 3:15；摩 2:6-7）。穷人不当被回避蔑视，而当得到救助（申 15:7-11；箴 17:5；19:17；参 弗 4:28）。早期教会悉心照料穷人，并为他们募集捐项（徒 6:1-7）。信徒受到严正警戒，决不可忽视穷乏之人（雅 2:14-17；约一 3:16-18）。

关于财富的劝诫（Counsel on Wealth）

先知阿摩司严词抨击富豪阶层，指出他们嗜好在家具、佳肴、宴乐与香膏脂粉上挥金如土。当人们对“约瑟的苦难”漠不关心——换言之，对他人身体的贫困或属灵的荒凉视若无睹时，奢华的生活便成了一种罪恶（摩 6:4-7）。那股“挥霍挥金的冲动”（urge to splurge）应当受到克制。基督徒所显明的标志，不应是“炫耀性消费”（conspicuous consumption），而当是“怜悯的关怀”（compassionate concern）。

物质主义（拜金主义）是指急切渴望占有并把持财产、一心为个人私利而钻营金钱的心态。在耶稣看来，物质主义既是愚蠢的，也是有罪的（路 12:13-24）。耶稣的比喻中有三分之一涉及经济与钱财；在这些比喻中，耶稣强调交账的责任，并警戒那种唯独利用才能和金钱来抬高自我、排斥他人的自私心态。保罗警戒人不可贪爱钱财（提前 6:6-10）。圣经竭力提倡知足常乐与简朴的生活（腓 4:11；提前 6:8）。

贪婪是十诫所明令禁止的（出 20:17），圣经对巧取豪夺与贪心发出了严厉的警告（路 12:15；犹 11）。以色列人在加低斯·巴尼亚时，妄图凭己意顽固争夺应许之地，结果遭到弃绝（民 14章）。先知以利亚严厉斥责亚哈王强占拿伯葡萄园的罪行（王上 21章）。基哈西因心生贪念……结果失去了一切！（王下 5:20-27）。巧取豪夺与贪心如出一辙、形影相随。在亚拿尼亚和撒非喇的故事中（徒 5章），贪心并伴随着诡诈欺瞒，其恶果之严重性显露无遗。

Responsible Living

Realigned Perspectives: Responsible living includes a full acknowledgment that life, time, abilities, opportunities, and material goods are from God. God is the source and rightful owner of all (Psalm 24:1). He is the origin of human life (Genesis 2:7; 21-23), and gives the gift of procreation whereby human beings come to life (Ruth 4:13; 1 Samuel 1:27). God gives the power to get wealth (Deuteronomy 8:18; 1 Chronicles 29:12), as well as abilities such as craftsmanship (Bezalel and Oholiab in Exodus 36:1) and insight (Solomon in 1 Kings 3:10-12). The land is repeatedly designated as “gift” or “given by God” to Israel. Deuteronomy alone has 30 such statements (e.g. 5:31; 9:6; 26:9). It was God to whom patriarchs such as Abraham and Isaac and Job owed their material prosperity ( Job 42:10). God is the giver of every good and perfect gift ( James 1:17).

God’s generous gift-giving calls for the human response of thanksgiving to the Giver. Believers are warned against an attitude of arrogance (1 Timothy 6:17-19). The successful and well-to-do are to resist the temptation to boast (Deuteronomy 8:11-17). The attitude instead is to be one of gratitude. Moreover, since God’s gifts are in the nature of “trusts,” humans are accountable for the way these gifts are managed and used.

Managing God’s Gifts: All God’s gifts—abilities, wealth, time, the environment—are to be managed well. The gifts of life and its pleasures are to be enjoyed (1 Timothy 6:17). Neither Old nor New Testament censures the rich for being rich, but both give ample instructions on the use of wealth and warn against temptations that come with wealth.

Jesus condemns selfishness but not all self-interest. Wealth is to be used as an investment to “store up for yourselves treasures in heaven” (Matthew 6:20, emphasis added). One cannot serve God and wealth (Matthew 6:24). Intent on seeking first the kingdom of God, Christians will order their finances according to that priority (Matthew 6:19-21, 33). Every spending decision, it has been said, is a spiritual decision.

A repeated exhortation is for people to be mindful of the poor. God expresses a preferential outlook toward the poor. The prophets have sharp words for those who exploit the poor (Isaiah 3:15; Amos 2:6-7). The poor are not to be shunned but helped (Deuteronomy 15:7-11; Proverbs 17:5; 19:17; cf. Ephesians 4:28). The early church cared for the poor and took offerings for them (Acts 6:1-7). Believers are admonished not to neglect the poor ( James 2:14-17; 1 John 3:16-18).

Counsel on Wealth: Amos strikes hard at the rich, noting their penchant for lavish expenditures for furniture, food, entertainment, and cosmetics. Luxurious living becomes wrong where there is an unconcern for the “ruin of Joseph”; in other words, inattention to the physical or spiritual impoverishment of others (Amos 6:4-7). The “urge to splurge” should be curbed. Instead of “conspicuous consumption,” Christians should be known by “compassionate concern.” Materialism is the eagerness to have and to hold possessions and the preoccupation with money for self-advantage. Materialism, according to Jesus, is both foolish and sinful (Luke 12:13-24). A third of Jesus’ parables deal with economics; in these, Jesus insists on accountability and warns against selfishness which utilizes abilities and money only for enhancement of the self to the exclusion of others. Paul warns against the love of money (1 Timothy 6:6-10). Contentment is urged, as is simplicity of life (Philippians 4:11; 1 Timothy 6:8).

Covetousness is prohibited (Exodus 20:17) and strong warnings are given against grasping and greed (Luke 12:15; Jude 11). Israel at Kadesh Barnea, when trying stubbornly to grasp the promised land, was denied it (Numbers 14). Elijah the prophet severely reprimanded King Ahab for wanting to seize Naboth’s vineyard (1 Kings 21). Gehazi coveted...and lost! (2 Kings 5:20-27). Grasping is a twin to coveting. The seriousness of coveting, accompanied in this instance by deceit, is demonstrated in the story of Ananias and Sapphira (Acts 5).

慷慨奉献（Generous Giving）

奉献的动机（Motivations）

有关慷慨财务奉献的教导集中在哥林多后书8至9章，保罗在此列举了慷慨施予的诸多理由：

  1. 第一，慷慨效法了耶稣舍己奉献的榜样（8:9）。
  2. 第二，我们的奉献捐助造福了他人的生命，上帝的名因此得着荣耀（9:12-14）。
  3. 第三，慷慨给奉献者本人带来巨大的属灵益处与赏赐（9:6, 14；另参 路 6:38；加 6:7）。
  4. 第四，施予效法了那些展现属灵成熟之人的榜样（8:1-5）。
  5. 第五，奉献有助于实现均平与平等的目的（8:14）。
  6. 第六，慷慨是对上帝感恩的切实回应（9:15）。

若是紧握拳头、一毛不拔，或是深陷于外物（汽车、地产、金钱、股票、豪宅、昂贵的度假）之中，以致无法拨出资金支持上帝的国度事工，这乃是自私有罪的，且与天国子民的生活背道而驰。正如一句名言所说：“我们的奉献，就是在为上帝的掌权投票。”

奉献的准则（Guidelines）

保罗敦促信徒实行定期、系统且按比例的财务奉献（林前 16:1；参 申 16:17）。十一奉献代表了一项基本基准（太 23:23；民 18:26；玛 3:10），但基督徒的奉献当超越十一的界限（林前 16:1-2；路 21:2-4）。正如有人所言：“十一奉献不是上帝筹集资金的计划，而是祂培养祂儿女成长的计划。”慷慨是解救贪婪之罪的良药。

基督徒的奉献需要行政上的推动与周密的规划（林后 8:16–9:5）。面对许多需要基督徒慷慨支持的崇高事工，信徒在财务抉择上当依靠祷告与圣灵的引导。将“仓库十一奉献”的原则诠释为在经济上支持地方教会及所属宗派的事工机构，是合情合理且合乎圣经的（玛 3:6-10）。对基督徒而言，奉献不应成为重担。信徒完全有充足的理由甘心乐意、欢欢喜喜、甚至满怀热情地施予。

Generous Giving

Motivations: Teaching about generous financial giving is concentrated in 2 Corinthians 8-9 where Paul lists reasons for generosity. First, generosity follows the selfgiving model of Jesus (8:9). Second, our contributions bring good to the lives of others, through which God is glorified (9:12-14). Third, generosity brings large benefits to the contributors (9:6, 14; also Luke 6:38; Galatians 6:7). Fourth, giving follows the example of others who model spiritual maturity (8:1-5). Fifth, giving helps achieve the goal of equality (8:14). Sixth, generosity is the tangible response of gratitude to God (9:15). It is sinfully self-serving and contrary to kingdom living to be tight-fisted or so attached to things (cars, properties, money, stocks, homes, expensive vacations) that finances are unavailable for God’s kingdom ministry. It has been well said: “We are voting for God’s rule when we give.”

Guidelines: Paul urged regular, systematic, and proportional financial contributions (1 Corinthians 16:1; cf. Deuteronomy 16:17). The tithe represents a standard (Matthew 23:23; Numbers 18:26; Malachi 3:10), but Christian giving goes beyond the tithe (1 Corinthians 16:1-2; Luke 21:2-4). In the words of another: “Tithing is not God’s plan for raising money, but his plan for raising his children.” Generosity is the antidote to the sin of covetousness. Christian giving calls for administrative prompting and planning (2 Corinthians 8:16-9:5). With many noble causes competing for a Christian’s generosity, the believer will rely on prayer and the Spirit’s guidance in money-matter decisions. The principle of storehouse tithing, interpreted as supporting financially the agencies of the local church and the denomination, is sensible and sound (Malachi 3:6-10). For the Christian, giving should not be burdensome. The believer has good reason to give cheerfully, with joy, even exuberance.

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

基督徒的管家职分，是指对上帝所造的秩序进行忠心、智慧且负责任的管理。本条的核心焦点在于对大地受造资源以及个人资源的管家职分。

Christian stewardship is the faithful, wise, and responsible management of God’s created order. The focus of this article is on the stewardship of the earth’s resources and of personal resources.

大地的管家职分（Stewardship of the Earth）

上帝已赋予人类作为受造界管家的责任。良好的管理既包括保护大地免遭破坏性剥削，也包括为了人类的福祉合理开发资源。教会可以提供有益的资讯与教导，使会众能够分辨如何最有效地发挥大地的潜能，同时又不破坏生态环境。

可以通过各项活动来提升信徒对环境问题的关注意识。例如，通过专项活动或社区行动推广垃圾分类与循环回收（recycling），兼具实用价值与象征意义。留心节约用水用电，也能推动环保实践的落实。使用回收再造材料，避免使用破坏环境的制品，既能提高环保意识，又有助于保护大地。地方教会定期鼓励此类活动，正与“治理全地”的创造使命相契合。

尽管门诺弟兄会信徒的生活日益城市化，但我们仍有相当多的人从事农业生产。农户常常面临两难抉择：一方面需要使用化学农药与化肥以提高产量，另一方面又希望避免使用化学制剂以保护生态环境。严肃对待大地管家职分的教会，决不应回避这一问题，而是应当建立对话论坛，让这些棘手问题能够在信仰群体中得以坦诚探讨。

Stewardship of the Earth

God has given humans the responsibility to be stewards of creation. Good management involves both protecting the earth from destructive exploitation and developing resources for the good of humankind. The church can provide helpful information and teaching that will enable its members to discern how best to manage the potential of the earth without damaging the environment.

Raising consciousness toward environmental concerns can be achieved through various activities. Encouraging recycling through special drives or community activism, for example, has both practical and symbolic value. Taking care to conserve water and energy also furthers participation in environmentally friendly practices. Using recycled materials and avoiding use of products that damage the environment can both raise awareness and help protect the earth. Periodic encouragement of such activities by the congregation is consistent with the creation mandate to rule over the earth. Although Mennonite Brethren have become increasingly urban, we continue to have a large number of our people engaged in agriculture. Farmers are often faced with the conflict between using chemicals to increase productivity and avoiding the use of chemicals to protect the environment. Instead of avoiding the issue, the church that takes seriously its role in stewardship of the earth will create forums in which these difficult questions can be addressed.

慷慨生活的策略（Strategies for Generous Living）

耶稣关于金钱的教导表明：金钱是生活的手段，而非生活本身的目的。深入查考耶稣的生平与信息，能为我们开启一条为主而活的丰盛人生之路。其核心焦点必须如同彼得前书 3:15 所说：“专心致志地完全归顺奉献于基督”（Phillips 译本意译；参和合本：“只要心里尊主基督为圣”）。

强大的世俗力量时刻在试图分散我们对基督的专注。如果我们意识不到问题的存在，就绝不可能采取步骤去解决问题。圣经指出了我们易于贪婪的本性，以及贪婪最终所导致的灵性贫困。个人的自我检视（盘点反思）是一项健康且必需的操练。作为这种检视的一部分，我们可以收集一些日常数据。我们可以记录每周的时间日志，涵盖以下各领域（及其他适宜项目）：

  • 用于挣钱/花钱的时间
  • 用于个人身心照顾的时间（睡眠、锻炼等）
  • 用于放松/休闲娱乐的时间
  • 与家人/朋友/邻里相处的时间
  • 参与教会相关活动的时间
  • 参与事奉/志愿服务的时间
  • 与上帝亲近的时间（灵修、祷告、读经）

这样一份时间审计或许无法全面评估每项活动中所付出时间的质量，但它是一个良好的开端。一个整体的时间与生活轮廓便开始显现出来。我们在哪里投入时间，正是衡量我们管家价值观的最有力指标。

Strategies for Generous Living

Jesus’ message regarding money is that it is a means for living, not an end in itself. A closer look at Jesus’ life and message can open the way for a fulfilling life lived for God. The focus must be that of 1 Peter 3:15: “Simply concentrate on being completely devoted to Christ” (Phillips).

Strong forces are at work to distract us from Christ. We are not likely to take steps toward solving a problem if we do not recognize a problem. Scripture points out our bent toward greed and the eventual impoverishment to which it leads. Personal stock-taking is a healthy and necessary exercise. As part of such a check, we might gather data. We might keep a weekly time log involving the following areas (and others as appropriate):

  • Time used to earn/spend money
  • Time used for personal care (sleep, exercise, etc.)
  • Time used for relaxation/entertainment
  • Time with family/friends/neighbours
  • Time in church-related activities
  • Time spent ministering/volunteering
  • Time with God

Such an audit may not evaluate the quality of time expended in each instance, but it is a beginning. The overall profile begins to take shape. Where we spend our time is a strong indicator of our stewardship values.

优先考量上帝之国的利益（Prioritizing Kingdom Interests）

作为基督徒生活，就是学习活出与上帝的掌权相称的生命。靠工作维持生计固然合乎基督徒体统，但若沉迷于赚钱以致挤占了属灵的追求，问题就随之而生了。在建立生活方式的过程中，我们的抉择展现了内心的优先次序。生活方式涵盖了在住房、陈设、衣着、奢侈品、娱乐、投资与储蓄以及奉献等方面的抉择。所有这些抉择，都是在某种既定优先次序的主导下作出的。

我们无时无刻不在实践着优先次序的取舍。例如，我们可能情愿买一辆小型二手车，因为对我们而言，家居装潢或高档家庭影院比交通工具的体面更重要。另一些人则可能宁愿住在仅有基本设施的狭窄住处，以便省下钱满足自己观光旅游的渴望。若将对耶稣及其国度的奉献注入这些考量中，优先次序的权衡便具有了更深刻的属灵维度。例如，上述的优先次序是否会发生转变——将旅行费用转用于在基督徒夏令营担任辅导员，或是参与短期宣教和救灾事工的自愿奉献？我们是否愿意减少外出就餐的开销，转而更多款待远道而来的国际留学生？

在许多北美基督徒心中，成功与财富往往占据着极高的优先地位。然而，先知耶利米呼召我们确立一个远高于这两者的终极优先次序：

“智慧人不要因他的智慧夸口，勇士不要因他的勇力夸口，财主不要因他的财物夸口。夸口的却因他有聪明，认识我是耶和华，又知道我喜悦在世上施行慈爱、公平和公义，以此夸口。这是耶和华说的。”（耶利米书 9:23-24，和合本修订版）

认识上帝——意味着与上帝活在充满生机的联结之中——是远高于财富与世俗成功的价值所在。

对于信徒而言，至高的价值属于基督和祂的国度。在理想的境况下，基督徒在这个世界上最渴盼见到的，莫过于上帝的公义与公平得以彰显，上帝之国的各项事业蓬勃发展。基督徒立志将自己的时间与资源奉献于上帝之国。这其中的实际含义显而易见：金钱与时间必须从生活的其他领域转向上帝之国的事工。如果将五百美元奉献给某个需要或事工，这五百美元就无法再用于投资股票或购买更大、更好的冰箱。

Prioritizing Kingdom Interests

To live as a Christian is to learn to live as fits the reign of God. While it is Christian to work to make a living, what becomes problematic is becoming so absorbed in making money that spiritual interests are crowded out. In establishing our lifestyle, we make choices that exhibit our priorities. A lifestyle includes choices about housing, furnishings, clothing, luxuries, entertainment, investments and savings, and giving. Such decisions are made with a set of priorities in mind.

We are constantly exercising priorities. For example, we may settle for purchasing a small used car because for us the decor of the home or a highquality entertainment centre is more important than mode of transportation. Others may choose to live in cramped living quarters with minimal comforts if by so doing they can indulge their yearnings for sightseeing and travel. Inject into these equations a devotion to Jesus and his kingdom, and the prioritizing process takes on complexity. For example, will any of the above priorities shift to using one’s travel money to spend time as a counsellor at a Christian camp, or to volunteer for short-term missions or relief work? Would one spend less on eating out and more on hospitality to international students?

Success and wealth are high priorities for many North American Christians. Jeremiah the prophet called for a priority higher than either of these. “Do not let the wise boast in their wisdom, do not let the mighty boast in their might, do not let the wealthy boast in their wealth; but let those who boast boast in this, that they understand and know me, that I am the LORD; I act with steadfast love, justice, and righteousness in the earth, for in these things I delight” ( Jeremiah 9:23-24 NRSV). To know God, which means to live in dynamic connection with God, is a value higher than wealth or success.

For a believer, greatest value is attached to Christ and his kingdom. Ideally, a Christian should want more than anything in the world to see God’s justice and righteousness prevail and kingdom causes succeed. Christians decide that their time and resources need to be committed to the kingdom. The implication is straightforward: money and time are shifted from other areas of life to kingdom work. If $500 is given toward a need or ministry, that $500 will not be available for investment in stocks or purchase of a bigger, better refrigerator.

管家职分的前提假设（Stewardship Assumptions）

管家职分的概念建立在若干前提假设之上，每一个假设都会在我们的实际生活方式中体现出来：

  1. 第一个基本假设涉及所有权（Ownership）。在西方文化中，个人拥有财产所有权；在某些集体文化中，群体共同拥有并分享财产；在古代崇拜巴力的地区，国王垄断了土地的所有权；而基督徒宣告：万物皆属于上帝。与此相符的合宜回应乃是感恩知足（腓 4:11-12）。如果上帝是真正的主人，那么无论所得看似微薄还是丰厚，我都有充分的理由心存感激。过度消费主义的生活方式正是由不知足的心态所催生的。将财产视为上帝赏赐的更深一层含义在于：我们蒙福，是为了使他人得福。如同上帝的其他恩赐一样，充裕的物质财富应当用来造福他人（雅 2:14-17；太 10:8）。
  1. 第二个假设涉及管理（Management）。管理包括注重创造性的生产，以及对财务与收支的审慎监督。每一位基督徒都是受托管理者，其职责是为所有者——上帝——的利益来管理资产。管家固然可以支取合宜的份额以维系自身生计，但其根本目标是为了增加主人的收益。财务管理的目标之一，是将资源置于稳妥安全的投资之中。耶稣教导我们，最稳妥安全的投资莫过于上帝的国度（太 6:19-34）。
  1. 第三个假设是交账的问责性（Accountability）。基督徒在进行投资决策时当肩负社会责任。个人的恩赐与财产绝非纯粹的私人事务；管家职分的履行具有公共维度的见证。耶稣警告说，在末世必有一场终极的大盘点与交账（太 25:14-46）。教会可以为会众设立相互守望的问责小组（accountability groups），使大家在具体的投资抉择和整体的生活方式议题上共同察验与分辨。

律法主义与个人主义乃是两个极端的危险倾向。生活准则难以简单地实行一刀切的标准化。在某些情况下，购置贵重物品或资产可能是对资源的值得称许的善用；但在另一些情境下，同样的支出可能是有害的，甚至是可悲的。

Stewardship Assumptions

The concept of stewardship turns on several assumptions, each of which plays itself out practically in our lifestyle. One basic assumption touches on ownership. In Western cultures, individuals own things. In some cultures, the group owns and shares things. In places where Baal was worshipped, the king owned the land. Christians say all belongs to God. A fitting response is thankful contentment (Philippians 4:11-12). If God is owner, I have strong reason to be thankful both for what is termed a little and what is termed a lot. A lifestyle of excessive consumerism is fueled by discontent. A further implication of seeing properties as God’s gift is that we are blessed in order to be a blessing. Like other gifts of God, material abundance is to be used for the benefit of others ( James 2:14-17; Matthew 10:8).

A second assumption involving stewardship is management. Management includes attention to creative production and oversight of finances and income. Every Christian is a manager of assets in the interest of the owner, God. An appropriate amount is retained for the livelihood of the manager, but the object is to enhance the owner’s gains. One objective of money management is to place the resources in secure investments. Jesus teaches that the most secure investment is the kingdom of God (Matthew 6:19-34). A third assumption in exercising stewardship is accountability. Christians should exercise social responsibility in making their investments. One’s gifts are not a private affair. There is a public dimension to the exercise of stewardship. Jesus warns of an end-time audit (Matthew 25:14-46). The church can offer accountability groups to its members that will allow for discernment in both specific investments and general lifestyle issues. Legalism and individualism stand as dangers at the extremes. Rules cannot be easily standardized. In some cases, expenditures involving costly goods or properties may be a praiseworthy use of resources, while in other circumstances such expenditures would be detrimental or even deplorable.

有效实践管家职分的实用建议（Practical Hints for Effective Stewardship）

在物欲横流与消费主义充斥的时代，以下建议有助于我们将精力聚焦于为上帝之国履行忠心的管家职分，并简化个人生活方式：

  1. 确立一份关于个人金钱管理的“使命宣言”（Mission Statement）：认真思想在信仰理想、收入水平、生活标准、投资、储蓄及退休养老等方面，哪些对你至关重要，且与上帝在你生命中的主权治理相一致。
  2. 根据“实用价值”而非“身份地位”的准则来购置物品：例如在汽车与服饰上，若以身份象征为主要考量，往往需要巨额的开销；但若以实用性为准则，支出几乎必然会大幅减少。
  3. 培养慷慨施予的习惯：亲身体会借着乐善好施、与人分享所带来的得胜自由。
  4. 将各项投资与大额支出作为恒切祷告与寻求劝诫的事项：寻求圣灵的引导，同时不可忽视信仰共同体中属灵同伴的智慧指引。
  5. 坚决抵制商业广告与消费主义的蛊惑宣传：正如一位学者所指出的：“抗衡并反思当下的世风（Contradicting the present），乃是当代福音派的核心使命。”当警惕当今铺天盖地的广告与营销说辞的诱惑——它们许诺通过占有更多、获取更多来带来幸福与满足。务要牢牢掌握对自己支出的主导权。

Practical Hints for Effective Stewardship

Following are a number of suggestions that can help focus efforts toward effective stewardship for God’s kingdom and the simplifying of one’s lifestyle in an age of materialism and consumerism: Establish a mission statement with respect to your money. Consider what ideals, income level, standard of living, investment, savings, and retirement issues are important to you and consistent with God’s rule in your life.

Acquire possessions according to criteria of usefulness rather than status. Cars and clothing, for example, call for excessive outlays if status is a major criterion. If usefulness is the criterion, expenditures are almost certainly less.

Cultivate the habit of generosity. Experience the freedom that comes from giving things away.

Make matters of investments and larger expenditures the subject of prayer and counsel. Seek the guidance of the Holy Spirit. Don’t neglect the guidance of individuals in the community of faith.

Refuse to accept the propaganda of advertising and consumerism. It has been observed that “Contradicting the present is the central evangelical task in our time.” Be wary of seduction by today’s endless ads and sales pitches that promise happiness and fulfillment through acquiring more and having more. Remain in control of your expenditures.

ARTICLE 16 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 16 条：工作、安息与主日 (Work, Rest, and The Lord’s Day)

Article 16: Work, Rest, and The Lord’s Day

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

引言

我们坚信，上帝创造的作为是人类活动的典范。虽然罪玷污并败坏了工作与安息，但蒙救赎的子民蒙召，应当将劳作与安息恢复到其应有的地位。

Work, Rest, and The Lord’s Day

We believe that God’s act of creation is the model for human activity. While sin has corrupted work and rest, redeemed people are called to restore labour and rest to their proper place.

工作 (Work)

基督徒作为照着上帝形象被造的受造物，当尽己所能忠心劳作，以此效法创造主。他们应当运用自己的能力与资源来荣耀上帝、服事他人。因着身负基督之名，所有信徒都蒙召当诚实、殷勤地工作，并以尊重和尊严对待他人。

Work

As creatures made in the image of God, Christians imitate the Creator by working faithfully as they are able. They are to use their abilities and resources to glorify God and to serve others. Because they bear the name of Christ, all believers are called to work honestly and diligently and to treat others with respect and dignity.

安息 (Rest)

正如上帝在第七日歇息了一切的工，人类也蒙召遵守定期的安息。安息是对上帝供应的感恩之举；安息是信靠之举，提醒人们：托住并维系生命的不是人的劳碌，而是上帝自己；安息也是盼望之举，预尝并期盼由耶稣复活所确立的终末永恒安息。

Rest

As God rested on the seventh day, people are called to observe regular times of rest. Rest is an act of thankfulness for what God has provided. It is an act of trust, reminding humans that it is not their work but God who sustains them. Rest is an act of hope, anticipating the future rest assured by the resurrection of Jesus.

主日 (The Lord’s Day)

顺从新约的榜样，信徒在一周的第一天聚集，记念基督的复活与圣灵的降临。在主日，信徒满怀喜乐地专心敬拜、聆听圣道教训、擘饼、祷告、团契相交与服事，将劳作严格限制于必要的工作与怜悯之善行。

The Lord’s Day

Following the New Testament example, believers gather together to commemorate the resurrection of Christ and the coming of the Holy Spirit on the first day of the week. On the Lord’s day, believers joyfully devote themselves to worship, instruction in the Word, breaking of bread, prayer, fellowship, and service, limiting their labour to work of necessity and deeds of mercy.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 1:26-31；2:15；3:14-19；出埃及记 20:8-11；利未记 25:1-7；申命记 5:12-15；诗篇 46:10；95:6-11；传道书 3:13；马可福音 2:23-3:6；路加福音 24:1-36；使徒行传 2:1；20:7；罗马书 14:5-10；以弗所书 6:5-9；歌罗西书 3:22-4:1；帖撒罗尼迦后书 3:6-10；希伯来书 4:1-10；10:23-25。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 1:26-31; 2:15; 3:14-19; Exodus 20:8-11; Leviticus 25:1-7; Deuteronomy 5:12-15; Psalm 46:10; 95:6-11; Ecclesiastes 3:13; Mark 2:23-3:6; Luke 24:1-36; Acts 2:1; 20:7; Romans 14:5-10; Ephesians 6:5-9; Colossians 3:22-4:1; 2 Thessalonians 3:6-10; Hebrews 4:1-10; 10:23-25.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

工作与闲暇是北美基督徒核心关切的议题之一。我们坚信，上帝造人乃是为了让人既能劳作又能安息；然而在人类的背叛与堕落中，我们败坏了劳作与安息。作为蒙救赎的子民，教会蒙召将劳作与安息恢复到人类生命中应有的地位。

Work and leisure are among the central concerns of North American Christians. We believe that God created people for both work and rest, but in our rebellion, we have corrupted both labour and rest. As a redeemed people, the church is called to restore labour and rest to their proper places in human life.

一、工作 (Work)

工作是人类生存中普遍存在且不可或缺的核心要素。在《创世记》中，上帝创造世界的行动本身就被描述为工作（创 2:2-3）。在伊甸园中，亚当被赋予为各样走兽命名的工作（创 2:18-20）。同样，《诗篇》104篇23节表明，从事劳作乃是人类受造的本性。因此，工作本身绝非堕落犯罪的产物。

罪所带来的恶果之一，乃是上帝咒诅了土地，从而使工作从与上帝同工的喜乐参与，演变为了与自然造化之间痛苦艰辛的搏斗（创 3:17-19）。此外，工作也会因人类的不义而遭到扭曲与败坏，正如古埃及残暴奴役以色列人的历史一样（出 1-13）。

人是按着上帝的形象被造的（创 1:26-27）。人具有上帝形象的表现之一，在于人天生具有社会性（关系性）。正是通过被造为男和女，人类共同彰显并反映出上帝的形象。社会本性与工作之间的紧密联系在《创世记》第2章中清晰可见：上帝为亚当寻找一个“配偶帮助他”；换言之，亚当在园中独居劳作并不好。对亚当而言，唯一相称的助手是另一个有位格的人（创 2:18-25）。因此，工作乃是人类受造之社会本性中不可分割的一部分。

《创世记》1章26节明确将受造拥有上帝的形象与“治理”（Dominion / 管理权）联系在一起。关于“治理”这一概念，必须注意两点：

  1. 治理意味着行动：人类受托负责对受造物进行恰当的善用与悉心的照管。
  2. 治理具有全备的管家责任：治理“海里的鱼、空中的鸟……各样走兽……全地”（创 1:28-30）的命令，应当被理解为涵盖对整个受造界的照料责任。虽然圣经文本并未在此逐一详述，但我们完全有理由将人类的责任延伸至自然界以外的整个人类文化产物。除了照管上帝所造的各样造物之外，人类也当负责任地善用人类文化中的各项要素，诸如经济、艺术、音乐、体育、教育、政治等等。各类正当的工作，都是人类当负的责任，也是人类受造的应有目的与归宿。

在《帖撒罗尼迦后书》3章10节中，使徒保罗阐明了理解工作的一项重要原则：“我们在你们那里的时候，曾吩咐你们说，若有人不肯做工，就不可吃饭。”每个人在力所能及的范围内都有责任劳作。保罗同样清楚指明，满足个人生活所需绝非工作的唯一理由，甚至不是首要理由。在《哥林多前书》16章2节中，他指出信徒应当在每逢七日的第一日，照自己的进项抽出若干留着，以便周济那些有需要的人。人不仅要自食其力、对自己负责，更有责任透过自己的工作所得去供应和帮补他人。

保罗在《以弗所书》6章5-9节和《歌罗西书》3章22节至4章1节中对仆人（奴仆）和主人的教导，向我们启示了基督徒如何能够救赎已被罪败坏的职场与劳动世界。保罗并未在字面上直接发起对奴隶制度的政治声讨；然而，他向做奴仆之人所发出的训诫，却要求他们活出超越奴隶身份的尊严：奴仆不可消极怠工、偷盗或抗命不从，反而当以忠心、尽责的敬业态度行事。他们应当如同自由人一样生活与劳作！同样，做主人的也当以公义、公平的态度对待仆人，如同对待与自己平等的同胞弟兄一样。保罗吩咐基督徒的生活行事，必须在根本上瓦解并解除奴隶制度的罪恶与毒素。

当代北美社会的消费资本主义（Consumer Capitalism）催生了另一种形式的奴役。有些人身处现代工厂的流水线中，发现劳动者与最终产品之间几乎没有任何有意义的情感或创造性联结；有些人从事推销与零售，内心却深知自己所兜售的商品毫无终极价值可言；还有一些人身居管理层，却承受着层层重压，被迫在削减劳工报酬的同时榨取更高的生产力。各种社会体制的挤压交织在一起，使工作沦为毫无意义的苦役与熬炼。在当今社会中，保罗的话语如同明灯，照亮了身处职场的基督徒：上帝呼召工人在履行职责时诚实利用时间，即使对最终产品的用途缺乏决定权，也要尽心尽力拿出高品质的工作成果；更要时刻谨记，自己在职场中的行事为人与专业表现，本身就是向上帝作出的美好见证。

Work

Work is a pervasive and essential element of human existence. The action of God in the creation of the world is described in Genesis as work (Genesis 2:2-3). In the Garden, Adam is given the work of naming the animals (Genesis 2:18-20). Similarly, Psalm 104:23 indicates that it is the nature of persons to engage in labour. Thus work itself is not a consequence of the fall into sin. One of the consequences of sin is that God cursed the ground, thereby changing work from joyful participation with God into a painful struggle against the elements (Genesis 3:17-19). Work can be corrupted by human injustice as in the case of Egypt enslaving Israel (Exodus 1-13). Human beings are created in the image of God (Genesis 1:26-27). One of the ways in which people are made in God’s image is that they are by nature social. It is by being both male and female that humans reflect the image of God. The connection between social nature and work is seen in Genesis 2. God seeks a helper for Adam. That is, it is not good for Adam to work alone in the garden. The only suitable helper for Adam is another person (Genesis 2:18-25). Work is part of the created social nature of human beings.

Genesis 1:26 explicitly links being made in the image of God with having dominion. Two things should be noted about the notion of dominion. First, dominion implies action. Humans are to be responsible for the proper use of and care for creation. Second, the command to have dominion over the “fish... birds...every living creature...the whole earth” (Genesis 1:28-30) should be understood to involve responsibility for all of creation. While it is not explicit in the biblical text, it is fair to understand human responsibility to extend to the products of human culture as well as to the natural world. In addition to caring for the creatures which God has created, humans are also responsible for use of such elements of human culture as economics, art, music, sport, education, politics, and the like. Work of all sorts is the responsibility and proper end of human beings.

In 2 Thessalonians 3:10, Paul articulates an important principle for understanding work: “We gave you this command: Anyone unwilling to work should not eat” (NRSV). Individuals are responsible to work as they are able. Paul is also clear that providing for personal needs is not the only, or even the primary, reason for working. In 1 Corinthians 16:2, he indicates that at the beginning of the week believers should set aside a portion of what they have to help those who are in need. While people are responsible for themselves, they are also responsible to use their work to provide for others. Paul’s instructions to slaves and masters in Ephesians 6:5-9 and Colossians 3:22-4:1 give some indication of how Christians can redeem the corrupted world of work. Paul does not directly condemn slavery. However, he does give instructions to slaves which command them to live as if they are not slaves. Rather than shirking their duties, stealing, and being insubordinate, the slaves are to act responsibly and faithfully. They are to act as if they are free people. Similarly, masters are to treat their slaves justly, fairly, and in the way that one would treat an equal. Paul told Christians to act in such a way that the evils of slavery were significantly undermined and disarmed.

The consumer capitalism of contemporary North America lends itself to another kind of slavery. Some people find themselves working in factories which provide very little meaningful connection between worker and product. Others find themselves selling products that they realize are of little ultimate significance. Some find themselves in management being pressured to demand more productivity with less compensation for workers. A variety of social pressures combine to make work seem like meaningless drudgery. The words of Paul provide a light for the Christian worker in our society. God calls workers to use their time honestly in performing work, to do quality work even if they have little control over the final product of their work, and to remember that their performance functions as a witness to God.

二、安息 (Rest)

《出埃及记》20章11节与《申命记》5章15节针对守安息日的诫命，提供了互为补充的双重视角：

  • 在《出埃及记》20章11节中，遵守安息日的诫命扎根于上帝亲自树立的创造典范；
  • 在《申命记》5章15节中，安息日则让人回想上帝将以色列人从埃及为奴之地拯救出来的救赎大工。

这两处经文提醒我们：安息是一个庄严的机会，让我们既为上帝的创造之功感恩，也为上帝的救赎大恩献上赞美。

安息日律例提醒我们，遵守安息日的歇息乃是一种信靠的举动。当以色列人在旷野漂泊时，他们必须笃信：为安息日所留存的吗哪绝不会发臭生虫（出 16:22-30）。同样，当上帝吩咐以色列人守安息年，整整一年不可耕种收割庄稼时，百姓必须用实际行动证明，他们全心信靠上帝必会供应他们所需（出 23:10-11；利 25）。在当今时代，经济财富与军事力量构成了强烈的诱惑，诱使人们将安全感建立在上帝之外的事物上。守安息的呼召迫使我们深刻省察：作为一个群体，我们究竟仰赖谁的供应？

《希伯来书》第4章提醒我们，终极的安息尚未完全实现（4:1）。上帝所应许的未来安息，是通过对耶稣的信心（4:2）以及向上帝顺服（4:6）而得以进入的。在那终极的安息中，安息与工作将不再彼此对立或脱节（4:9-10）。在一个同时败坏了工作与安息的堕落文化中，这一应许赐给信徒坚实的力量去救赎工作与安息的真义。正是这永恒的盼望，托住了逆境中的上帝子民。

Rest

Exodus 20:11 and Deuteronomy 5:15 give complementary explanations of the command to rest on the Sabbath. In Exodus 20:11, the command to honour the Sabbath is grounded in the pattern of creation demonstrated by God. Deuteronomy 5:15 recalls God’s delivering Israel from slavery in Egypt. These two passages remind us that rest is an opportunity to thank God for creating us and also for redeeming us.

Sabbath laws remind us that observance of Sabbath rest is an act of trust. When the Israelites wandered in the desert, they were required to trust that manna would not rot when kept for the Sabbath (Exodus 16:22-30). Similarly, when Israel was commanded to observe Sabbath years in which they did not plant or harvest grain, the people were called to show by their actions that they trusted in God to provide for them (Exodus 23:10-11; Leviticus 25). Economic and military security provide contemporary temptations to put our trust in something other than God. The call to Sabbath observance calls us to examine carefully whom we as a people rely on for provision. Hebrews 4 reminds us that rest is not yet fully realized (4:1). The future rest promised by God is the result of faith in Jesus (4:2) and on obedience to God (4:6). The future rest will be a time when rest and work are no longer at odds (4:9-10). This provides believers with the strength to redeem both work and rest in a culture which corrupts both aspects of life. Hope sustains people in the face of adversity.

三、主日 (The Lord’s Day)

在新约圣经中，“主日”（The Lord’s Day）这一称号仅在《启示录》1章10节中出现过一次。在古罗马帝国文化中，特定日子被定为向凯撒效忠崇拜的“皇帝之日”（Augustus Day / Imperial Days）。早期教会决意分别为圣设立并庆祝“主日”，与当时他们身处的偶像崇拜与异教文化构成了鲜明对照。

今天，北美人群所面临的拜偶像诱惑之一，就是崇拜“休闲之神”（the god of leisure）。然而，世俗意义上的休闲不过是一种虚假的赝品安息。我们许多人把安息与消遣娱乐混为一谈，沉浸在自夸成就之中，而不再仰赖上帝为我们的供应者。我们误以为休闲是自己靠拼搏“挣来的”，却未能认识到真正的安息是来自上帝白白的恩典与厚赐。当代的北美基督徒再次面临呼召：要将每周的一日宣告为主的日子（主日），而不是仅仅把周末视作“属于我自己的消遣时光”。

显而易见，新约时代的信徒在一周的第一天聚集，是为了记念耶稣基督的复活（林前 16:1-2；徒 20:7）。教会历代悠久的传统正是效法初期信徒的这一实践。七日的第一日作为专门分别为圣敬拜上帝的日子，是极其适切的：因为它同时提醒信徒记念上帝起初的创造（创 1:1）、基督胜过死亡的复活（太 28:1；可 16:2；路 24:1；约 20:1），以及五旬节圣灵的降临（徒 2）。

正如前文所述，《出埃及记》20章与《申命记》5章提醒我们，安息与敬拜之间存在着密不可分的内在联系。这意味着，定期敬拜的一个核心内容，应当是数算并记念上帝为祂子民所成就的奇妙大能作为。我们记念上帝作为的重要方式之一，就是举行并领受圣餐（主餐）。在其他层面，我们的敬拜也应当包含数算与记念上帝如何创造并托住这一群全新的立约子民。

然而作为互为平衡的对照，记念保罗在《罗马书》14章5节的话是大有益处的：“有人看这日比那日强；有人看日日都是一样。只是各人心里要意见坚定。”保罗提醒我们，我们在某一天举行敬拜，乃是教会所采纳的一种约定俗成的惯例。从神学本质上而言，具体使用哪一天并不是绝对的律法条规；但显而易见、至关重要的是，基督徒必须分别出某一天来全心敬拜上帝。

作为在基督里新造的人，我们蒙召去救赎自己的工作；作为蒙救赎的圣约子民，我们蒙召透过放下手中的劳作，切实行出感恩、信靠与盼望。我们的安息应当以敬拜上帝和与众人彼此相交为显著特征。我们的敬拜与团契，应当成为终极景象的前瞻与预尝——届时，万国、各族、各方的信徒，都将一同在三位一体上帝荣耀的同在中得享永远的安息。

The Lord’s Day

The only use of the term “The Lord’s day” in the New Testament occurs in Revelation 1:10. Roman culture set aside certain days as “emperor’s days.” The commitment of the early church to celebrate a “Lord’s day” is in direct contrast to the idolatrous pagan culture in which they found themselves. Today, one of the idolatrous temptations that North Americans face is to worship the god of leisure. Leisure is a counterfeit rest. Many of us have confused rest with leisure, glorying in our own accomplishments rather relying on God as our provider. We believe we have earned leisure, not recognizing rest as a gift from God. Contemporary North American Christians are again called to proclaim a day of the week as the Lord’s day rather than thinking of the weekend as “time for myself.”

It is clear that New Testament believers gathered on the first day of the week to remember the resurrection of Jesus (1 Corinthians 16:1-2; Acts 20:7). It is a long tradition of the Church to imitate the practice of those believers. The first day is appropriate as a day set aside for worship in that it reminds believers of the creation (Genesis 1:1), the resurrection (Matthew 28:1; Mark 16:2; Luke 24:1; John 20:1), and the day of Pentecost (Acts 2). As noted earlier, Exodus 20 and Deuteronomy 5 remind us that there is an intimate connection between rest and worship. This suggests that a regular part of worship should be remembering the mighty acts of God on behalf of His people. One of the ways that we remember the acts of God is through the celebration of the Lord’s Supper. In other ways our worship should also involve remembering what it is that God has done in creating and sustaining a new people.

As counterpoint, however, it is useful to remember the words of Paul in Romans 14:5: “Some judge one day to be better than another, while others judge all days to be alike. Let all be fully convinced in their own minds” (NRSV). Paul reminds us that our observance of a day of worship is a convention that we adopt. It does not matter which day is used. Clearly it is essential that Christians set aside some day to worship.

As new creatures, we are called to redeem our work. As a redeemed people, we are called to tangibly show our thankfulness, trust, and hope by setting aside our work for a time. Our rest should be characterized by worship of God and fellowship with other people. Our worship and fellowship should be a foreshadowing of the time when believers of all nations and languages rest in the presence of the triune God.

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

工作与安息是北美教会面临的重大课题。我们所处的社会被一种根深蒂固的“忙碌欲求”所驱使——要么忙于追逐经济效益，要么忙于追逐娱乐消遣。我们的自我认同与这种对忙碌的渴求紧紧捆绑在一起：我们误以为，唯有当自己具有生产力时，才是有用的人、才有价值。我们在时间使用上的价值观与优先次序，彻底被这种忙碌的冲动所塑造。因此，《信仰宣言》深切认识到圣经关于工作、安息之需要以及遵守主日的教导具有极其重大的现实意义。

Work and rest are major issues for the North American church. We are a society driven by a deep-seated need to be busy, either with economically profitable activity or with recreation. Our self-image is tied to this need to be busy: we are useful, worth something, when we are productive. Our values and priorities regarding time use are shaped by the drive to busyness. Therefore the Confession of Faith sees the importance of biblical teaching regarding our work, our need for rest, and our observance of the Lord’s day.

一、工作 (Work)

我们所从事的工作构成了我们人生的主要组成部分。约翰·雷德科普（John Redekop）在《今日信仰》（Faith Today，1989年9/10月号，第18-23页）的一篇文章中估计：“一个普通的加拿大上班族，从全职工作的第一天起到退休庆祝会为止，大约会在工作岗位上度过88,000个小时。”他由此得出结论：“我们大多数人醒着的时间里，有将近40%是花在工作上的。”

然而从圣经角度来看，工作绝不仅仅是在“岗位工位”上所度过的时间。广义的工作还包括：

  • 照料自己的家庭（提供食物、遮风避雨的住所、安全感与稳定生活）；
  • 维护社会正常运转所必需的基础设施（政府治理、医疗卫生、公共交通、商业贸易等）；
  • 探索上帝受造界的广度（科学）与攀登人类文化的高峰（艺术）。

教会必须向信徒清晰传达：凡是负起责任的生活方式，各类正当的工作都是荣耀而尊贵的。企业高管、车库里的汽修工人、全职操持家务的母亲或父亲——都应当受到牧养与鼓励，在自己的劳作中寻见上帝所赐的尊严与目的。有一间堂会曾借着设立“劳动节主日”（Labour Day Sunday）来记念这一点：那天邀请每位信徒穿着平时工作日的工作服来教会聚会（当然，只有在平时不这样穿的情况下这才有特殊意义！）。在这样的主日，聚焦职场世界的见证分享与讲道教导显得尤为适切。另一间教会则开设了聚焦职场世界的成人基督教教育系列课程，邀请从事各行各业的信徒接受访谈，回答以下三个关键问题：

  1. “你在工作中获得的最大奖赏与满足感是什么？”
  2. “有哪些宏观或全球性的力量正在影响你的行业？”
  3. “你如何在自己的职业中融合与践行你的信仰？”

在我们的教导中，区分“天职/呼召”（vocation）与“职业/职业活动”（occupation）会大有助益：

  • 我们的“职业”（occupation）是指占据我们具体时间的事物：商业经营、家务料理、专业业务、农场劳作、求学读书等等；
  • 我们的“天职”（vocation）则是上帝对我们的“呼召”（calling，林前 7:17）。它是上帝在特定的职业与社会角色中，为我们的属灵生命成长所量身定制的独特心意。

不论我们具体从事何种工作，我们的根本呼召与天职乃是在凡事上效法基督、活出基督的样式；而我们世上的职业，正是这一属灵蜕变得以展开的舞台。有时，我们的属灵天职会引导我们转换具体职业：有些人为了跟随基督的呼召，毅然退出了军队（符合门诺和平主义非抵抗信仰传承），或是离开了存在不道德行为的商业公司，或是投身于全职教牧事工。我们渴望让每一位基督徒都明白：人人都有上帝的呼召，人人都在从事圣工（ministry）。

雷·拜斯特伦（Ray Bystrom）在其十期系列文章的第一篇《给职场人的十句话》（Ten Words For Those Who Work，刊于《职场》杂志 Marketplace，24/6，第11页）中对天职的阐述极具启发性。他说：“在生命中体察到上帝呼召的意识，应当激励全体上帝的子民——而绝非仅仅是教牧人员和宣教士——不论身处何种行业、工种、职业或事业，都当竭尽所能做到最好（西 3:23）。正如马丁·路德·金（Martin Luther King Jr.）曾说的那样：‘如果你蒙召做一名扫街工，你就应当像米开朗基罗作画、贝多芬谱曲、莎士比亚写诗那样去打扫街道。你应该把街道打扫得如此干净整洁，以至于天地间的天军与世人都不得不赞叹：这里曾住着一位伟大的扫街工，他把自己的工作做到了极致。’”

Work

A major component of our lives is the work we do. John Redekop, in an article in Faith Today (Sept/Oct 1989, 18-23), estimates that the “average working Canadian spends some 88,000 hours on the job from the first day of full-time employment until the retirement celebration.” He concludes that “most of us spend almost 40 percent of our waking time at work.” But biblically speaking, work is more than just time spent “on the job.” Work includes taking care of one’s household (providing food, shelter, security, stability), providing the infrastructure so that society can function (government, health care, transportation, commerce, etc.), and exploring the breadth of creation (science) and the heights of human culture (the arts). It is important for the church to communicate that all types of work are honourable as ways of living responsibly. The executive, the mechanic in the garage, the stay-at-home mom or dad—all these ought to be helped to find the God-given dignity and purpose of their work. One congregation has celebrated this by having a “Labour Day” Sunday in which everyone is to come to church in their weekday work clothes. (Of course, that would only work if it weren’t already the normal practice!) Testimonies and teaching focused on the world of work would be appropriate for such a day. Another church had an adult Christian Education series focusing on the world of work. People who worked in different jobs were interviewed with the following three questions: “What are the greatest rewards of your work?” “What are the larger or global forces that affect your occupation?” “How do you integrate your faith and your occupation?”

In our teaching, it can help us if we distinguish between our “vocation” and our “occupation.” Our occupation is what takes up our time: business, housekeeping, professional duties, the farm, school, and so on. Our vocation, on the other hand, is our “calling” from God (1 Corinthians 7:17). It is God’s uniquely tailored will for our spiritual growth, within a certain set of occupations and roles. Our calling, our vocation, is to become Christlike in whatever we do; our occupations form the stage on which this transformation takes place. Sometimes, our vocation will lead us to change occupations: some have moved out of the military, or out of a business with unethical practices, or into full-time pastoral work as a result of following the call of Christ. We want all Christians to understand that they have a calling, a ministry. Ray Bystrom is helpful as he describes vocation in his first of a ten-part series on “Ten Words For Those Who Work” (Marketplace, 24/6, p 11). He says, “a sense of God’s calling in one’s life should prompt all God’s people—not just clergy and missionaries—to do the very best, whatever their trade, job, occupation or career (Colossians 3:23). As Martin Luther King once said, ‘If you are called to be a street sweeper, you should sweep streets as Michelangelo painted or Beethoven composed music or Shakespeare wrote poetry. You should sweep streets so well that the host of heaven and earth will say, Here lived a great street sweeper who did his job well.’”

二、牧养不在职场中的人群 (Ministering to Those Out of the Workforce)

那些不在职场劳动大军中的人群又当如何牧养呢？教会必须时刻谨慎自守，切不可按着人的职业与社会地位将人划分为三六九等（雅 2:1-5）。人们往往习惯于将自我价值完全建立在自己的工作成就上：“我的工作定义了我是谁。”随后，我们在不知不觉中踏出了危险的第二步：“我的职业收入与地位决定了我有多大价值。”这是一种极其危险且属世的世俗假设，教会必须予以坚决的抵制！我们必须学会不从自己“所做的事”去寻找自我价值，而是从自己在上帝眼中的“宝贵身份”去确立价值。特别是在失业率居高不下的社区、因身心残障而无法就业的人群中，或是退休人员密集的会众里，教会清晰传达以下信息至关重要：无论每个人的就业与经济状况如何，在上帝眼中，人人都具有至高的神圣尊严。

我们当如何切实有效地牧养这些人？

  1. 转变交往习惯：我们可以格外留意，不要在接触新朋友时习惯性地脱口就问：“那么，你是做什么工作的？”
  2. 准确传讲真理：我们必须分清，保罗在《帖撒罗尼迦后书》3章10节所说的（“若有人不肯做工，就不可吃饭”），针对的是那些“拒绝做工的人”，而绝非那些“无法工作的人”。
  3. 关顾失业群体：教会必须体恤并体认失业者所承受的情感与经济重压。教会的职责是提醒失业信徒，一个人的位格价值绝不取决于是否拥有一份有偿报酬的工作。在毫不轻视失业所带来的痛苦的同时，教会可以为暂时失业的肢体提供在教会与社区中参与有意义服事的机会。
  4. 扶助残障人士：身心残障人士往往在世俗职场中遭受边缘化。教会的职责是为这些人的尊严与价值作见证，并带头确保不同能力程度的人都能获得从事有意义劳作的机会。
  5. 重构退休观念：如果我们以上帝的创造劳作为典范，我们就不会把今生的工作仅仅看作是通往“安享退休”的跳板。相反，最充实、最具意义的退休生活，应当是充满劳作与行动的生活——这种劳作不再受制于眼前的生计压力，而是更多由我们的恩赐、兴趣与能力所驱使，去造福教会与社会。

Ministering to Those Out of the Workforce

What about those who aren’t in the workforce? Churches ought always to be careful that we do not rank people according to their occupation ( James 2:1-5). We tend to place a lot of value on what we do: “I am what my job says I am.” And we unconsciously take a second step: “I’m worth what my job says I’m worth.” This is a dangerous assumption that needs to be firmly resisted by the church. We must learn to find our worth not in what we do, but in who we are in God’s sight. Especially in communities with high unemployment, or where there are people with disabilities that keep them from working, or with a lot of retirees, it is important that we project the message that all people have dignity in God’s eyes, regardless of their job situation.

How can we effectively pastor these people? We can be careful that our first question to newcomers isn’t automatically, “So, what do you do?” Furthermore, we should make sure that Paul’s word in 2 Thessalonians 3:10 (“Anyone unwilling to work should not eat” NRSV) isn’t directed at those who can’t work, but at those who refuse to work. As to the situation of the unemployed, there are several things that the church can do. First, we must be careful to recognize the emotional and financial stress that the unemployed will face. It is the role of the church to remind the unemployed that their value as a person is not tied to having a paying job. Without minimizing the pain of unemployment, the church can offer the person who is temporarily out of work opportunities for meaningful service within the community. Second, persons with disabilities are often marginalized in the workplace. It is the role of the church to witness for the worth of these people and to take the lead in seeing to it that people with different abilities are afforded opportunities for meaningful labour. Third, if we accept the work of God as our model, we will not view our work as primarily a means to an enjoyable retirement. Rather, the most fulfilling retirement is one filled with activity (work) that is less driven by immediate needs and more by our interests and abilities.

三、工作与意义 (Work and Meaning)

另一个严峻的挑战，源于现代社会中某些工作表面上的虚无与无意义感——许多人体验到这种徒劳如同一座牢笼，甚至是一种新型的奴役。有些人从事机械重复、看似缺乏宏观价值的工种；有些人销售着自己心知肚明毫无终极意义的产品；另一些人则在报酬微薄的情况下被逼追求更高的业绩指标。现代社会的重重压力，使得工作显得毫无价值甚至面目可憎。

对于致力于在“周一到周六的日常世界”中施行祝福并彰显尊严的教会而言，这是一场真正的牧养挑战。《传道书》提供了一个极佳的切入点：它不仅以同理心道出了这种日光之下的虚空与无意义，更指引了终极的出路——在每日日常的恩赐中得享喜乐（吃喝劳碌，传 3:13），并持守忠心敬畏事奉造物主上帝（传 12:1, 13）。

不仅如此，教会更应帮助基督徒走得更远：在圣灵的引导下，枯燥单调的职业可以被奇妙更新，使工作本身以及工作中所建立的各种人际关系，蜕变为具有属灵维度的活动——正如耶稣呼召加利利海边的普通渔夫，使他们从事为上帝的国度“得人如得鱼”的崇高使命一样（可 1:17）。无论从事何种工作，基督徒都蒙召诚实而优秀地完成工作，因为他们深知，自己的职业操守与工作态度正是在向上帝作见证。

Work and Meaning

A further challenge arises from the apparent meaninglessness of some modern work, a futility that many people experience as a trap, even a kind of slavery. Some may perform jobs which are repetitive or seem to lack larger value, or sell products that appear to have little ultimate significance. Others may suffer the stress of trying to achieve higher productivity with lower compensation. Many modern pressures can make work seem meaningless or even sinister. This is a true challenge for the church that seeks to bless and dignify the Monday to Saturday world. Ecclesiastes is a helpful starting point, both as an empathetic expression of this meaninglessness, and for its eventual conclusion: delight in the daily blessings (food and drink, 3:13), and persevere in serving God the Creator (12:1, 13). Christians should be helped to go further than this. Through the guidance of the Spirit, dreary occupations can be transformed so that they, or the relationship-building that they permit, become spiritually-focused activities, such as the fishermen whom Jesus called to start “catching people” for the kingdom (Mark 1:17). Whatever the job, Christians are called to work honestly and well, knowing their performance and attitude serve as a witness to God.

四、安息 (Rest)

作为一个根植于北欧（及东欧德裔门诺社群）历史传承的宗派，门诺弟兄会常常展现出所谓的“新教工作伦理”（Protestant work ethic）：勤勉、节俭，以及一种普遍不愿停下脚步享受劳动果实的倾向。如今，许多北美基督徒在对工作的痴迷、以牺牲安息与闲暇为代价这一方面，与不信者几乎毫无二致。

尽管俗语说“闲懒的手是魔鬼的工场”确实有其道理，但当我们把游手好闲（懒惰）等同于神圣的安息与闲暇时，我们就彻底错失了安息的福分。圣经提醒我们，受造的人类兼具劳作与安息的双重需要。安息在上帝的创造心意中，从来不是次于工作的附庸品。劳作与安息若保持合宜的平衡，就构成了上帝所设立的生命节奏，这是活出神国生活所必不可少的。内科医师理查德·斯文森（Richard Swenson）在其著作《留白》（Margin）中，呼吁生活在巨大压力下的读者重获平衡与生命留白，他写道：“唯有在安息之时，我们才暂停‘治理全地’的劳碌，转而俯伏敬拜那位赋予人治理权柄的创造主。”

我们必须确保对“安息”拥有一种清晰且合乎圣经的纯正理解。安息绝非仅仅是无所事事（对许多人来说，强行什么都不做反而像是一种折磨）。安息是使身心灵得以彻底更新的过程，因此在具体形式上具有独特的个体差异。

与此同时，圣经中的安息日律例教导我们，安息必然具有群体性的维度，因为整个家室（包括家庭成员、雇工、寄居者以及牲畜所构成的社会经济单元）都被涵盖在安息日的诫命中（出 20:10）。我们建议，这种安息应当在我们的社会家园与属灵家园中具体彰显出来——也就是在我们的家庭与教会之中。各个家庭可以选择以不同的方式来守安息，例如共进特殊的安息日爱筵，分别出时间一同祷告和读经，或是设立专属的家庭之夜（参阅卡罗尔·布拉佐 Carol Brazo 所著《不寻常的家》No Ordinary Home，该书为基督徒守安息提供了极具启发的视角；尤金·毕德生 Eugene Peterson 在《角度的运作》Working the Angles 一书中关于安息日的章节也极具助益）。

此外，我们必须警惕，切不可为了守安息日而破坏了整体时间的平衡。如果我们把七天的工作量硬塞进六天里拼命透支，以便在每周“腾出一天休息”，这种做法是完全行不通的。这完全违背了圣经中将安息视为一种属灵操练与信靠祭物的真义。定期安息带来的属灵福分，正在于向我们显明：我们并非人生的终极主宰，我们必须完全仰赖上帝及其信实的看顾与掌管。理想的基督徒生命绝不是匆忙奔波的生命，而是一种让闲暇留白与超然视角积极融入每日、每时的生命肌理之中的生活，而绝非仅仅体现在七日中的某一天。

Rest

As a denomination with its roots in northern Europe, Mennonite Brethren tend to display what has been called the “Protestant work ethic”: industriousness, frugality, and a general reluctance to pause and enjoy the fruits of labour. At present, many North American Christians are indistinguishable from their non-Christian counterparts in their obsession with work at the expense of rest and leisure. While there is truth to the proverb that “idle hands are the devil’s workshop,” we miss the blessing of rest when we equate idleness with rest and leisure. Scripture reminds us that we were created with a need for both work and rest. Never was rest secondary to work. Rest and work, when held in proper balance, provide the Godordained rhythm of life that is necessary for life in the kingdom of God. In his book Margin, the physician Richard Swenson, challenging his readers to a restored balance and margin for stressed lives, says, “it is at Sabbath time we suspend dominion work and instead worship the dominion-Maker.” We want to make sure we have a clear and biblically sound understanding of “rest.” Rest is not just inactivity (which might seem a chore to many people). Rest is what allows for the renewal of body and spirit, and is therefore something uniquely individual. At the same time, the biblical Sabbath laws teach us that rest should have a corporate dimension, since the whole household (the socio-economic unit of family, workers, livestock) is included in the Sabbath command (Exodus 20:10). We suggest that this kind of rest should be visible both in our social and spiritual households: our homes and our churches. Families have chosen to celebrate Sabbath in different ways, such as in a special meal with time set aside for prayer and Scripture reading, or with a special family night. (See Carol Brazo, No Ordinary Home, for an inspiring window into Christian Sabbath-keeping; Eugene Peterson’s chapter on the Sabbath in Working the Angles is also helpful.) Further, we ought to be careful that we do not practice the discipline of rest at the expense of the balance of our time. It doesn’t work to cram seven days’ worth of work into six, so that we can say that we are “taking one day off “ each week. That runs against the grain of the biblical concept of rest as a discipline and a sacrifice. Part of the blessing of a period of rest is to show us that we are not in control, that we are dependent on God and His providence. The ideal Christian life is not a hurried life; it is a life where leisure and perspective are an active part of the fabric of every day, every hour; not just during one day in seven.

五、安息日的真义 (The Meaning of Sabbath)

犹太人对安息日的理解十分值得我们汲取借鉴。在犹太传统中，安息日的休息被视为一段“感官与心灵丰富体验”的时光（sensual time）——这是一段透过人类所有的感官，尽情欢欣品味上帝创造之美好的神圣时刻。安息日的安歇为人提供了与上帝亲密相交的良机。犹太拉比传统相传，上帝在西奈山向摩西大声宣告了十诫中的九条，唯独安息日的诫命，上帝是柔声耳语的——因为安息日是与上帝深情相拥、亲密交融的时刻。虔诚的犹太人会朗诵《雅歌》来迎接安息日的降临：一是因为所罗门的诗篇赞美了作为创造高潮的亲密之爱；二是因为安息日被视为上帝与其圣约子民之间宛如新婚庆典般的深情相约。这一视角极大地帮助我们基督徒重新体认圣经所设立的安息那种充满生命力、活泼丰盛的属天福分。

无论安息采取何种外在形式，无论包含或排除哪些具体活动，我们都必须确保守住基督徒安息时段的核心原意：那就是以舍己付代价的方式向上帝献上感恩与信靠，享受属天的福乐与平安，并在上帝创造的美善世界中欢喜踊跃。对于全教会（或团契小组）守安息的群体承诺而言，也是同理。这种群体安息需要自律，也需要顺服群体就如何守安息所达成的共识。随着守安息的操练逐渐内化于心，其间的祝福便跃居首位，安息日便得以照着上帝的美意在我们身上成就其奇妙大工——使我们在属灵、情感以及身体上经历全方位的苏醒与更新。

The Meaning of Sabbath

We stand to learn something from a Jewish understanding of the Sabbath. In Jewish tradition, the Sabbath rest is seen as a “sensual” time: a time to delight, through all the human senses, in the goodness of God’s creation. The Sabbath rest gives opportunity for intimacy with God. Jewish tradition says that God loudly announced the Ten Commandments to Moses—all except the Sabbath command. This one he whispered, because the Sabbath is for intimacy with God. Pious Jews will recite from the Song of Solomon to welcome the Sabbath, first because Solomon’s poem celebrates the sexual intimacy which is the climax of the creation story, and second because the Sabbath is seen as a wedding celebration between God and His covenant people. This perspective helps us as Christians to understand the sensual and life-giving blessing of biblically appointed rest.

Whatever form it takes, whatever activities it includes or excludes, we want to make sure that the primary intentions for the Christian’s period of rest are upheld: a sacrificial expression of thanksgiving to God and trust in God, a time of blessing and peace, an opportunity to delight in the goodness of God’s world. The same holds true for a congregational (or smaller group) commitment to observe a Sabbath rest. This kind of rest takes discipline and submission to the group consensus as to how it will be observed. As the discipline of rest is internalized, the blessings become primary, and the Sabbath rest is allowed to have its way with us as God intended: for spiritual, emotional, and physical renewal.

六、主日 (The Lord’s Day)

论到圣经关于主日的教导，在教牧层面主要引发了两个关键问题：

  1. 我们应当如何（以及何时）遵守主日？
  2. 圣经关于安息日的教导与基督徒的主日之间究竟有何内在关联？

先探讨第二个问题会对我们大有裨益。

圣经中的安息日与基督徒的主日绝不能直接划等号！换言之，我们绝不能把守主日当作是在照搬遵守所谓的“基督教安息日”。尽管许多基督徒（如部分清教徒传统）可能持有这种观点，但我们必须坚决摒弃这种做法，因为这与再洗礼派（Anabaptist）的圣经诠释进路背道而驰——正如再洗礼派坚决拒绝将洗礼视为新约的（婴儿）割礼、拒绝将教会领袖等同于旧约祭司、拒绝将教堂建筑等同于旧约圣殿，以及拒绝将所谓的“基督教国家”及其发动的战争等同于旧约的神权以色列国一样。主日固然是我们践行从安息日所提炼出的神圣原则的良机，但在旧约律法意义上，主日绝不是安息日。

那么，安息日的“神圣性”（圣洁，创 2:3；出 20:11）又当如何看待？我们是否冒着亵渎上帝在创世之初所赐福之物的危险？

我们首先必须说明，上帝起初赐福并定为圣日的是“第七日”，而不是“第一日”。因此，若要严格恪守安息日的神圣律法，我们就应当遵守星期六（更准确地说，是从周五日落到周六日落），如同犹太人、基督复临安息日会以及少数其他群体所做的那样。在整本圣经中，第七日的本体“神圣性”从未被转移到另一天。

然而，因着耶稣基督的降临，两件决定性的事情发生了翻天覆地的转变：

  1. 耶稣基督是安息日的主：耶稣亲自宣告自己是“安息日的主”（太 12:8），因此祂拥有远高于安息日律例本身的至高地位与权威。
  2. 复活开启了新创造：透过祂荣耀的复活，耶稣将七日的第一日设立为“新创造”（New Creation）破晓并闯入这旧世界体系的神圣日子。

关于“圣洁与神圣性”的问题在五旬节得到了更为深刻的升华——当时初代信徒在七日的第一日“都聚集在一处”（徒 2:1）。作为一切神圣成圣之源头的圣灵，被浩浩荡荡地倾注在一个群体身上！由此可见，上帝的祝福与神圣性，如今首要显明在一群“圣洁的子民”——由圣灵所充满的弥赛亚群体（教会）身上，而非寄托于特定的神圣时辰或神圣处所。即使第七日依然具有神圣意义，但在上帝眼中，教会远比任何日子都要圣洁！当一间教会因为在“主日是否允许从事某项活动”上无法达成一致而陷入分裂时，这种纷争与分裂在上帝眼中，远比所谓的“干犯基督教安息日”更加令人痛心与忧伤。

这一切都向我们表明：我们绝不把旧约的安息日律法作为理解主日真义的首要依据；我们单单定睛于耶稣、祂复活的大能以及圣灵的丰盛赐下。至此，我们回到了最初的问题：我们究竟应当如何遵守主日？

遵守并庆祝主日的最佳时间，是在一周的第一天——也就是星期日，正如教会自使徒时代起一贯的历史实践一样。如此行是一项重大的神学宣告：我们宣告历史至关重要！在星期日庆祝主日，表明我们笃信上帝真实介入了人类历史——宇宙超然的主宰在千百年前某一个具体的星期日清晨，亲临并介入了这个破碎的地球。它表明我们传承着另一件超自然历史事件的血脉：那就是五旬节圣灵的倾倒，它标志着上帝真正子民的诞生。从个人经验我们深知，虽然庆祝朋友的生日不一定非要在生日当天不可，但当天庆祝无疑最为适切贴切。同理，在上帝最伟大的历史作为真实发生的那个日子（主日）来庆祝祂的大能救赎，是最为合宜得体的。

新约的信仰群体在主日聚集。我们可以说，这是一次属灵的大家庭聚会，旨在彼此勉励、互相激发“爱心，勉励行善”（来 10:24）。我们今天所谓的“去教会聚会”，正是源于圣徒在主日的这种聚集；因此，教会性的聚集是庆祝主日不可或缺的核心部分。我们务要确保，我们教会的聚会切实体现了新约对于“上帝子民聚集时应当且能够发生何事”的关切与期望。（参见第6条“教会的本质”，以及第1条“上帝”教牧应用中论及敬拜的段落。）

The Lord’s Day

There are two main pastoral issues that arise when it comes to the Bible’s teaching about the Lord’s day. First, how (and when) should we observe it? Second, what is the connection between the biblical teaching of Sabbath rest and the Lord’s day? We will be helped if we discuss the second question first. The biblical Sabbath and the Christian Lord’s day are not to be equated. That is, we do not observe the Lord’s day as if it were literally a Christian Sabbath. While many Christians may hold to that practice, we must reject it as inconsistent with an Anabaptist approach to the Bible (in the same way that Anabaptism rejects the notion of baptism as a Christian [infant] circumcision, church leaders as the Christian equivalent of Old Testament priests, a church building as the counterpart of the Old Testament temple, or a Christian nation and its wars as functionally equivalent to Old Testament theocratic Israel). The Lord’s day may be the occasion for us to practice principles which we derive from the Sabbath, but it is not a Sabbath in the Old Testament sense.

What about the “sanctity” of the Sabbath (Genesis 2:3; Exodus 20:11)? Are we not in danger of desecrating something which God blessed at the beginning of creation? We have to say, first of all, that God’s blessing was placed upon the seventh day, not the first, and therefore a strict practice of the sanctity of the Sabbath would lead us to observe Saturday (or more specifically, Friday sundown to Saturday sundown), as do Jews, Seventh Day Adventists, and a few other groups. The actual “sanctity” of the seventh was never, scripturally speaking, transferred to a different day.

But two things changed with Jesus. First, he proclaimed himself to be the Lord of the Sabbath, and therefore to have a greater status and authority than Sabbath observance itself. Second, he appointed with his resurrection the first day of the week as the day on which the New Creation broke into this world system. The question of “sanctity” was pressed further at Pentecost, when the first believers were “all together in one place” on the first day of the week (Acts 2:1). The Holy Spirit, who is the source of all divine sanctification, was poured out onto a people. Thus God’s blessing and sanctity are now seen above all in a sacred people, the messianic community of the Spirit, and not in sacred times or places. While the seventh day may still be sacred, the Church is more sacred in God’s eyes. When a church is divided because it cannot agree on whether or not a certain activity is “permitted” for a Christian on Sunday, this division is more grievous to God than any perceived “breaking” of a Christian Sabbath. All of this goes to say that we do not look to the Sabbath law as our primary source for understanding what the Lord’s day is about. We look to Jesus, the power of his resurrection, and the gift of the Spirit. And so we return to our original question: How should we observe the Lord’s day?

It is best to celebrate the Lord’s day on the first day of the week, Sunday, as has been the practice from the earliest days of the church. To do so is a significant theological statement: we say that history matters. Celebrating Sunday as the Lord’s day says that we believe that God intervened in history, that the transcendent Lord of the universe intersected with this broken planet on a particular Sunday morning centuries ago. It says that we are part of an unbroken line of descent from another supernatural event, the outpouring of the Holy Spirit, which signified the birthing of the true people of God. We know from personal experience that we don’t need to observe a friend’s birthday on the particular day; but it feels more appropriate to do so. In the same way, it is most appropriate to celebrate God’s greatest acts in history on the day they really happened.

The New Testament communities of faith assembled on the Lord’s day. It was, we may say, a spiritual family gathering, for the purpose of encouraging and prompting people “toward love and good deeds” (Hebrews 10:24). Our practice of “going to church” derives from this assembling of the saints, and thus the church-related gathering is an important part of celebrating the Lord’s day. We will want to make sure that our church gatherings do indeed reflect the New Testament concerns for what can and ought to happen when God’s people gather. (See Article 6, Nature of the Church, and portions on worship in the pastoral application of Article 1, God.)

七、替代性聚会日 / 其他敬拜日子 (Alternative Days)

对于我们大多数人而言，主日提供了一个绝佳的机会去操练圣经所要求的安息（并领受其属天福分）。正因如此，我们在《信仰宣言》中明确宣告：信徒应当将自己的劳作限制于“必要的工作与怜悯之善行”。

然而对于某些人而言——例如从事医疗保健、公共安全等“关键公共服务”的人员——主日休息可能根本无法实现。教会应当鼓励这些肢体寻求其他方式来操练安息的纪律，并勉励他们在自己的职业服事中竭力荣耀耶稣（从而在实质上尊荣主日）。

教牧人员以及其他深度投入主日繁重事工的同工，往往深刻体会到周日绝不是自己的休息日。教会务要确保其受薪与志愿同工能够实践合乎圣经的安息。特别是教会必须确保其牧者能够享有必需的安息休假（包括每周的定期休息日与较长期的安息年休假 Sabbatical），以维持荣耀上帝且身心健康的可持续工作模式。

有些堂会选择在主日以外的其他日子举行主要的教会崇拜聚会。我们该如何评估这种现象？

首先，新约圣经清楚表明，具体的日子并非首要的关键问题（罗 14:5-6），任何一天都可以用来荣耀主。在周日以外举行聚会的动机，通常被解释为“为了向外拓殖外展”与“为了提供灵活性”。

我们建议，明智的教会领袖当在上述两种动机之间进行敏锐的辨识。因为“灵活性”是一把双刃剑，它既能促进也能削弱教会的外展使命。毫无疑问，灵活性确实能够增强外展，它让更多人在更方便的时间来到教会，并且往往提供了不同崇拜风格的选择。

然而，这种所谓的灵活性也会严重损害教会，因为它容易掩盖人对个人时间的占有欲与私心。它可能会让门徒训练中一个至关重要的领域——在时间使用上顺服上帝——被束之高阁、不被触及。如果举行周中聚会仅仅是为了让人可以在一两个小时内“完成聚会任务”，好让他们在周末肆无忌惮地去度假木屋狂欢消遣，那么我们最好认真省察这背后的真实动机与冲动。那种认为“周末是我个人的神圣时间”的心态，暴露出了亟待解决的属灵深层问题——即管家职分（Stewardship）以及向耶稣全然顺服的主权问题。我们同样必须警惕对现代“娱乐与体育之神”的偶像崇拜：对体育运动和消遣娱乐漫无节制的狂热，极易侵蚀并削弱信徒委身聚集于上帝家里的坚定心志。

有些人或许会反驳：“但是，人们来参加周中聚会，难道不比完全不参加聚会更好吗？”答案显而易见：是的。但是一间健康的教会绝不会对这一领域的潜在隐患视而不见——正如教会绝不会漠视那些虽然在周日清晨“恪守宗教仪式”准时参加聚会、却在周一至周六过着放纵不敬虔生活的信徒之属灵光景一样。

Alternative Days

For most of us, Sunday provides a good opportunity to practice the discipline (and receive the blessing) of biblical rest. Because of this, we have chosen to say in our confession that believers limit their labour “to work of necessity and deeds of mercy.” For some, however—those who work in “essential services” such as health care and public safety, for instance—Sunday rest may not be an option. These people ought to be encouraged to find other ways of practicing the discipline of rest, and urged to consider ways they can honour Jesus (and thus the Lord’s day) through their occupational services. Pastors and others who invest heavily in Sunday activities often feel that Sunday is definitely not a day of rest. Churches should ensure that their workers can practice a biblical rest. Churches should make sure that their pastors, especially, are able to take necessary sabbatical rest (both weekly and longer term) in order to maintain a God-honouring and healthy pattern of work. Some congregations hold their main church gatherings on days other than Sunday. How do we evaluate this? First, the New Testament is clear that the day is not of primary significance (Romans 14:5-6). Any day can honour the Lord. The motive for non-Sunday services is generally given as outreach and flexibility. Wise church leadership will, we suggest, distinguish carefully between these two motives, because “flexibility” is a two-edged sword that can both enhance and cripple a church’s outreach. It will enhance it, of course, by allowing more people to come at more convenient times, and often with a choice of the kind of church gathering being offered.

But such flexibility can also cripple a church by masking a possessiveness of one’s time. It can permit a major area for discipleship, submitting one’s time-use to God, to go untouched. If a midweek gathering is used so that people can “get in” their hour or two of church, leaving them free for a weekend at the cottage, we would do well to discern what impulses and motivations lie behind this. An attitude that says “the weekend is my time” tells us that there are some spiritual issues which need addressing, issues of stewardship and submission to Jesus. We ought also to be on guard against idolizing the modern gods of recreation and sports. Unchecked enthusiasm for sports and recreation can weaken commitment to gathering with the family of God.

Some will respond, “But isn’t it better for people to come to a midweek gathering than not go at all?” The answer, obviously, is yes. But the healthy church will not ignore potential concerns in this area—just as it will not ignore the condition of those who might “religiously” attend Sunday morning service but live ungodly lives for the rest of the week.

八、工作与安息的恩赐 (Gifts of Work and Rest)

劳作与安息交替的节律，乃是造物主上帝赐下的宝贵恩赐。工作属于我们神圣呼召（天职）的一部分；安息则是亟待恢复的属灵操练。遵守主日的最佳方式，乃是将这一天分别为圣，作为敬拜、团契相交与服事的时间。律法主义固然会扼杀圣经安息日教导的精神，但上帝关于工作与安息的神圣蓝图，乃是基督徒丰盛、多结果子且可持续生活的不二典范。

Gifts of Work and Rest

The pattern of work and rest is a gift from God the Creator. Work is part of our divine calling or vocation. Rest is a spiritual discipline that needs to be recovered. The Lord’s day is best observed as a time for worship, fellowship, and service. While legalism can destroy the spirit of biblical Sabbath teaching, the divine plan of work and rest is the model for productive and sustainable Christian living.

参考文献 四、参考书目与文献导读
