ARTICLE 17 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 17 条：基督教与其他信仰 (Christianity and Other Faiths)

Article 17: Christianity and Other Faiths

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

耶稣是唯一的道路（Jesus Is the Only Way）

我们坚信，上帝在耶稣里所赐的拯救恩典，是使人类与上帝和好的唯一途径。救恩虽向所有人预备并开放，但唯有那些信靠主耶稣基督的人，才拥有得享永生的确据。

Jesus Is the Only Way

We believe that the saving grace of God in Jesus is the only means of reconciling humanity with God. Although salvation is available to all, only those who put their faith in the Lord Jesus Christ have the assurance of eternal life.

上帝普世的见证（God’s Universal Witness）

上帝从未撇下任何人，使其没有领受关于创造主良善与大能的见证。然而因着人类的叛逆，人们选择压制真理。尽管在其他宗教中可能存在某些真理的元素，但圣经严厉警戒不可听从虚假的教导。基督徒当以尊重的心态对待其他信仰与哲学思想的人，但仍需本着爱心与迫切感，向万民宣扬基督是全人类唯一的救恩之道。

God’s Universal Witness

God has not left anyone without a witness to the Creator’s goodness and power. Due to human rebellion, people have chosen to suppress the truth. While elements of truth may be found in other religions, Scripture warns against false teaching. Christians treat people of other faiths and philosophies with respect, but lovingly and urgently proclaim Christ as the only way of salvation for all peoples.

上帝的主权（Sovereignty of God）

上帝深爱世人，不愿有一人沉沦。在至高的恩典主权中，上帝可能会以超乎人类理解的方式与人沟通。圣经明确教导，那些弃绝福音的人必落在神圣的审判之下；而那些从未听闻福音之人的永恒结局，则全然在上帝的手中。我们的神圣使命是向所有文化中的万民宣扬基督是唯一的得救之路。审判全地的主必行公义。

Sovereignty of God

God loves the world and does not want anyone to perish. In sovereign grace, God may communicate with people in ways that are beyond human comprehension. The Bible teaches that those who reject the gospel are under divine judgment; the eternal destiny of those who have never heard the gospel is in God’s hands. Our task is to proclaim Christ as the only way of salvation to all people in all cultures. The Judge of all the earth will do what is just.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

创世记 18:25；诗篇 19:1-4；传道书 3:11；以赛亚书 46:1-10；55:8-9；以西结书 33:1-20；约拿书 1-4；马太福音 8:5-13；25:31-46；28:18-20；马可福音 7:24-30；路加福音 9:51-56；12:47-48；约翰福音 1:12；3:16, 36；4:8-42；12:12-26；14:6；使徒行传 1:8；4:12；10:1-8, 34-36；14:16-17；17:22-31；罗马书 1:18-24；2:1-16；10:9-21；11:33-35；哥林多前书 3:11；12:3；提摩太前书 2:4-5；彼得后书 3:9；启示录 20:15。

📖 Scripture References

Genesis 18:25; Psalm 19:2-4; Ecclesiastes 3:11; Isaiah 46:1-10; 55:8-9; Ezekiel 33:1-20; Jonah 1-4; Matthew 8:5-13; 25:31-46; 28:18-20; Mark 7:24-30; Luke 9:51-56; 12:47-48; John 1:12; 3:16, 36; 4:8-42; 12:12-26; 14:6; Acts 1:8; 4:12; 10:1-8, 34-36; 14:16-17; 17:22-31; Romans 1:18-24; 2:1-16; 10:9-21; 11:33-35; 1 Corinthians 3:11; 12:3; 1 Timothy 2:4-5; 2 Peter 3:9; Revelation 20:15.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

随着文化与宗教多元化的不断加深，我们所在众多社区的人口结构持续发生变迁，深入思考基督教与其他宗教信仰之关系的相关议题变得尤为迫切。《信仰宣言》第17条正是针对这些核心神学问题所作出的回应。

As growing cultural and religious diversity continues to change the makeup of many of our communities, it is important to consider questions regarding Christianity and other faiths. Article 17 of the Confession of Faith deals with these issues.

一、耶稣是唯一的道路（Jesus is the Only Way）

第17条的第一部分确立了三个核心论点：

  1. 它断然否定了宗教多元主义（pluralistic view of religions），宣告“在耶稣里的救恩，是使人类与上帝和好的唯一途径”；
  2. 它明确指出“救恩向所有人预备并开放”；
  3. 它清晰界定了究竟何种人“拥有得享永生的确据”。

1. 弃绝多元主义（Rejection of Pluralism）

在当代北美文化中，多元主义（pluralism）已成为看待宗教信仰的主导思维范式。多元主义主张：各大主要宗教（甚至可能所有宗教信仰）都是通往上帝的同等有效的道路。然而，圣经极其清晰地拒绝了这种多元主义假说。

耶稣本人的自我认知（self-understanding）表明，唯独他自己才是通往上帝的真正道路。在与撒玛利亚妇人的对话中，耶稣明确将自己视为认识上帝的真正途径，而非拘泥于在撒玛利亚的山上还是在耶路撒冷的圣殿中敬拜（约 4:8-26）。《约翰福音》14章6节更记载了耶稣斩截明了的宣告：“我就是道路、真理、生命；若不藉着我，没有人能到父那里去。”初代教会显然坚定相信耶稣是得救的唯一点（徒 4:12；提前 2:5；来 9:15；12:24）。

将耶稣确立为通往上帝的唯一道路，不仅与旧约圣经中承认以色列为上帝拣选之民的启示一脉相承，更是其终极的成全与应验。在旧约的时代，亲近上帝是通过一个特定的民族——即以色列民；而在新约的恩典中，则是通过一位特定的位格——即耶稣基督。

最后，初代教会的实际行动也构成了对多元主义的断然否定。在古罗马帝国的统治与希腊化文化之下，形形色色的宗教蓬勃兴盛。然而，初代的使徒与教会坚定地呼召无论是犹太人还是外邦异教徒，都要离弃虚妄，归向弥赛亚耶稣，并降服于他的至高主权之下。

2. 救恩向所有人预备（Salvation Available to All）

福音虽然首先临到了犹太民族，但上帝的恩典绝不受限于他们（约 4:22；徒 10）。上帝满怀慈爱地切愿全人类都能蒙恩得救（提前 2:4；彼后 3:9）。新约圣经所描绘的新天新地的终末异象，正是一个来自各邦国、各支派、各民族之人都将共同同在的敬拜共同体（赛 66:18, 22-23）。

3. 永生的确据（Assurance of Eternal Life）

根据《罗马书》10章9节与13节的应许，凡口里认耶稣为主、心里信上帝叫他从死里复活的人，必然得救。因此，他们拥有得享永生的可靠确据。

然而，这些经文并没有在逻辑上绝对断言“唯有那些当下已将信心投注于耶稣的人才可能蒙赐永生”，从而为上帝在主权中将永生恩赐临到其他人保留了神圣的可能性。但是，假若真有其他人蒙赐永生，他们自己对此也是未曾知晓的；因此，他们并不拥有永生的客观确据与主观平安。这一论点自然引申出了一个极其深邃的议题：那些从未听闻福音之人的最终结局如何？关于这一点，本注释将在后文进一步探讨。

Jesus is the Only Way

The first section of Article 17 makes three major points. First, it rejects a pluralistic view of religions by declaring that “Jesus is the only means of reconciling humanity with God.” Second, it notes that “salvation is available to all.” Third, it identifies those people who “have the assurance of eternal life.” Rejection of Pluralism: In North American culture, pluralism has become the dominant way of thinking about religious faith. Pluralism is the view that the major religious faiths (and perhaps all religious faiths) are equally valid paths to God. Scripture is clear in rejecting pluralism.

Jesus’ understanding of himself was that he alone was the path to God. In his conversation with the Samaritan woman, Jesus clearly regarded himself as the way of knowing God, rather than going to the mountain in Samaria or the temple in Jerusalem ( John 4:8-26). John 14:6 records Jesus saying explicitly, “I am the way and the truth and the life. No one comes to the Father except through me.” It is clear that the early church believed Jesus was the only way to salvation (Acts 4:12; 1 Timothy 2:5; Hebrews 9:15; Hebrews 12:24). Regarding Jesus as the only way to God is consistent with and a fulfillment of the Old Testament recognition that Israel is God’s chosen people. Access to God comes through a particular people—the nation of Israel—in the old covenant, and through a particular person—Jesus Christ—in the new covenant. Finally, the practice of the early church is a clear rejection of pluralism. A variety of religions flourished under Roman rule and culture. The church called both Jews and pagans to turn to Jesus the Messiah and submit to his lordship.

Salvation Available to All: While the gospel came to the Jewish people first, it is not limited to them ( John 4:22; Acts 10). God actively desires that all people be saved (1 Timothy 2:4; 2 Peter 3:9). The vision of the new heaven and the new earth is one in which people from every nation will be present (Isaiah 66:18, 22-23). Assurance of Eternal Life: It is clear from Romans 10:9 and 13 that those who place their faith in Jesus will be saved. Thus they have the assurance of eternal life. However, these verses do not say that only those who put their faith in Jesus will be saved, allowing for the possibility that others may be granted eternal life. If others are granted eternal life, they are unaware of it. Thus they do not have the assurance of eternal life. This suggests the issue of the fate of those who have not heard the gospel. That will be addressed in a later section of this commentary.

二、上帝普世的见证（God’s Universal Witness）

第17条的第二部分深入探讨了两个紧密相关的维度：其他宗教的思想内容，以及信奉其他宗教的人本身。

1. 其他宗教的内容（The Content of Other Religions）

上帝在外在藉着受造的浩瀚宇宙向所有人彰显自己（诗 19:1；徒 14:17；罗 1:18-20），在内在则藉着道德良知（罗 2:15）以及人心中对神圣与永恒的直觉意识（传 3:11）向全人类显明。

尽管上帝如此普遍地显明他自己，但并非所有人都能自觉而清晰地认识上帝。罪恶的全面败坏使得许多人的心眼蒙蔽，对上帝在自然界与良心中的启示视而不见、颠倒黑白（罗 1:18-23）。即便如此，当使徒保罗身处雅典时，他敏锐地察觉到那些敬拜偶像之人（徒 17:16, 23）以及哲学学派信徒（徒 17:18）内心对独一真活上帝的某种摸索与寻求，甚至承认即便在异教文化与诗词中，也可能蕴含着对上帝某种初步、原始的认知碎片（徒 17:28）。

依照保罗的神学论述，人类藉由受造世界与内在良知所获得的关于上帝的知识，已经足够确立人的道德责任，以至于世人在未能承认并敬拜独一真活上帝时“无可推诿”（罗 1:20；2:14-15）。从理论原理上讲，人若能完全依照自己所领受的律法行事为人，这种对上帝的认知本可导向救恩（罗 2:6-8, 14-16）。然而，保罗极其严正地指出，现实中世人都犯了罪，亏缺了上帝的荣耀，在公义的上帝面前全属有罪，绝无一人能靠行律法称义（罗 3:20, 23）。

2. 信奉其他宗教的人（Persons Who Profess Other Religions）

耶稣的门徒蒙召以舍己的爱去对待所有人，甚至包括自己的仇敌（太 5:44；22:39）。然而，当今世俗的多元文化往往将“爱与尊重”曲解为“禁止表达任何分歧与反对意见”。

与此相反，耶稣基督的十字架福音往往是绊脚的、带有尖锐挑战性的（林前 1:23）。福音要求人献上终极无保留的绝对忠诚，弃绝过往所有与之竞争的效忠对象（可 10:29-30）；福音正如一柄“两刃的利剑”，必然会在听闻福音之人的心灵深处引发剧烈的道德与属灵震荡（来 4:12）。因此，基督徒所持守的真爱与尊重，绝不意味着我们在传讲福音时必须妥协真理、刻意回避分歧，或是消除福音原本具有的颠覆性。

然而，正如保罗在雅典亚略巴古讲道时所树立的典范，我们应当竭力去寻找人们既有观念与耶稣基督福音之间的“契合点/接触点”（points of contact，徒 17:16-34）。英国著名神学家阿利斯特·麦格拉思（Alister McGrath）鼓励基督徒主动在非信徒的思想世界中寻找这些接触点。所谓“接触点”，是指一个人既有世界观中与基督教世界观产生共鸣或重合的元素。著名宣教家唐·理查森（Don Richardson）在其经典著作《和平之子》（Peace Child）与《众心深处的永恒》（Eternity in Their Hearts）中，生动展示了在极其多样的原始与异教文化中发现神圣接触点的震撼范例。基督徒若能敏锐识别这些契合点，就能以此作为桥梁，诚挚邀请非信徒进一步深思并接受福音更完全的核心真理。

God’s Universal Witness

The second section of Article 17 treats two issues: the content of other religions and the persons who profess other religions.

The Content of Other Religions: God is made known outwardly to all people through creation (Psalm 19:1; Acts 14:17; Romans 1:18-20) and inwardly to all people through a moral conscience (Romans 2:15) and a sense of the divine (Ecclesiastes 3:11). Though God makes Himself known, not all people are consciously aware of God. The corruption of sin blinds many to God as revealed in creation and conscience (Romans 1:18-23). Still, at Athens, Paul recognized a searching for God on the part of followers of idols (Acts 17:16, 23) and philosophical systems (Acts 17:18) and allowed that there may have been an elementary knowledge of God even in pagan religions (Acts 17:28).

According to Paul, the knowledge of God that is acquired through creation and conscience is sufficient for people to be “without excuse” for failing to acknowledge God (Romans 1:20; 2:14-15). It appears that this knowledge of God can, in principle, lead to salvation, if people live according to the law they have (Romans 2:6-8, 14-16). Paul is clear, however, that all people have sinned against God and stand guilty before God (Romans 3:20, 23).

Persons Who Profess Other Religions: Disciples of Jesus are called to treat others, including enemies, with love (Matthew 5:44; 22:39). The pluralistic culture often interprets love and respect as prohibiting disagreement. The gospel of Jesus Christ, however, is often offensive (1 Corinthians 1:23). It demands one’s ultimate loyalty, rejecting all previous loyalties (Mark 10:29-30) and is a “two-edged sword” which may cause internal turmoil for the person hearing the gospel (Hebrews 4:12). So love and respect do not demand that we refrain from disagreement or potential offence as we present the gospel. However, as Paul did in Athens, we must try to identify points of contact between what people already believe and the gospel of Jesus (Acts 17:16-34). British theologian Alister McGrath invites Christians to look for these points of contact with non-believers. Points of contact are those elements of a person’s worldview that coincide with a Christian worldview. Don Richardson, in such books as Peace Child and Eternity in Their Hearts, provides fascinating examples of points of contact with a variety of different cultures. If the believer can identify some points of contact, these can provide a way of inviting a nonbeliever to consider other elements of the gospel.

三、上帝的主权（Sovereignty of God）

第17条的第三部分聚焦于神学与牧养中最具挑战性的两个核心议题：弃绝福音者的结局，以及从未听闻福音者的结局。

1. 弃绝福音的人（Those Who Reject the Gospel）

圣经毫不含糊地指出，那些在真理光照下断然拒绝耶稣福音的人，必落在上帝公义的定罪与审判之下（约 3:18；约壹 2:23）。上帝的神圣定罪意味着与上帝的荣美与恩典永远隔绝（帖后 1:9），这在圣经启示中被称为“第二次的死”（启 2:11；20:6）。

2. 未曾听闻福音的人（Those Who Have Never Heard）

这一议题引发了一个极其艰难而沉重的问题：那些一生之中从未有机会听闻福音的人，有可能得救吗？

如前所述，在抽象的法则上，人若能完全行出公义的行为本有可能获救，但事实上全人类都被圈在罪中，绝无一人能藉此得救。因此，那些从未听闻福音的人若有可能蒙恩得救，那必定唯独是因信得救。如前文论证，人从受造界或良心所领受的上帝知识，足以使人在上帝面前无可推诿；但关键问题在于：一个人若仅凭对受造界或良心中所彰显之上帝所作出的信心回应，是否足以构成得救的信心？

为了使我们在这一极其敏感的议题上的神学思考条理化，让我们仔细审视以下两个命题陈述：

  • 命题（A）：耶稣基督的生、死与复活，为人类的救恩提供了唯一的客观可能性。
  • 命题（B）：一个人必须对耶稣基督的生、死与复活具有明确的理性认知，并必须明确地将信心投向耶稣，才能得救。

这两个命题乍看之下似乎只是同义反复，但在神学实质上却存在着至关重要的分水岭：

  • 命题（A）描述的是使拯救在历史上客观成为可能的基石事件；
  • 命题（B）界定的则是一个个体为了主观支取救恩所必须具备的认知与信靠条件。

关于未曾听闻福音者的终极命运，当代神学主要形成了三种截然不同的立场：

  1. 多元主义（Pluralism）：认为命题（A）与命题（B）皆为虚妄。
  2. 包容主义/包容论（Inclusivism）：认为命题（A）为真，但命题（B）为假。
  3. 排他主义/排他论（Exclusivism）：认为命题（A）与命题（B）皆为真。
  • 多元主义认为通往上帝的途径多种多样，彼此同等有效。如前所述，门诺弟兄会根据圣经全面拒绝多元主义。
  • 包容主义者坚持命题（A）为真，而命题（B）为假。换言之，凡最终得救的人，耶稣基督的受死与复活是其蒙拯救的唯一根源与基石；然而，一个人完全有可能蒙了耶稣基督的拯救，却在生前未曾意识到施行拯救的正是耶稣。

包容主义促使我们重温神学上关于普遍启示（general revelation）与特殊启示（special revelation）的关键区分。普遍启示是上帝藉着大自然的创造与人类良心向全人类普遍展示的知识；特殊启示则是上帝透过历史中的耶稣基督并圣经启示之言所赐下的直接救赎性启示。普天下所有人都领受了普遍启示，但许多人一生未曾得见特殊启示。包容主义者认为，那些从未听闻耶稣之名的人，如果真诚根据藉着普遍启示所获得的上帝认知而悔改信靠真神，他们就有可能得蒙拯救。然而，若他们得救，依然完全且唯独是因着耶稣在加略山十字架上所成就的救赎功劳。

  • 排他主义者则坚称命题（A）与命题（B）皆为绝对真理。换言之，基督的十字架与复活是唯一的救恩之源，且一个人为了得救，必须在意识中明确承认耶稣是主和救主。如果一个人从未听闻耶稣，他就断然无法得救，因为他不可能将明确的信心投注于一位他未曾知晓的基督身上。单纯的普遍启示绝不足以使人得救，特殊启示是得救不可或缺的绝对前提。

既然包容主义与排他主义都坚定认同命题（A）为真——即救恩唯独藉着耶稣才有可能——那么实质分歧就在于命题（B）是否成立：即是否唯有口头明确信靠耶稣之名才能得救？

圣经中确实有一些经文似乎强烈支持排他主义，表明对耶稣受死与复活的明确认知是得救的前提。例如《约翰福音》3章18节宣告：“……不信的人，罪已经定了，因为他不信神独生子的名。”同样，《约翰一书》5章12节严明指出：“人有了神的儿子就有生命，没有神的儿子就没有生命。”

然而，有些经常被排他主义者引用的经文（如罗马书 10:9 与 使徒行传 4:12），经过严格审视后，并不必然构成对命题（B）的绝对证明。如前所述，《罗马书》10:9 与《使徒行传》4:12 明确宣告的是：凡信靠耶稣的人必能得救；但经文的句法逻辑并没有封闭性地断言：唯独那些在历史中已明确信靠耶稣名字的人才可能蒙上帝施恩拯救。

另一方面，圣经中亦存在一些似乎与严格排他主义张力颇大的经文。例如《提摩太前书》4章10节宣告：“我们劳苦努力，正是为此，因为我们的指望在乎永生的神；他是万人的救主，更是信徒的救主。”这节经文意味深长地启示：上帝的拯救恩典有可能以超乎寻常的方式临到那些未曾明确信靠耶稣的人。

此外，旧约时期的以色列列祖以及《希伯来书》11章所列举的庞大信心伟人云彩，他们在世时显然都未曾拥有关于耶稣生平、十字架受死与肉身复活的明确历史知识。在本质上，今天那些从未有机会听闻福音之人的处境，与基督道成肉身之前的人们是完全等同的。在旧约时代，人们完全有可能藉着当时所赐下的有限启示，向上帝投诚坚定的信心。甚至在旧约中，某些被神圣接纳的真敬拜者（例如麦基洗德，创 14:17-20；叶忒罗，出 3:1）根本就不在亚伯拉罕肉身支派的圣约血脉之内！同理，今天那些尚未听闻耶稣福音的人当中，亦可能有人凭借仅有的启示对至高上帝存有真纯的敬畏与信心。我们人类绝无权柄能够穷尽上帝将他救赎恩典临到世人的一切神圣方式（赛 55:8-9）。

因此，《信仰宣言》第17条展现出了深邃的神学智慧：它既未草率地全面为包容主义背书，也未教条式地断然倒向排他主义。因为圣经本身并未对未听闻福音者的终极命运给出机械封闭的定论。诚如亚伯拉罕在为所多玛的命运向上帝恳求时所作出的庄严宣告：“审判全地的主岂不行公义吗？”（创 18:25）上帝必以他至高、圣洁与绝对公义的标准施行裁决。

Sovereignty of God

The third section of Article 17 addresses two issues: the fate of those who reject the gospel and the fate of those who have never heard the gospel. Those Who Reject the Gospel: The Bible is clear that those who reject the gospel of Jesus are condemned by God ( John 3:18; 1 John 2:23). Condemnation by God is eternal separation from God (2 Thessalonians 1:9) and is a “second death” (Revelation 2:11; Revelation 20:6).

Those Who Have Never Heard: This topic involves a very difficult question. Is it possible for those who have never heard the gospel to be saved? As noted earlier, it is in principle possible to be saved by works of righteousness, but no one will in fact be saved that way since all have sinned. If salvation is possible for those who have never heard the gospel, it will be salvation by faith. As noted earlier, knowledge of God from creation or conscience is sufficient for accountability to God. Is having faith in God as known only through response to creation or conscience sufficient for a saving faith?

To bring some order to our thinking about this issue, let us consider two statements: (A) The life, death, and resurrection of Jesus provides the only possibility of salvation for human beings.

(B) A person must have explicit knowledge of the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus and must explicitly put his or her faith in Jesus in order to be saved. These statements may seem to be two ways of saying the same thing, but there is a significant difference between them. Statement (A) describes the event that makes salvation possible. Statement (B) tells what a person must believe in order to receive salvation.

Consider three views on the fate of the unevangelized. Pluralism holds that both (A) and (B) are false. Inclusivism holds that (A) is true but (B) is false. Exclusivism holds that both (A) and (B) are true.

Pluralism holds that there are many ways to God, all equally valid. As noted earlier, Mennonite Brethren reject pluralism.

The inclusivist holds that (A) is true but (B) is false. That is, the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus are the source of the salvation for anyone who is saved. However, it is possible for someone to be saved by Jesus without realizing it is Jesus who is saving them.

Inclusivism reminds us of the distinction between general revelation and special revelation. General revelation is the information about God that is available to all through observation of creation. Special revelation is the direct revelation of God through Jesus and through the words of Scripture. Everyone receives general revelation. Some people do not receive special revelation. According to the inclusivist, people who have never heard of Jesus may be saved if they respond appropriately to the knowledge of God that they have gained through general revelation. Nonetheless, if they are saved, they are saved because of what Jesus did on the cross.

The exclusivist says that both (A) and (B) are true. That is, the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus are the source of salvation, and in order to be saved a person must explicitly acknowledge that Jesus is the Saviour. If a people have never heard of Jesus, they cannot be saved because they cannot explicitly put their trust in Jesus. General revelation alone cannot be sufficient for salvation. Special revelation is necessary.

Since inclusivism and exclusivism agree that (A) is true, that salvation is only possible through Jesus, the question is whether or not (B) is true, that only confessed faith in Jesus can save. Some texts seem to support exclusivism by suggesting that explicit knowledge of Jesus’ life, death, and resurrection is necessary for salvation. John 3:18, for instance, says, “...those who do not believe are condemned already, because they have not believed in the name of the only Son of God” (NRSV). Similarly, 1 John 5:12 says, “Whoever has the Son has life; whoever does not have the Son of God does not have life” (NRSV).

Some texts, such as Romans 10:9 and Acts 4:12, are often used to uphold exclusivism but do not really support it. As noted earlier, Romans 10:9 and Acts 4:12 say that all who believe in Jesus will be saved; they do not say that only those who explicitly believe in Jesus will be saved.

On the other hand, some texts seem inconsistent with exclusivism. First Timothy 4:10 says, “For to this end we toil and struggle, because we have our hope set on the living God, who is the Saviour of all people, especially of those who believe” (NRSV). This suggests that some might be saved without explicitly believing in Jesus. Furthermore, the Old Testament patriarchs and others mentioned in Hebrews 11 did not have explicit knowledge of the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus. Similarly, people who have never heard the gospel are for all practical purposes in the same position as the people prior to the advent of Christ. It was possible for them to put their faith in God through the revelation given them. Some such believers, such as Melchizedek (Genesis 14:17-20) and Jethro (Exodus 3:1), were outside of the line of Abraham. In the same way, some who have not heard the gospel of Jesus may nonetheless have faith in God. We are not privileged to know all of the ways that God makes His saving grace available to human beings (Isaiah 55:8-9).

Article 17 wisely does not endorse either inclusivism or exclusivism since there is not a conclusive answer to the question of the fate of the unevangelized. As recorded in Abraham’s conversation with God regarding the fate of Sodom, God will judge justly (Genesis 18:25).

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

随着我们教会周边社区在宗教信仰上日益多元化，北美的牧者与教会领袖越来越频繁地面临关于基督教与其他信仰关系的现实拷问。在牧养视角中，描述当今北美信仰多元现象的一个极为贴切的词汇是“宗教多元现状”（Religious Plurality）。这一概念与“宗教多元主义”（Religious Pluralism）有着本质区别：后者是一种断言“所有宗教都是通往上帝的等效途径”的世俗哲学主张（参见前文第17条注释）。

With increasing frequency, pastors and church leaders in North America face questions about the relationship of Christianity to other faiths, as the communities surrounding our churches become more religiously diverse. A useful way to describe the growing diversity of faith commitments in North America is “religious plurality.” This is somewhat different than “religious pluralism,” a term often used to state a philosophical position which considers all faiths to be equal paths to God. (See Article 17 Commentary.)

一、宣教的驱动力（Motivation for Mission）

虽然门诺弟兄会坚决拒绝宗教多元主义哲学，但第17条却以极其积极和充满信心的姿态面对“宗教多元现状”的现实：它明确宣告“救恩向所有人预备并开放”。换句话说，我们在新约圣经中所领受的耶稣基督的福音好消息，对于那些委身于其他宗教信仰的人们而言，同样是至高无上的大好信息！上帝藉着耶稣基督之死为全人类所开辟的救恩大道，同样向所有其他信仰的人敞开。这种神学进路非但没有削弱宣教热情，反而强烈激励信徒更加坚定地向世人宣认：耶稣是唯一的道路。

宣言中“得享永生的确据”这一核心表述，指明了基督徒有幸能与其他信仰群体分享的救恩至美维度之一。圣经极其清晰地见证了这种唯独藉着福音而来的活泼盼望。但这仅仅是新约圣经中所启示的浩瀚救恩汪洋中的一泓清泉。在传讲福音时，我们同样应当大力宣扬：基督在十字架与复活中彻底击溃了邪恶、罪恶与死亡的权势；基督将人从仇敌魔鬼沉重的压制辖制中完全释放出来；以及耶稣应许赐给所有甘愿舍己跟从他的门徒丰盛而充满荣耀的新生命。

牧者与教会领袖应当在讲台宣道与门徒培训中，竭力传讲那些曾激励初代基督徒彻底翻转其时代的基石真理，以此不断激发全教会的宣教热忱。宣教的终极动力，源于我们渴望全地万民都认识上帝在世界中的至高主权、上帝对普世世人的深切大爱，以及上帝“不愿有一人沉沦，乃愿人人都悔改”的圣洁心肠。第17条条文后所列举的丰富圣经经卷，为牧者提供了极佳的讲道经文库与属灵武器。

本条信条发出了铿锵有力的行动呼召：在第二部分的结语中明确指出，基督徒的真正道路在于“本着爱心与迫切感，向万民宣扬基督是唯一的救赎之道”；同样，在第三部分的末尾，宣言将向万国万民传扬基督福音庄严定性为“我们不可推卸的神圣职责与使命”（our task）。

Motivation for Mission

While we do not agree with religious pluralism, Article 17 approaches the reality of religious plurality in a positive way: it states that “salvation is available to all.” In other words, the good news of Jesus Christ which we know from the New Testament is also good news for people who are committed to other faiths. The way of salvation which God opened for us through the death of Jesus is open to people of other faiths as well. This approach encourages believers to strongly affirm Jesus as the Only Way. The phrase “the assurance of eternal life” indicates one of the dimensions of salvation which Christians have the privilege of sharing with people of other faiths. Scripture speaks clearly to this hope, which comes through the gospel alone. But this is just one of many aspects of salvation in the New Testament which could be highlighted. One could also speak of the defeat of the powers of evil, sin, and death, liberation from the oppressive power of the enemy, and the abundant life that Jesus promises to those who follow him. Pastors and church leaders can help congregations strengthen their motivation for mission by providing preaching and teaching on the truths which propelled the first Christians to evangelize their world. Mission is motivated by our desire to proclaim God’s sovereign authority in the world, God’s love for the world, and God’s desire that no one would perish but all come to repentance. The Scripture passages listed in support of Article 17 suggest good starting points for such encouragement.

The article sounds a definite call to action, stating at the end of the second section that the Christian way is to “lovingly and urgently proclaim Christ as the only way of salvation for all peoples.” Similarly, the end of the third section describes proclamation of the gospel among all peoples as “our task.”

二、生活在其他信仰群体之中（Living Among People of Other Faiths）

起草第17条的部分初衷，固然是为了应对北美社会的人口结构变迁——越来越多的世界各宗教信奉者正搬入我们教会周围的社区。然而我们必须意识到，全球其他地区的门诺弟兄会教会（例如印度与刚果），从其诞生之初就是在一个异教林立、充满信仰竞争的环境中被建立起来的。在各自的社会中，他们世代作为少数群体而生活、受苦与见证。这些海外教会的许多属灵先辈与当代信徒，早已对如何面对其他宗教信仰进行了极为深刻的神学生死反思；他们完全有资格、也有能力成为北美门诺弟兄会弟兄姊妹在面对多元信仰处境时的良师益友。

圣经为我们面对其他信仰群体提供了无比丰饶的启示资源。整部新约圣经的成书背景，正是初代基督徒在整个罗马帝国中作为微不足道的边缘极少数群体，在各色异教神祇竞争环伺的处境中写成的。福音书中所记录的关于耶稣的独一见证、保罗书信中字字千钧的基督论信经宣告、以及《使徒行传》中第一代信徒在异教狂澜中所展现的无畏宣教，全都是在多元宗教的烈火氛围中淬炼而成的。因此，所有这些圣经叙事，对于今天北美及全球教会所面临的处境，都具有极为直接、震撼的现实指导意义。

在教牧讲道与信徒造就中：

  • 《腓立比书》2:5-11 为宣讲耶稣基督的绝对神性、谦卑道成肉身与被上帝升为至高的荣耀，奠定了坚如磐石的根基；
  • 《歌罗西书》1:15-20 是宣扬基督的先在性（pre-existence）、万有之上的至高无上性（supremacy）以及他在创造与救赎中核心枢纽地位的绝佳讲台篇章；
  • 《希伯来书》1:1-4 庄严宣告了在上帝向人类说话的历史进程中，耶稣基督具有终极、不可替代的核心地位；
  • 《罗马书》10:9-13 斩截有力地宣告耶稣是全人类独一无二的主；
  • 《约翰福音》则从始至终回荡着上帝藉其独生子耶稣基督赐下唯一拯救途径的绝对真理。

Living Among People of Other Faiths

Article 17 was drafted partly in response to demographic developments in North America, where people of many religions are moving into the communities surrounding our churches. It is useful to note, however, that Mennonite Brethren churches in other parts of the world, such as India and Congo, emerged in the midst of competing faiths. They continue to function as minorities in their societies. Many in these churches have already thought through important questions about other faiths, and may be in a position to help their MB sisters and brothers in North America.

The Bible offers many helpful resources for questions surrounding people of other faiths. The New Testament was written at a time when the Christian population was a tiny minority in the midst of competing faiths. The witness to Jesus recorded in the Gospels, the Christological confessions in the letters of Paul, the bold evangelism of the first Christians recorded in Acts all took place in a multi-faith atmosphere. All of these biblical stories thus become very relevant for the situation which is emerging in North America. For example, Philippians 2:5-11 provides a solid foundation for preaching the divinity, incarnation, and exaltation of Jesus. Similarly, Colossians 1:15-20 is a great launching pad for preaching Jesus’ pre-existence, supremacy, and role in creation. Hebrews 1:1-4 proclaims the centrality of Jesus in the way God has spoken to the world, and Romans 10:9-13 makes unmistakably clear that Jesus is Lord of all people. The Gospel of John announces the truth of God’s one way of salvation through his son Jesus Christ.

三、警惕非圣经视角的应对方式（Non-Biblical Approaches）

面对其他信仰群体的议题之所以日益迫切，另一个严峻的原因在于：部分基督徒正日益受到非圣经的世俗应对思路的诱惑。

当代一种极为流行的世俗解决方案，是将基督教的仁爱歪曲为一种毫无真理原则的“宽容”（tolerance）概念。这种观点宣称：基督徒若要善待其他信仰的人，就必须承认所有宗教在本质上都是平等的。这种世俗思维试图通过主张“所有宗教皆为真、无一为假”来抚平宗教冲突。然而在这种非基督教的折衷主义视角中，耶稣基督被无情地贬低为人类历史上众多救主当中的区区一位。

这与整部新约圣经的见证彻底背道而驰！新约圣经的核心教导正是：耶稣是万有之主，除他以外别无救主（参见参考书目中约翰·埃·托斯 John E. Toews 所撰写的重要论文）。接受这种世俗多元主义方案的致命恶果，在于它最终必将导向对主耶稣基督之荣耀与主权的背叛与弃绝。

当教会内部开始有人选择世俗非圣经观点、背弃新约圣经对基督的清晰见证时，教会领袖必须敲响震耳欲聋的警钟。教牧领袖面临的重大牧养挑战在于：如何切实引导我们的会众以发自肺腑的爱心与慷慨宽广的胸怀去拥抱非基督徒朋友，同时却能毫不妥协、坚如磐石地持守关于耶稣基督的独一真理！

Non-Biblical Approaches

Another reason that the question of other faiths has become more urgent is that some Christians are attracted to non-biblical approaches to religious plurality. One popular solution is an idea of “tolerance” which says that to be kind to people of other faiths, Christians need to say that all religions are equal. This secular notion copes with religious diversity by stating that all religions are true and none false. In this non-Christian view, Jesus becomes only one saviour among many.

But this runs counter to the witness of Scripture. The central teaching of the New Testament is that Jesus is Lord of all, and that there is no other Lord. (See article by John E. Toews listed in the bibliography.) The danger in accepting secular solutions to religious plurality is that they can lead to denial of the Lord Jesus Christ.

When Christians begin to choose non-biblical views over the New Testament witness to Jesus, there is cause for alarm. The challenge for church leaders is to help our congregations think of their non-Christian friends with love and generosity, while still holding firmly to the truth about Jesus.

四、对待其他宗教的态度（Attitudes Toward Other Faiths）

第17条在探讨非基督教信仰体系中是否存在真理这一问题时，采取了极为严谨审慎的态度。然而宣言明确肯定：“上帝从未撇下任何人，使其没有领受关于创造主良善与大能的见证”（参见 罗 1:20），并将《使徒行传》第10章（彼得与哥尼流）和第17章（保罗在雅典亚略巴古）确立为深入反思这一课题的核心圣经依据。

为了使全教会在这一议题上获得更为广阔的属灵视野，一个极具价值的教牧建议是：主动向那些亲身经历过非基督教世界宗教处境的门诺弟兄会跨文化同工与学者请教。教会可以邀请来自非洲和亚洲门诺弟兄会教会的资深讲员，或是邀请那些长期深入研究其他宗教、长期生活在异教群体之中的宣教士，来到地方教会与会众进行深度的交流与座谈。

Attitudes Toward Other Faiths

Article 17 takes a cautious approach to the question of truth in non-Christian faiths. But it affirms that “God has not left anyone without a witness to the Creator’s goodness and power” (see Romans 1:20), and lists Acts 10 (Peter and Cornelius) and Acts 17 (Paul in Athens) as helpful passages for this question.

A good idea for shining further light on this question is to consult Mennonite Brethren who have experienced world religions first-hand. Invite speakers from African and Asian MB churches, or missionaries who have studied other faiths and lived among people of other faiths, to interact with your congregations.

五、尊重其他信仰的人士（Respecting People of Other Faiths）

然而，第17条在谈及基督徒对待其他信仰人士的实际态度时，表达得极其直截了当。信条明确指示：我们要以发自内心的“尊重”（respect）相待。这一立场为我们在日常生活与社区见证中展现尊重与友善，开辟了三条切实可行的实践途径：

1. 展现跨文化的邻舍之爱（Cross-cultural neighbourliness）

与其他信仰人士之间的文化差异，不仅不是阻碍，反而能够成为建立深厚人际关系的良性契合点。门诺弟兄会本身就是一个拥有深厚跨文化背景的全球属灵大家庭（global fellowship）。对我们而言，文化的多元丰富绝非令人头疼的障碍，相反，它是我们应当由衷肯定、拥抱与欢庆的上帝恩赐。当我们真诚欣赏并尊重邻舍的文化背景时，就为日后探讨彼此可能存在深刻分歧的属灵与永恒议题，奠定了坚固的信任与友谊基石。

2. 在基层处境中展开跨信仰对话（Interfaith conversation at the local level）

在真诚友善的邻舍关系中，基督徒应当坦荡地向他人宣认自己的信仰，同时更要心存谦卑、极其专注地聆听他人对自己信仰的真实描述。这两种姿态都是对其他信仰人士表达真正尊重的体现。

在跨信仰交往中，基督徒必须竭力避免将其他信仰简单化、刻板印象化或恶意丑化；相反，我们当学会从信徒本人的亲身生命体验中去切实理解他们的信仰内涵。事实上，许多其他信仰的朋友非常乐见基督徒展现出对自己信仰的坚定确信，并且由衷敬重那些能够毫不犹豫、坦诚分享基督之爱的人。

3. 把握在公共道德与社会议题上携手合作的契机（Cooperation on social issues）

在公共道德与伦理领域，基督徒与其他信仰人士往往发现彼此站在相同的立场上。许多其他信仰的人士与我们共同持守一个至关重要的世界观基石：人类生命乃是活在一位赐下道德法则的创造主上帝面前，人必须向造物主负责。

这种共同的道德认知，往往会在诸如保护未出生胎儿生命（反堕胎）、捍卫婚姻与性道德纯洁、以及关怀孩童道德教育等关键公共议题上，促成高度的价值共识。在这些关乎社会良心与公共福祉的议题上与其他信仰人士携手并肩、共同发声，正是基督徒在公共广场上向邻舍表达尊重的极具建设性的实践途径。

Respecting People of Other Faiths

Article 17 is straightforward, however, on the Christian attitude toward people of other faiths. It says that we are to treat them with respect. This opens the way for many practical expressions of respect and friendliness. First, we can express cross-cultural neighbourliness. Cultural differences with people of other faiths can provide contact points for building good relationships. Mennonite Brethren are a culturally diverse, global fellowship. This cultural diversity is not something which poses a problem for us. Rather, it is something we can affirm and celebrate. As we show appreciation for the cultures of others, we build a strong foundation for discussing spiritual issues where there may be disagreement.

Second, we can engage in interfaith conversation at the local level. In the context of friendly relationships, Christians should confess their faith to others and listen carefully to how others describe their faith. Both of these actions show respect to people of other faiths. Avoid stereotyping or caricaturing other faiths. Rather, learn to know other faiths as their practitioners themselves experience them. Many people of other faiths are grateful when Christians show a strong faith of their own and do not hesitate to speak openly of it.

Third, we can take opportunities to cooperate on social issues. Christians and people of other faiths frequently find themselves on the same side in public issues of morality. Many people of other faiths share the very important perception that human life is lived in response to a Creator God who gives humans laws for their behaviour. This can lead to similar views on such issues as abortion, sexual morality, and the education of children. Standing together with people of other faiths on these social issues is another way that Christians can treat others with respect.

六、谦卑信靠神圣的主权与公义（Trusting in God）

在我们各地方教会的会众当中，不时会有弟兄姊妹为那些一生未曾听闻耶稣基督福音之人的永恒命运而深感困惑、焦虑甚至痛苦挣扎。

面对这一困扰千年的神学难题，《信仰宣言》第17条并没有给出廉价、教条式的武断解答，而是极其谦卑敬虔地将这一奥秘“完全交托在上帝的手中”。它引导我们信靠：那位全智全能的神圣上帝必行绝对美善与公义的事。

正如条文所指引的，面对浩瀚奥秘的上帝作为，我们有限受造的人类应当在神学知性上保持高度的谦逊与敬畏（modesty）。然而，基督徒绝不能因奥秘而陷入行动的瘫痪，我们必须全力基于圣经中已经清晰显明的心意而刚强前行！

教会坚不可摧的宣教驱动力，建立在深刻的属灵确信之上：关于耶稣基督的福音，正是上帝拯救一切相信之人的浩大大能（罗 1:16）；宣教的磐石基柱，在于我们向诸天全地所作出的至高宣信——耶稣基督是全宇宙独一无二的至高之主！宣教更是我们在蒙受了上帝藉其独生爱子受死所成就的浩瀚救恩之后，满心涌流出的炽热感恩与颂赞；是我们热切渴望自己所领受的丰盛属灵福分，也能普惠临到全地所有其他信仰群体的宏大胸怀！

Trusting in God

Some worshippers in our congregations struggle with questions about the eternal destiny of those who have not heard the gospel of Jesus Christ. Article 17 does not give an answer to that question, but leaves it “in God’s hands.” It counsels trust that God will do what is right.

As the article suggests, it is good to maintain a measure of modesty concerning how much we humans know about God’s ways in these matters. However, Christians should act upon what can be clearly known from Scripture. Strong motivation for mission is based on the deep conviction that the good news about Jesus is the power of God for the salvation of everyone who believes. Its foundation is the confession that Jesus Christ is Lord of the universe. It is a response of gratitude to God for saving us through the death of His Son, and a desire that the blessings we enjoy should reach people of other faiths as well.

ARTICLE 18 门诺弟兄会信仰宣言 · 中英双语对照典藏本

第 18 条：基督最终的得胜 (Christ's Final Triumph)

Article 18: Christ’s Final Triumph

信条条文 一、信仰宣言条文
Confession of Faith The Confession of Faith
🇨🇳 中文信条条文
🇬🇧 Original Confession Text

核心宣告

我们相信，我们的主耶稣基督将在今世的末了带着荣耀得胜并肉眼可见地再临。教会必须时刻预备迎见主，存着迫切的期盼等候祂随时临近的再来。

Christ’s Final Triumph

We believe that our Lord Jesus Christ will return visibly and triumphantly at the end of the present age. The church must always be prepared to meet the Lord, living in expectation of his imminent return.

末世（The Last Days）

在基督初临与再临之间的这末世时期，教会在世上履行其使命。信徒常因见证基督而忍受苦难与逼迫。尽管面临邪恶势力的敌挡，教会坚信基督国度必获最终得胜。这末后的世代将随着基督的再临而告终。

The Last Days

In these last days, between the first and second coming of Christ, the church carries out its mission in the world. Believers often endure suffering and persecution because of their witness to Christ. In spite of opposition by evil powers, the church is assured of the final victory of Christ’s kingdom. These last days come to an end with Christ’s return.

死亡（Death）

因基督已借着祂的复活粉碎了死亡的权势，信徒便毋需惧怕死亡这最后的仇敌。基督的追随者离世后便去与主同在。当基督再临之时，他们将要复活并领受新的身体。在基督降临时仍活着的信徒将要改变，同样承受全新而荣耀的身体，得享上帝永恒国度中的生命。

Death

Since Christ destroyed the power of death by his resurrection, believers need not be afraid of death, the last enemy. Christ’s followers go to be with the Lord when they die. When Christ returns, they will be raised and receive new bodies. Believers who are alive at Christ’s coming will be transformed and will also receive new and glorious bodies, fit for life in God’s eternal kingdom.

审判（Judgment）

当基督再临时，祂将摧毁一切邪恶权势，包括敌基督。撒但以及所有拒绝基督之人，都将被定罪落入地狱承受永远的刑罚，永远与上帝的同在隔绝。信徒则必须显现在基督的审判台前，其一生的生命将受鉴察，其劳苦将得赏赐。因着上帝的恩典，他们将进入上帝永恒统治的喜乐之中。

Judgment

When Christ returns, he will destroy all evil powers, including the antichrist. Satan and all those who have rejected Christ will be condemned to eternal punishment in hell, forever separated from the presence of God. Believers must appear before the judgment seat of Christ to have their lives examined and their labours rewarded. By God’s grace, they will enter into the joy of God’s eternal reign.

新造（The New Creation）

当基督显现时，上帝所有的儿女都将与祂完全联合，并要在荣耀中与祂一同掌权。痛苦、悲哀与死亡将被彻底废除，蒙救赎的人将被招聚到新天新地之中，在那里他们将与天使一同永永远远敬拜上帝。上帝将更新万物，上帝要在万物之中成为万有。这是所有信徒所持守的有福盼望。

The New Creation

All God’s children will be united with Christ when he appears, and they will reign with him in glory. Pain, sorrow, and death will be abolished, and the redeemed will be gathered into the new heaven and new earth, where together with the angels they will worship God forever. God will make all things new, and God will be all in all. This is the blessed hope of all believers.

📖 经文引证 (和合本 CUV)

马太福音 24:29–31；25:13；马可福音 13:32–37；路加福音 16:9；23:43；约翰福音 14:1–3；使徒行传 2:17；罗马书 8:18–22；哥林多前书 3:13–15；15:26；哥林多后书 5:10；腓立比书 1:23；帖撒罗尼迦前书 4:13–18；5:1–11；帖撒罗尼迦后书 1:5–12；2:1–12；提多书 2:13；希伯来书 1:2；9:26–28；彼得前书 1:20；4:7；约翰一书 2:18；3:2–3；启示录 19:17–21；20:7–15；21–22章。

📖 Scripture References

Matthew 24:29-31; 25:13; Mark 13:32-37; Luke 16:9; 23:43; John 14:1-3; Acts 2:17; Romans 8:18-22; 1 Corinthians 3:13-15; 15:26; 2 Corinthians 5:10; Philippians 1:23; 1 Thessalonians 4:13-18; 5:1-11; 2 Thessalonians 1:5-12; 2:1-12; Titus 2:13; Hebrews 1:2; 9:26-28; 1 Peter 1:20; 4:7; 1 John 2:18; 3:2-3; Revelation 19:17-21; 20:7-15; 21-22.

神学注释 二、神学研读注释
Theological Commentary Commentary
🇨🇳 中文神学注释
🇬🇧 English Commentary

使徒彼得在首个基督徒五旬节复活基督之灵的倾降中，看见了末世的开启。他引用先知约珥的话解释道：“神说：在末后的日子，我要将我的灵浇灌凡有气血的”（徒 2:17）。圣灵的降临标志着基督在世上救赎大功的告成，因此可以说，基督的第一次降临拉开了末世的序幕。正如《希伯来书》作者所说：“就在这末世借着他儿子晓谕我们”（来 1:2）。

The apostle Peter saw the beginning of the last days in the outpouring of the Spirit of the risen Christ on the first Christian Pentecost. Quoting the prophet Joel, he explained, “In the last days, God says, I will pour out my Spirit on all people” (Acts 2:17). The coming of the Spirit marked the completion of Christ’s redemptive work on earth, and so one can say that the first coming of Christ inaugurated the last days. “In these last days,” said the writer to the Hebrews, “he has spoken to us by his Son” (1:2).

末世（The Last Days）

使徒约翰在其第一封书信中告诉读者，他们正生活在“末时”之中（约一 2:18）。保罗在写给哥林多教会的书信中提醒道：“时候减少了”（林前 7:29）；彼得也指出：“万物的结局近了”（彼前 4:7）。雅各同样强调第一世纪的信徒生活在末后的日子：“你们也当忍耐，坚固你们的心，因为主来的日子近了”（雅 5:8）。《希伯来书》作者切望信徒彼此勉励，“既知道那日子临近，就更当如此”（来 10:25）。在圣经的最后一卷书中，数次提到了基督初临与再临之间这段过渡期的短暂与紧迫（启 1:1；22:7, 20）。

从第一世纪起，教会就一直生活在新约作者所谓的“末世”之中。末世由基督的救赎之工与圣灵的倾降所开启，并将在祂于荣耀中再临之时终结。在这段过渡期内，教会在世上履行其使命。正如耶稣所预言的，教会见证耶稣是唯一的救恩之路，这往往会招致逼迫与深重的苦难。拔摩海岛上的先见看见众圣徒归入荣耀的家乡，“是从大患难中出来的”（启 7:14）。尽管历经患难，教会最终必将得胜，因为基督已借着祂的受死与复活战胜了一切邪恶权势。

信徒常常感到困惑的一个问题是：如果基督在经过两千年的等候之后仍未再临，那么在新约时代怎么能说这世代的结局“近了”呢？对此，我们只能说，上帝看时间与我们看时间截然不同。在祂看来，“千年如已过的昨日”（诗 90:4），“一日如千年，千年如一日”（彼后 3:8）。信徒始终生活在今世与来世的交界线上；正是因为这个缘故，经文勉励我们要时刻保持属灵的警醒，预备好在主再来时迎见祂。

The Last Days

John in his first epistle told his readers that they were living in “the last hour” (2:18). Writing to the Corinthians, Paul reminded the church that “the time is short” (1 Corinthians 7:29), and Peter said “the end of all things is near” (1 Peter 4:7). James also underscored that believers in the first century were living in the last days. “Be patient and stand firm, because the Lord’s coming is near” (5:8). And the writer to the Hebrews wanted believers to encourage one another “all the more as you see the Day approaching” (Hebrews 10:25). In the last book of the Bible, we have several references to the brevity of this interim between the first and second coming of Christ (Revelation 1:1; 22:7, 20).

From the first century on, the church has lived in what the New Testament writers call “the last days.” They were inaugurated by Christ’s saving work and the outpouring of the Spirit, and they will end when he returns in glory. During this interim, the church carries out its mission in the world. The church’s witness to Jesus as the only way of salvation often leads to persecution and intense suffering, as Jesus predicted. The seer of Patmos saw the saints coming home to glory “out of the great tribulation” (Revelation 7:14). In spite of suffering, the church triumphs in the end because Christ conquered all evil powers by his death and resurrection.

A question that often puzzles believers is: How could the end of this age be near in New Testament times, when Christ has not yet come after 2,000 years of waiting? To that, we can only say that God views time differently from the way we do. With Him, a thousand years are like a day, and a day is like a thousand years (2 Peter 3:8). Believers always live at the borderline between this world and the next, and for that reason they are encouraged to be spiritually awake at all times, ready to meet the Lord when he comes.

死亡（Death）

尽管基督借着祂的复活打破了死亡的权势，但死亡仍是“尽末了所毁灭的仇敌”（林前 15:26）。因此，在世代末了基督尚未再临的延宕期间，信徒仍不免一死。当主降临时，虽有许多人活着（帖前 4:15），但在漫长的等候、作工与守望中，已有极多的人离世。死亡不仅在生命旅途的终点逼近我们，而且我们在毕生之中都能敏锐意识到死亡的存在。虽然信徒对死亡的过程本能地存有畏惧，但他们却绝非“因怕死而为奴仆”（来 2:15）。

圣经作者在谈及信徒之死时，运用了各种生动的比喻与委婉说法。死亡被看作是“拆毁帐棚”（林后 5:1）、“离世归去”（腓 1:23；提后 4:6）。“睡了”是对死亡十分常见的委婉称呼（帖前 4:14；林前 15:51）。逝去也被比作脱下衣服（林后 5:3-4），以及种子播种入土（林前 15:42-43）。

关于在我们主的初临与再临之间离世的信徒所处的具体状态（居间状态，intermediate state），圣经并未提供详尽无遗的信息。然而，我们深知死亡绝不能“叫我们与上帝的爱隔绝；这爱是在我们的主基督耶稣里的”（罗 8:38-39）。甚至在死亡中，基督徒仍被称为是“在基督里”的（帖前 4:16；林前 15:18）。保罗写道：“我们若活着，是为主而活；若死了，是为主而死。所以，我们或活或死总是主的人”（罗 14:8）。关于在基督里离世之人所处居间状态的最明确论述，见于腓立比书 1:23；保罗在面临殉道之际坦然宣告：“我正在两难之间，情愿离世与基督同在，因为这是好得无比的。”如果死后与基督同在远胜于今世的生活，那么我们便可以同使徒约翰一同赞叹：“从今以后，在主里面而死的人有福了！”（启 14:13）。

Death

Although Christ by his resurrection broke the power of death, death is the last enemy to be destroyed (1 Corinthians 15:26). And so, as the return of Christ at the end of the age is delayed, believers must die. A great many will be alive when the Lord comes (1 Thessalonians 4:15), but a great number have already died during this long time of waiting, working, and watching. Death confronts us not only at the end of life’s journey, but we are conscious of death throughout life. And while believers naturally fear the process of dying, they are not “in slavery by their fear of death” (Hebrews 2:15). The biblical writers use a variety of figures of speech and euphemisms when they speak of the death of believers. It is seen as the dismantling of a tent (2 Corinthians 5:1), a departure (Philippians 1:23; 2 Timothy 4:6). “Sleep” is a common euphemism for death (1 Thessalonians 4:14; 1 Corinthians 15:51). Dying is compared to undressing (2 Corinthians 5:3, 4), and to the sowing of seed in the ground (1 Corinthians 15:42, 43).

We do not have detailed information on the state of believers who die during this time, between the first and second advent of our Lord. However, we know that death cannot “separate us from the love of God that is in Christ Jesus our Lord” (Romans 8:38, 39). Even in death, Christians are said to be “in Christ” (1 Thessalonians 4:16; 1 Corinthians 15:18). “Whether we live or die, we belong to the Lord,” writes Paul (Romans 14:8). The clearest statement on the intermediate state of the dead in Christ is found in Philippians 1:23, where Paul, facing martyrdom, boldly states: “I am torn between the two: I desire to depart and be with Christ, which is better by far.” If to be with Christ after death is far better than life here on earth, we can say with the apostle John, “Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on” (Revelation 14:13).

末日（The Last Day）

我们不知道末世（这末后的日子）还会延续多久。然而，我们绝不应当指责上帝耽延了基督的再临，因为祂延长这等候的时间，乃是为给人悔改的机会（彼后 3:9）。但是，当上帝命定的时刻敲响时，这末后的日子就将终结于“末日”。

耶稣屡次提及“末日”（约 6:39, 44, 54；12:48）。这末日也被称为“世界的末了”（the consummation），即今世的终局落幕（太 13:39, 40, 49；24:3；28:20）。有时末日被简称为“那日”（帖前 5:5；林前 3:13；来 10:25），这实际上是“主的日子”的简称（帖前 5:2；帖后 2:2）。它也被称为“大日”（犹 6；启 6:17；16:14），以及“耶稣基督的日子”（林前 1:8；林后 1:14；腓 1:6；2:16）。它既是“上帝震怒的日子”（罗 2:5），也是“得赎的日子”（弗 4:30）。此外，它还被称为“末期”（可 13:7；林前 15:24）。

我们距离终局究竟有多近，无人知晓；尽管正如保罗早在第一世纪所言：“因为我们得救，现今比初信的时候更近了。黑夜已深，白昼将近”（罗 13:11-12）。圣经告诫基督徒切勿试图为世代的终结、为那末日设定日期。“但那日子、那时辰，没有人知道，连天上的使者也不知道，子也不知道，惟有父知道”（可 13:32）。基督的来临将如夜间的贼一样；也就是说，我们绝不可能预先测定祂再来的时辰（帖前 5:2；彼后 3:10）。

在门诺会的历史中，曾发生过一些令人痛心的事件，有人曾为基督的再来定下确切日期。既然此类预言无一例外均被证明是错误的，而且既然我们的主告诫我们切莫妄定日期，我们就当警惕一切试图通过观察当今社会的政治、经济或社会动向来推算基督再临迫近性的企图。耶稣确实提到了许多“时代的预兆”，但这些预兆正是伴随整个今世教会历史的常态——逼迫、饥荒、地震、战争等。它们的用意绝非助长对基督二次降临时辰的臆测，而是要让基督的门徒清醒地认识到，他们是在怎样的一个世界中履行使命（可 13:5-13）。

当世代末了基督再临之时，所有在基督里死的人都要复活，活着的圣徒都要改变。“我们不是都要睡觉，乃是都要改变，就在一霎时，眨眼之间，号筒末次吹响的时候”（林前 15:51-52）。这被称为“头一次的复活”（启 20:5-6），因为“其余的死人还没有复活，直等那一千年完了”。

我们现今拥有的身体是上帝所设计、适应在今世地上生活的，但“血肉之体不能承受上帝的国”（林前 15:50）。因此，当基督再临时，信徒将获赐全新的身体，与祂“荣耀的身体”相似（腓 3:21）。“所种的是必朽坏的，复活的是不朽坏的；所种的是羞辱的，复活的是荣耀的；所种的是软弱的，复活的是强壮的；所种的是血气的身体，复活的是灵性的身体”（林前 15:42-44）。

The Last Day

We do not know how long the last days, the end times, will be extended. However, we should not accuse God of delaying the second coming of Christ, for He extends this time of waiting in order to give people an opportunity to repent (2 Peter 3:9). But when God’s hour strikes, the last days will come to an end with “the last day.”

Jesus spoke repeatedly of “the last day” ( John 6:39, 44, 54; 12:48). This last day is also called “the consummation,” the wrap-up of this age (Matthew 13:39, 40, 49; 24:3; 28:20). Sometimes the last day is called simply “the day” (1 Thessalonians 5:5; 1 Corinthians 3:13; Hebrews 10:25), which is really shorthand for “the day of the Lord” (1 Thessalonians 5:2; 2 Thessalonians 2:2). It is known also as “the great day” ( Jude 6; Revelation 6:17; 16:14), and “the day of Jesus Christ” (1 Corinthians 1:8; 2 Corinthians 1:14; Philippians 1:6; 2:16). It is both “the day of wrath” (Romans 2:5), and “the day of redemption” (Ephesians 4:30). Also it is called “the end” (Mark 13:7; 1 Corinthians 15:24).

How close we are to the end is not known, although, as Paul said already in the first century, “our salvation is nearer now than when we first believed. The night is nearly over, the day is almost here” (Romans 13:11, 12). Christians are warned not to try to set dates for the end of the age, the last day. “No one knows about that day or hour, not even the angels in heaven, nor the Son, but only the Father” (Mark 13:32). Christ will come as a thief in the night; that is, we cannot predetermine the time of his coming (1 Thessalonians 5:2; 2 Peter 3:10).

In Mennonite history, there have been some sad incidents in which dates were set for the return of Christ. Since such predictions have always proved to be wrong, and since our Lord warned us not to set dates, we should be wary of all attempts to determine the imminence of Christ’s return by observing political, economic, or social developments in our society. Jesus did mention a number of “signs of the times,” but they are the kind of signs that attend the life of the church throughout the present age—persecution, famine, earthquakes, war, and the like. They are not meant to encourage speculation on the time of Christ’s second advent, but rather to make the followers of Christ aware of the kind of world in which they would carry out their mission (Mark 13:5-13).

When Christ returns at the end of the age, all the dead in Christ will rise, and the living saints will be transformed. “We will not all die, but we will all be changed, in a moment, in the twinkling of an eye, at the last trumpet” (1 Corinthians 15:51, 52 NRSV). This is called the first resurrection (Revelation 20: 5, 6), for “the rest of the dead did not come to life until the thousand years were ended.”

The bodies which we now have were designed by God for life on this earth, but “flesh and blood cannot inherit the kingdom of God” (1 Corinthians 15:50). And so believers will be given new bodies when Christ returns, which are like “the body of his glory” (Philippians 3:21 NRSV). “What is sown is perishable, what is raised is imperishable. It is sown in dishonour, it is raised in glory. It is sown in weakness, it is raised in power. It is sown a physical body, it is raised a spiritual body” (1 Corinthians 15: 42-44 NRSV).

千禧年（Millennium）

圣经中唯一明确提及基督千禧年统治的经文（启 20:1-10），在门诺弟兄会中有过不同的理解。后千禧年论（Post-millennialism）认为，在基督再临之前，世界将见证一个普遍承认基督为王的黄金时代；这种观点在我们教会的读经者中普遍未获认同。

部分门诺弟兄会信徒持无千禧年论（Amillennialism）。根据这一观点，基督的统治在祂第一次降临时便已开启。撒但如今已被捆绑，属灵上死的人正复活得生，享受上帝之国度的丰盛福祉。在千禧年（无论这一时期有多长）的末尾，撒但将再次被释放，并对上帝及其国度发动最后的猛烈反扑。但撒但及其随从将遭到彻底的溃败，并被永远驱逐出上帝的同在。

前千禧年论（Pre-millennialism）在门诺弟兄会中更为普遍。按照对启示录 20 章的这种理解，千禧年紧随基督的二次降临。然而，关于千禧年的本质，存在着多元的观点。此外，有些人并非从数学字面意义、而是从象征意义来理解这一千年。正因为存在这些不同见解，且由于我们对千禧年的理解并不实质影响我们的日常生活与宣教使命，门诺弟兄会更倾向于不在其《信仰宣言》中硬性规定或纳入某种特定的千禧年观点。

Millennium

The one passage in the Scriptures that mentions the millennial reign of Christ (Revelation 20:1-10) has been understood in different ways by Mennonite Brethren. Post-millennialism, in which it is thought that before Christ returns, this world will witness a golden age in which Christ is acknowledged as King, is not generally held by Bible readers in our churches. Some Mennonite Brethren espouse amillennialism. According to this view, Christ’s reign was inaugurated at his first coming. Satan is now bound and the spiritually dead are being raised to life and enjoy the blessings of God’s kingdom. At the end of the millennium (however long this period might be), Satan is loosed once more and he makes a final onslaught on God and His kingdom. But Satan and his followers are totally defeated and banished forever from the presence of God.

Pre-millennialism is more widely held by Mennonite Brethren. According to this understanding of Revelation 20, the millennium follows the second coming of Christ. However, there is diversity of thought on the nature of the millennium. Also, the thousand years are understood by some not mathematically but as a symbolic number. Because of these different views, and because our understanding of the millennium does not affect our daily life and mission in any significant way, Mennonite Brethren prefer not to include a particular view of the millennium in their Confession of Faith.

审判（Judgment）

审判并非一个令人愉快的话题，但如果我们渴望忠于圣经，就不能回避这一“开端”的基本教义（来 6:1-2）。虽然上帝对人类罪恶的震怒甚至在当今也透过不时临到人类的诸般审判由天显明（罗 1:18），但终将有一个“上帝震怒，显他公义审判的日子”来到（罗 2:5）。世上最慈爱的耶稣，在其讲道与比喻中，曾极其响亮且清晰地宣称审判之日必定来临（太 7:19；8:12；25:31-46；约 3:16；5:29）。使徒们也同样极其明确地宣告，不敬虔的人“要受刑罚，就是永远沉沦，离开主的面和他权能的荣光”（帖后 1:9）。

人类的大敌魔鬼及其所有的随从，都将被扔进硫磺火湖之中（启 20:10），即“为魔鬼和他的使者所预备的永火”（太 25:41）。同样的命运也等待着在世代末了出现的敌基督，基督将用口中的气灭绝他（帖后 2:8）。凡拒绝福音的人都将承受永远的刑罚（帖后 1:8；彼前 4:17），所有背道者亦复如是（来 10:26-27）。“信子的人有永生；不信子的人得不着永生，神的震怒常在他身上”（约 3:36）。

恶人所受的刑罚在圣经中有多种描绘。他们被称为落入地狱（太 5:22, 29, 30；可 9:43, 45, 47）。地狱也被称为“火湖”（启 19:20；20:14, 15）。耶稣曾论及“地狱的火”（太 5:22）。它也是一处黑暗之地，“在那里必要哀哭切齿了”（太 8:12；22:13；25:30）。它被称为“第二次的死”（启 20:6）、刑罚与沉沦（帖后 1:9；林前 1:18）。与此形成鲜明对比的是，信徒蒙拯救“脱离将来的忿怒”（帖前 1:10）。

Judgment

Judgment is not a palatable subject, but if we want to be faithful to the Scriptures we cannot avoid this “elementary” doctrine (Hebrews 6:1, 2). And whereas God’s wrath against human wickedness is revealed from heaven even now in the judgments that strike humankind from time to time (Romans 1:18), there will yet come a “day of wrath, when God’s righteous judgment will be revealed” (Romans 2:5 NRSV). In sermon and parable, Jesus, the kindest person that ever lived on this earth, proclaimed loudly and clearly that a day of judgment was yet to come (Matthew 7:19; 8:12; 25:31-46; John 3:16; 5:29). The apostles too made it very clear that the ungodly “will suffer the punishment of eternal destruction, separated from the presence of the Lord and from the glory of his might” (2 Thessalonians 1:9 NRSV). The devil, the archenemy of humankind, and all his hangers-on will be cast into the lake of fire and brimstone (Revelation 20:10), “the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels” (Matthew 25:41). The same fate awaits the antichrist, who emerges at the end of the age, and whom Christ will destroy with the breath of his mouth (2 Thessalonians 2:8). Those who have rejected the gospel will suffer eternal punishment (2 Thessalonians 1:8; 1 Peter 4:17) as will all apostates (Hebrews 10: 26, 27). “Whoever believes in the Son has eternal life; whoever disobeys the Son will not see life, but must endure God’s wrath” ( John 3:36 NRSV).

The punishment of the wicked is described in various ways. They are said to go to hell (Matthew 5:22, 29, 30; Mark 9:43, 45, 47). Hell is also called “the lake of fire” (Revelation 19:20; 20:14, 15). Jesus spoke of “the fire of hell” (Matthew 5:22). It is also a place of darkness “where there will be weeping and gnashing of teeth” (Matthew 8:12; 22:13; 25:30). It is called “the second death” (Revelation 20:6), punishment and destruction (2 Thessalonians 1:9; 1 Corinthians 1:18). By contrast, believers are rescued “from the coming wrath” (1 Thessalonians 1:10 NRSV).

上帝永恒的统治（The Eternal Reign of God）

凡信靠基督及其救赎恩典的人，都拥有永生的盼望。耶稣曾向门徒应许，祂去是为他们预备地方，并且将来必再来接他们到祂那里去（约 14:2-6）。祂称这荣耀的所在为“我父的家”（约 14:2），其中有许多住处——足以容纳历世历代所有的圣徒。当基督驾着荣耀的云彩降临时，祂必将属于祂的人接入荣耀的家乡，“这样，我们就要和主永远同在”（帖前 4:17）。天父的家是“上帝所造，不是人手所造，在天上永存的房屋”（林后 5:1）。耶稣也论及“永存的帐幕”（路 16:9）。

圣徒这属天的家园也被称为乐园（路 23:43；林后 12:4）。“得胜的，我必将神乐园中生命树的果子赐给他吃”（启 2:7）。在耶稣的一个比喻中，祂使用了一个极具犹太色彩的喻道词汇指称天堂，称之为“亚伯拉罕的怀里”（路 16:22）。耶稣宣告，从东从西将有许多人来，在天国里与亚伯拉罕、以撒、雅各一同坐席（太 8:11-12）。在启示录第 19 章中，约翰以盛大的羔羊婚筵描绘了蒙救赎者的福乐，羔羊与其新妇将在筵席上庆贺他们永恒的联结。关于天堂展开详尽描绘的画面之一，是圣城新耶路撒冷（启 21, 22）。这就是那座“有根基的城，就是神所经营所建造的”（来 11:10）。历代圣徒一直翘首期盼着那座“将来的城”（来 13:14），即天上的锡安山（启 14:1）。

在今世，我们行事为人是凭着信心，不是凭着眼见（林后 5:7）；我们如今是“对着镜子观看，模糊不清”（林前 13:12），但当末日来临之时，我们将与基督面对面。“主若显现，我们必要像他，因为必得见他的真体”（约一 3:2）。得见上帝，就是得见祂的荣耀。耶稣曾祷告，愿祂的追随者得以看见祂在创立世界以前所享有的荣耀（约 17:24）。今世的苦楚，“若比起将来要显于我们的荣耀就不足介意了”（罗 8:18）。

当我们在世代末了显现在基督面前时（林后 5:10），祂将因我们奉祂的名所行的一切赏赐我们。各人将照自己的劳苦得自己的赏赐（林前 3:8）。上帝绝不会忘记我们的作为以及我们向他人所显出的爱心（来 6:10）。当信徒跨入荣耀之门时，“他们息了自己的劳苦”，并且“作工的果效也随着他们”（启 14:13）。

在荣耀中与基督同在，意味着在最深层面上体验极致的喜乐。上帝要擦去他们一切的眼泪（启 7:17）：“不再有死亡，也不再有悲哀、哭号、疼痛，因为以前的事都过去了”（启 21:4）。上帝所有的儿女都切盼着那一天，得以“进来享受你主人的快乐”（太 25:21, 23）。而在我们等候那日之时，我们拥有坚定的确据：上帝“能保守你们不失脚，叫你们无瑕无疵、欢欢喜喜站在他荣耀之前”（犹 24）。

The Eternal Reign of God

Those who put their trust in Christ and his redeeming grace have the hope of eternal life. Jesus promised his disciples that he was preparing a place for them and that he would someday come and take them to himself ( John 14:26). He calls this glorious place “my Father’s house” ( John 14:2) in which there are many rooms—room enough for all the saints of all the ages. When Christ comes in clouds of glory, he will take those who belong to him home to glory, “and so we will be with the Lord forever” (1 Thessalonians 4:17). The Father’s house is “a building from God, an eternal house in heaven, not built by human hands” (2 Corinthians 5:1). Jesus also spoke of the “eternal dwellings” (Luke 16:9).

This heavenly home of the saints is also known as paradise (Luke 23:43; 2 Corinthians 12:4). “To everyone who conquers, I will give permission to eat from the tree of life that is in the paradise of God” (Revelation 2:7 NRSV). In one of his parables, Jesus uses a very Jewish figure of speech for heaven, calling it “Abraham’s bosom” (Luke 16:22 KJV). People will come from east and west and sit at table with Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob in the kingdom of heaven, says Jesus (Matthew 8:11, 12). In Revelation 19, John describes the bliss of the redeemed in terms of a great wedding feast in which the Lamb and his bride celebrate their eternal union. One of the pictures of heaven that is expanded in some detail is that of the holy city, the New Jerusalem (Revelation 21, 22). This is “the city with foundations, whose architect and builder is God” (Hebrews 11:10). Throughout the ages, the saints have looked forward to “the city that is to come” (Hebrews 13:14), the heavenly Mount Zion (Revelation 14:1).

In this age, we walk by faith, not by sight (2 Corinthians 5:7); we see by means of “a mirror dimly” (1 Corinthians 13:12 NRSV), but when the last day comes, we shall see Christ face to face. “We know that when he appears, we shall be like him, for we shall see him as he is” (1 John 3:2). To see God is to see His glory. Jesus prayed that his followers might see the glory which he had before the foundation of the world ( John 17:24). The sufferings of this life “are not worth comparing with the glory that will be revealed in us” (Romans 8:18).

When we appear before Christ at the end of the age (2 Corinthians 5:10), he will reward us for all that we did in his name. Each person will receive his or her rewards according to their labours (1 Corinthians 3:8). God will not overlook our work and our love for others (Hebrews 6:10). When believers enter the gates of glory “they will rest from their labour” but “their deeds will follow them” (Revelation 14:13).

To be with Christ in glory means to experience joy at its deepest level. God will wipe away all tears (Revelation 7:17): “Death will be no more; mourning and crying and pain will be no more, for the first things have passed away” (Revelation 21:4 NRSV). All of God’s children look forward to the day when they will “enter into the joy” of the Lord (Matthew 25:21, 23 NRSV). And while we wait for that day, we have the assurance that God “is able to keep [us] from falling and to present [us] before his glorious presence without fault and with great joy” ( Jude 24).

教牧应用 三、教牧实践应用
Pastoral Application Pastoral Application
🇨🇳 中文教牧应用
🇬🇧 English Pastoral Application

“基督最终的得胜”这一信仰条款在两个基本层面引发了切实关切的牧养议题：第一涉及死亡与来生，第二涉及末世论与“末世”。圣经呼召基督徒带着对终局的瞻望来度日。因着这样的视野，主在我们生命中得享完全的主权，校准我们的生活优先次序，引领我们渐臻成熟。这样的人生必将毫无保留地赢得属天的称赞，听见“做得好”的赞许回响。

本条款的教导对信徒而言是极大的安慰，对非信徒而言则是严肃的警诫。它向所有信徒宣告确据与抚慰之言，尤其是那些在挣扎、疲惫或经历亲友离世哀恸中的人；同时，它也向那些疏忽大意和漠不关心之人发出悔改与信靠的呼召。

警醒守望是带着终局视野度日的基督徒之显著印记。这种警醒不仅关乎对时间的留心，更关乎由此塑造的生活方式。唯有未作准备的基督徒才会活得好像自己必定拥有七十余载光阴，而后才需要在审判者面前陈明交账。然而，交账的截止日期可能从两个方面急剧提前：一是死亡，二是基督的再临。

The article on “Christ’s Final Triumph” raises practical pastoral issues in two basic areas: the first involving death and the afterlife and the second involving eschatology and the “last days.” The Bible calls Christians to live their lives with the end in view. With such a perspective, the Master is given free rein to align our life priorities and nurture us to maturity. Such a life will receive, without reservation, the acclaim of heaven as the words “well done” are sounded.

The teaching of this article is a comfort to the believer and a warning to the non-believer. It speaks words of assurance to all believers, especially those struggling, weary, or grieving death. It is a call of repentance and faith to the careless and indifferent.

Watchfulness is a distinguishing characteristic of the Christian living with the end in view. This watchfulness pertains not only to the timing, but to the resultant lifestyle as well. Only unprepared Christians live as though they will almost certainly have upwards of 70 years before their work is presented before the Judge. The completion date may be hastened dramatically on two fronts: by death or Christ’s second coming.

对死亡的视角（Perspectives on Death）

人生的公分母乃是死亡。当人们目睹他人的离世或亲自面对死亡的前景时，都会直面这一无可规避的现实。这些经历为事工开展与牧养关怀提供了绝佳的契机。

基督徒时刻预备好坦然面对死亡。他们笃定从容地生活，因为深知死亡乃是归家的召唤，引领他们归向真正公民身份所属的家乡。他们体会到自身生命的脆弱与必死性，但这绝非完全令人心神不宁的念头。虽然他们敏锐察知人生的无常，却绝不会病态地沉溺于对死亡与濒死的恐惧担忧之中。

当因疾病或年迈而面临死亡迫近时，心怀焦虑是完全合乎人情的正常反应。从今生通往死亡的过渡是一场鲜有路线图指引的旅程，唯有我们的主耶稣亲自开辟了道路。因此，心中存有些许忧虑是完全可以理解的。

为了预备这一跨越，重温圣经中关于上帝儿女的诸般应许，对于带来平安与确据尤为有益。能够坚固并鼓舞信徒的，往往更多在于他们与上帝之间亲密关系的笃厚，而不仅是天堂的极尽绚丽。

对信徒而言，末世的审判毋需恐惧战兢，而应当被视为“领取成绩单的日子”。能否“毕业”（得救）早已毋庸置疑，但信徒将根据各自的忠心程度领受不同的奖赏。一个人的名字只要记录在“生命册”上，就毫无惧怕的理由。在耶稣基督里，信徒得以稳固站立。

现代医学技术有时使延长生命的决策变得错综复杂。基督徒固然享受地上的生命，却毋需诉诸非常规或极端的手段去勉强延命。他们深知，今世尘世的生命绝非终极的实在。然而，无论协助自杀还是主动安乐死都不应当被采纳，因为唯独上帝拥有赐予和取走生命的绝对主权。（参见第十四条：人类生命的尊严）。

Perspectives on Death

The common denominator of life is death. People are confronted with this reality as they witness the death of someone else or face the prospect personally. These experiences provide an excellent opportunity for ministry and pastoral care.

Christians are prepared to face death at any time. They live confidently in the knowledge that death is a summons home to the place where their real citizenship resides. They feel their mortality, but it’s not an altogether unsettling thought. Though keenly aware of life’s uncertainty, they are not morbidly preoccupied with death and dying.

When faced with imminent death because of disease or old age, it’s normal to face the prospect with anxiety. The passage from life to death is a trip with few road maps, except that of our Lord Jesus. Certainly, some apprehension is to be expected.

To prepare for the passage, rehearsal of the promises of Scripture concerning God’s children can be particularly helpful to bring assurance. It is the strength of the relationship with God more than the extravagant beauty of heaven that is likely to encourage the believer.

End-time judgment for believers should not be dreaded, but seen as report card day. Graduation is not in question, but different awards will be received on the basis of faithfulness. If one’s name is in the “Book of Life,” there is no reason to fear. In Jesus Christ, the believer stands firm.

Medical science has sometimes made decisions regarding extension of life difficult. While Christians enjoy life, they have less need to prolong it by extraordinary means. They know that life on earth is not the final reality. However, neither assisted suicide nor active euthanasia should be pursued since it is God’s exclusive right to give and take life. (See Article 14: The Sanctity of Human Life.)

基督徒的葬礼（The Christian Funeral）

基督徒的葬礼始终洋溢着盼望的底色。这绝非意味着哀伤应当被刻意压抑。死亡依然会带来痛苦与忧伤，正如与挚爱之人经历漫长的离别一样。完全的得胜只有当生死轮回被彻底打破、生命单独永存直到永远之时才会全然实现。悲恸是真实的，但盼望同样真实。

葬礼或追思礼拜是一项崇拜行动。筹备礼拜为健康地缅怀逝者生平、哀悼亲友离世提供了契机。礼拜的目标既是尊荣逝去挚爱的生命，也是勉励家属、亲朋与会众继续活出有意义、有盼望、有目标的人生。葬礼上的宣道劝勉是一次契机，引导会众思想生命的意义，将焦点引向创造主上帝、救赎者基督以及安慰者圣灵。从逝者生平中提炼出一个核心比喻或意象，能使劝勉信息更有焦点，也能给沉浸在哀伤中的家庭带来极大的勉励。若丧家尚未认信基督，他们或许能够接纳基于上帝在基督里的恩典信息、简短却发人深省的安慰与盼望之言。牧者必须格外谨慎，绝不可辜负丧家的信任，将他人的悲伤当作宣泄严酷论断或进行投机式布道的讲台。与此同时，牧者也决不能传递“廉价恩典”的观念。许多牧者的实践表明，清晰宣告上帝的恩典信息，往往为日后与悲伤中的亲友进行深入的福音交谈铺平了道路。

不同地域可能存在不同的丧葬习俗。牧者敏锐察知文化与地域上的敏感性至关重要。有些人倾向于先举行仅限至亲参加的安葬礼，随后举行公开的追思礼拜；另一些人则坚持在葬礼上开棺瞻仰遗容，然后再行安葬。（有关筹划葬礼的具体指导，参见出版物《回应呼召》[Following the Call]，第 138–140 页，Kindred Productions 出版）。

有些人可能对火葬与土葬的选择存有疑问。旧约与新约圣经均默认土葬是安放遗体的常规习俗。然而，并不存在任何禁止选择火葬的神学障碍。不过，由于圣经中有时将“火”与审判相联系，家属的情感感受往往成为更为关键的考量因素。

The Christian Funeral

A Christian funeral is permeated with an undertone of hope. That is not to say that grief should be suppressed. Death still brings distress and sadness as with any prolonged separation from a loved one. Full victory will be gained when the cycle of life and death is broken, when life alone will prevail for eternity. Grieving is a reality, but so is hope.

The funeral or memorial service is an act of worship. Planning for the service gives an opportunity for healthy remembering of the life and for grieving the loss of the deceased. The goals of the service are to honour the life of the loved one, as well as to encourage the family, friends, and community to continue living with meaning, hope, and purpose. The funeral meditation is an opportunity to reflect on the purpose of life with reference to God as Creator, Christ as Redeemer, and the Spirit as Comforter. Finding a central metaphor suggested by the life of the person being memorialized can give focus to the meditation and encouragement to the grieving family. If a family does not confess faith in Christ, they may be open to brief but challenging words of comfort and hope based on the message of God’s grace in Christ. The pastor must take care not to violate trust by using someone’s sorrow as a pulpit for hard-edged judgment or opportunistic evangelism. At the same time, the pastor must not project a notion of “cheap grace.” Many have found that clearly articulating the message of God’s grace opens the way for evangelistic opportunities in later conversations with grieving family and friends. Different areas may have different funeral customs. It is important for a pastor to be aware of cultural and regional sensitivities. Some prefer to practice a more intimate burial service followed by a public memorial service. Others insist on an open casket at the funeral followed by burial. (For help in planning the funeral, see the Kindred Productions publication Following the Call, 138-40.)

Some may have questions about cremation versus burial. Both the Old and New Testaments assume burial as a customary means of laying the body to rest. However, there is no theological issue which would prevent choosing cremation. Sometimes, however, fire is associated with judgment and family sensitivities become the more crucial consideration.

在基督再临光照下的优先次序（Priorities in Light of the Second Coming）

对于警醒的基督徒而言，主的第二次降临是有福的盼望。无论在地上经历过何等喜乐，与在空中迎接我们慈爱的主和救主所带来的喜乐相比，世上的一切都将黯然失色。因此，警醒且预备妥当的基督徒总是“轻装前行”。我们轻看物质财富，因为我们深知这个世界及其上的一切都会朽坏消逝。我们用世上短暂的事物换取永恒的益处，从而将我们的心深深扎根在天国的土壤中。我们清楚意识到，审判者将鉴察我们如何为了上帝的国度使用财富与时间，而积攒自私的财富与奢靡放纵必将受到负面的审断。

人们应当意识到，在繁荣富裕的年代，对预言和基督再来的关注往往会减退；而在艰难逼迫或其威胁成为常态之处，对耶稣再来的渴慕通常更为强烈。生活在优渥安逸年代的人，在基督显现时往往更容易措手不及、毫无防备。

正如死亡往往突如其来，警醒的信徒深知基督的再来会让许多人始料不及。没有人知道、也没有人能够知道上帝何时会发出最终的号令。历世历代以来，特别是在千禧年狂热的高潮期，自封的先知们常常预定日期，具体到某一特定日子，或笼统到某一特定年份。对于此类推测，不论其采用何种手段来预测耶稣再来的时辰，我们都切莫予以理睬。此类预测不仅狂妄自大，而且彻头彻尾违背圣经。唯有天父知道那时辰。

然而，我们认真研读预示终局来临的各样事件之本质，乃是应当的。对圣经预言的研究，应当与圣经其余部分的真理保持平衡。对此茫然无知可能导致缺乏预备；但若是长期将其作为首要的关注焦点，往往会导致走极端、视角偏颇，甚至引发教会分裂。它可能会造就一批“满脑子属天思想，却在地上毫无用处”的人。

警醒的信徒清楚知道，争夺人心思意念与灵魂的属灵争战将愈演愈烈。迷惑人的神迹奇事将成为诱使一些人离道反教的催化剂（太 24:24）。机警警醒的基督徒绝不会被耀眼的表象与虚浮的喧嚣所蛊惑，而是切切寻求上帝对他们生命的旨意。神在其爱子里赐给我们的信息，对于我们活出一个被圣灵充满的生命而言，已绰绰有余。

我们若时刻警醒，并与上帝、与人保持合宜的关系，我们的生活优先次序就毋需在最后一刻仓促发生剧烈的调整。预备妥当的生命以警醒与自律为特征。这些态度具体彰显在恒切祷告、圣洁纯全以及舍己奉献服事的生活之中（太 25:31-46）。

Priorities in Light of the Second Coming

For the watchful Christian, the second advent is a blessed hope. No matter what joys are experienced on earth, they will certainly be eclipsed by the joy of meeting our loving Lord and Saviour in the air. Therefore, the watchful and prepared Christian “packs light.” We hold lightly to material possessions because we realize that the world and everything in it will wear out and disappear. We exchange the temporal things of this world for eternal gain, thus planting our hearts firmly on heaven’s soil. We realize that the Judge will consider how wealth and time were used for God’s kingdom, and that hoarding and indulgence will be viewed negatively.

One should be aware that in times of prosperity and wealth, interest in prophecy and Christ’s return tends to be diminished. Where hardship and persecution or their prospect become the norm, the longing for Jesus’ coming usually intensifies. Those living in times of greater ease and prosperity are more likely to be caught off guard at Christ’s appearing.

Just as death can be unexpected, the watchful believer understands that Christ’s coming will surprise many. No one knows or can know when God will give the final signal. From time to time, especially during times of millennial fervour, self-proclaimed prophets set dates as specific as a certain day or as general as a certain year. No attention should be paid to such speculations regardless of the means employed to predict the time of Jesus’ return. Such predictions are not only presumptuous, but thoroughly unbiblical. Only the Father knows the time.

However, we do well to study the nature of the events which will signal the coming of the end. Biblical prophecy should be studied in balance with the rest of Scripture. Ignorance of it may result in lack of readiness. Making it the prime focus over an extended time often leads to excesses, skewed perspectives, and even division. It can result in a people who are “so heavenly minded they are of no earthly good.”

Watchful believers are aware that the battle for the minds and souls of people will intensify. Deceptive signs and wonders will be the catalyst for some to turn away (Matthew 24:24). Alert Christians will avoid being mesmerized by flash and sizzle, but will rather seek after the will of God for their lives. The message God has given us in His Son is more than sufficient to live a Spirit-filled life.

If we are watchful and living in right relationship with God and others, our priorities will not need dramatic last-minute realignment. Prepared lives are characterized by alertness and self-discipline. These attitudes find their expression in lives of prayer, purity, and sacrificial service (Matthew 25:31-46).

平安与和平的视角（A Perspective of Peace）

具备末世视角的信徒所展现的另一显著特质，就是平安（peace）。在这充满政治动荡与经济不确定的世界中，基督在这世代末了的得胜再临，带给人内在安静的笃定。世界局势瞬息万变、战争与炸弹、基因操控、犯罪或大规模的暴行，可能会让人得出“世界已然失控”的结论。然而，深知上帝并未将这个世界弃绝给命运或人类的任意妄为，而是要迎来对一切毁灭性邪恶权势的高潮式终极得胜，乃是巨大安慰的泉源。作为基督徒，我们借着信心确信上帝的计划坚不可摧。上帝暂且容忍人类的悖逆，但祂对万有的统管主权从未动摇。每位牧者在公开讲道或个别辅导中，都有机会坚固信徒的心：上帝最终的统治全然确定。

在面对个人遭受的不公时，同样的平静安稳亦在心中掌权。伸冤与报应并非我们当行的事，而是上帝的专属主权。我们深知神明察每一个发生的侵害，且必将施行远超我们所能成就的更高公义。因着这一确据，我们得以学会饶恕，将伤害我们的人交托在上帝的看顾与审判之中。

这绝不意味着我们对一切不公义之事采取消极被动的态度。凡有人受压迫之处，我们都寻求挺身而出为之代求与干预。我们不相信单凭人的努力就能在地上建立千禧年国度，但我们积极促进公义，乃是因为我们本属于这样的国度。

带着对终局的瞻望，我们也能以极大的坚忍与盼望去面对各样的艰难困苦。我们深知苦难仅仅是今世的定数，而在来世将不再有任何眼泪。我们所受的痛苦，正是上帝用来将我们塑造为祂所喜悦的杰作之工具。

我们当今的社会汲汲于追求成功。每个人都被鼓励为未来设定目标与规划，这些规划可能包括经济自由或个人自我实现。然而在这些追求中，最巨大的空虚往往是属灵委身的缺失。在这种时代氛围中，天国的盼望与属天赏赐的确据，能够成为激励人专注度日、活出使命的强大动力。“所以，你们当用这些话彼此劝慰”（帖前 4:18）。

A Perspective of Peace

Another characteristic of a believer who has an end-time perspective is peace. Christ’s triumphal return at the end of this age is a quiet assurance in a world of political turmoil and economic uncertainty. The fast pace of changing world events, wars and bombs, genetic manipulation, crime, or large-scale atrocities may lead one to conclude that the world is out of control. To know that God hasn’t abandoned this world to fate or to the whims of humans but to a triumphant, climactic victory over the destructive forces of evil is a source of great comfort. As Christians, we are assured through our faith that God’s plan remains secure. God tolerates human disobedience for the time being, but overall control is never in question. Every pastor has occasions in public teaching or private counselling to reassure believers that the final reign of God is sure.

A similar sense of calm prevails when faced with questions of personal injustice. Revenge and retaliation are not ours to carry out. These are God’s domain. We know that God sees every abuse that occurs and will bring about a greater justice than we could ever accomplish. With this knowledge, we find it possible to forgive, releasing individuals to God’s care.

This does not mean that we take a passive stance toward all matters of injustice. Where people are oppressed, we seek to intervene on their behalf. We do not believe we can create the millennial kingdom through human effort, but we work for justice because we belong to such a kingdom.

With the end in view, we also face hardship of various kinds with considerable perseverance and hope. We know that suffering is a given for this world only and that in the next there will be no tears. Our pain is a tool which God may use to shape us into the masterpiece He desires.

Our society is focused on success. Every person is encouraged to set goals and plans for the future. These plans may include economic freedom or personal fulfillment. Often the greatest void in these pursuits is spiritual commitment. In this climate, the hope of heaven and the assurance of heavenly rewards can be an incentive for focused living. “Therefore encourage each other with these words” (1 Thessalonians 4:18).
